# Tinkerex simplification audit — 2026-10-19 (the coordinator's canonical record)

> **Committed to the repository on 2026-10-19** from the audit session's scratchpad, unchanged in
> substance. Supporting material lives beside it: the 34 subsystem reports and the five
> audit-the-audit reports in `docs/audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/`, the worker brief
> (`BRIEF.md`), the audit-pass brief (`AUDIT-PASS-BRIEF.md`) and the coordinator's two adjudication
> aids (`COORD-summary.md`, `COORD-priorities-draft.md`) in `docs/audit-2026-10-19-simplification/`.
> Citations of the form `audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/Sxx.md:N` are relative to `docs/`.
> The audit itself modified nothing; this commit adds only these files. The three one-off patch
> scripts the audit log mentions were working files and are not committed.

Repository: /Users/charlesschwartz/Desktop/repos/tinkerex  
HEAD at audit start: 75124d67982f4c6d110e39e21c0b58452aa3916e (main, 2026-10-18)  
Pre-existing untracked file (must stay untouched): app/assets/images/landing/library.jpg  
Audit date: 2026-10-19. Audit-only: no edits, tests, commits or pushes. Read-only inspection only.

FINAL STATE (2026-10-19, end of audit): 35 inventory rows (S01–S35), all terminal — 32 reviewed with findings, 3 explicit skips (S30 docs, S31 vendored, S35 per-file test review). 54 findings recorded in §2: 46 ranked in §6 (14 P1 · 17 P2 · 13 P3 · 2 P4), 8 demoted to tickets after the materiality adjudication, 1 rejected-as-written and narrowed (F-S07-2), 6 narrowed. Five audit-the-audit passes (coverage, overlap, materiality, schema, priority) ran as fresh read-only agents and were adjudicated by the coordinator (§7). Repository unchanged throughout (verified: `git status --porcelain` shows only the pre-existing untracked landing image; HEAD 75124d67; no stash).

Scope of the audit: materially useful simplifications in data structures, state representation,
control flow, algorithms and ownership. Per-worker threshold: at most two opportunities per
subsystem; `skip` when nothing clears the bar. Not in scope: stylistic consistency, hypothetical
extensibility, line-count trimming, moving branching behind a new type.

---

## 1. Subsystem inventory (the coverage contract)

Statuses: queued | in review | recommend | skip.  
Every tracked source path in the repo is assigned to exactly one row below (shared docs under
`docs/invariants/` are read-only inputs to several rows and are listed where they bind). Out of
scope (not repo content): `.claude/worktrees/*` (an untracked worktree copy), `maker-app*.zip/.dmg`,
`storage/`, `tmp/`, `log/`, `sidecar/boxes/` beyond its 8 tracked files, `node_modules`.

### S01 — FileLayer port: factory, exceptions, value objects, capabilities, broadcast batching
- Boundary: `app/services/file_layer.rb`, `app/services/file_layer/{capabilities,delivery,file_info,trash_entry,upload_begun,upload_finalized,subtree}.rb`, `app/services/library_broadcast.rb`, `lib/library_backend.rb`, `lib/library_backend_check.rb`, `config/initializers/library_backend_check.rb`, `app/controllers/concerns/library_capabilities.rb`. Tests also: `test/services/library_broadcast_test.rb`.
- Interfaces/call sites: `FileLayer.current`/`.build`; the port verbs every adapter answers (`stat`, `stat_all`, `read`, `download`, `trashed`, `mkdir`, `move`, `delete`, `undelete`, `write`, `begin_upload`, `put_chunk`, `finalize_upload`); `Capabilities` read by views, `LibraryCapabilities#require_capability!`, `ScanJob`/`TrashPurgeJob` gates; `LibraryBroadcast.batch`.
- Tests: `test/services/file_layer/{factory,value_objects,subtree}_test.rb`, the three shared contracts `adapter_contract.rb`/`adapter_mutation_contract.rb`/`adapter_write_contract.rb`, `test/lib/library_backend_{check,required}_test.rb`, `test/boot_scan_test.rb`, `test/integration/v1/capability_gating_test.rb`.
- Status: recommend (2 findings, F-S01-1, F-X-1) — harvested & verified 2026-10-19

## 2. Confirmed opportunities
(filled in as rows complete; each entry carries the full 8-field schema and names its authoritative row)

### F-S04-1 — ChangelogSync: parse each sync-server reply shape into one typed node-state value (resync arm silently drops version/size/manifest) [NARROWED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication]
- Authoritative row: S04. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S04.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED against `app/services/file_layer/chunk_remote/changelog_sync.rb:166` (full_resync upserts tree nodes with no `version:`), `:213-231` (`version ||= payload["version"]`, manifest via the `@current_manifest` ivar), `docs/sync-protocol.md` §8.4 (tree nodes carry `version_id`/`size`/`manifest` FLAT), the sibling Rust server's `services/tree.rs` `TreeNode` (flat), and `changelog_sync_test.rb:148-150` whose fake tree node nests a `"version"` key no server sends.
- Evidence: as above. The resync path is unreachable today (`prune_changelog` in tinkerex-sync has no caller — verified by grep) and becomes live the day a prune is scheduled.
- Current complexity / invalid state: one applier guesses between two wire shapes; on the /v1/tree shape the guess fails, so a resync repairs structure but leaves `current_version_id`/`size`/manifest rows stale: new files 404 on read, changed files serve old bytes, until the node's next `put`.
- Proposed representation: a `NodeState` Data value with `from_change(entry)` and `from_tree(node)` constructors feeding one `apply`; the ivar side-channel and the `||` guesses disappear. Mirrors the Rust client's `change_to_sync`/`tree_node_to_sync` → `SyncNode` → `apply_remote`.
- Smallest scope: `changelog_sync.rb` only (nest `NodeState`, replace `upsert` + `@current_manifest`, two call sites); correct the three fake tree nodes in `changelog_sync_test.rb`; optionally one journey test pinning the REAL tree shape.
- Risks: must keep (a) a pruned-version `put` leaving `current_version_id` alone, (b) `store_manifest` idempotency, (c) the no-node-payload branch, (d) the nudge fed by `apply_page` ONLY (project-intake.md invariant), (e) `deleted` false for tree nodes. No data migration (no live mirror has resynced). chunk_remote-only production code; unit tests run on all three legs.
- Validation: correcting the fake makes the existing resync test go RED on unchanged code (demonstrates the defect); new tests (b)-(f) in the report with their mutations (`from_tree` reading `n["version"]`; dropping `entry["manifest"]`; collecting nudges inside `apply`).
- Confidence: high (four independent sources agree on the wire shape). Blast radius: one file + its test. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): NARROWED. The `NodeState` Data type adds more than the fix needs. Scope becomes: pass `version:` and `manifest:` explicitly at the two `upsert` call sites (`changelog_sync.rb:166`, `:186-188`); delete the `version ||= payload["version"]` guess (`:222`) and the `@current_manifest` side channel; correct the three fake tree nodes in `changelog_sync_test.rb` to the flat `version_id`/`size`/`manifest` shape (§14e: the fake shared the code's misunderstanding). Latent at HEAD: `prune_changelog` (`../tinkerex-sync` `services/gc.rs:202`) has no caller (coordinator-verified), so `gapped?` never fires today. Priority: low-medium; no dependency; land before any prune is scheduled upstream.

### F-S01-1 — One reading of LIBRARY_BACKEND with explicit unset/unknown states; retire the `chunk` sentinel and the dead `LibraryBackend` module
- Authoritative row: S01. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S01.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `app/services/file_layer.rb:169` defaults to `share` and `:203` raises on unknown; `lib/library_backend_check.rb:107` defaults `check!`'s `backend:` to the retired `chunk` (a "match no arm" sentinel) and `:126` `required_in_production!` passes any non-blank value; `config/initializers/library_backend_check.rb` calls both with no args; `lib/library_backend.rb` (`DEFAULT = "chunk"`) has zero callers in app/lib/config (three test call sites only); `lib/tinkerex/registration.rb:47` is a fourth raw reader. Commits 0fd8c8e0 and 287ddac5 exist and each patched one reader.
- Current complexity / invalid state: four readers, four normalisations, four copies of the name list. An unknown/typo'd/retired/space-padded value passes BOTH boot guards and then raises on every response (the CSP `connect_src` lambda resolves `FileLayer.current` per response, per §14a) and every job — the same /up-500 symptom 287ddac5 fixed for the blank case only.
- Proposed representation: `LibraryBackendCheck.configured` → nil | known name | raise `UnknownBackend < ArgumentError`; `boot!` (unit-testable: unknown refuses everywhere, unset refuses in production and boots in dev); `check!` requires `backend:`; factory reads `configured || "share"`; delete `lib/library_backend.rb`, its hand-require and autoload-ignore entry.
- Smallest scope: `lib/library_backend_check.rb`, `config/initializers/library_backend_check.rb`, one line in `app/services/file_layer.rb`, delete `lib/library_backend.rb` + `config/application.rb:9-14,24`, retarget 3 test call sites, reword two §14a bullets.
- Risks: ONE intended behaviour change — an unknown value now refuses the boot (and `db:prepare`) instead of booting then 500ing. (S18 WITHDRAWS the optional follow-on for `V1::DevicesController#library_info`: it deliberately asks the SERVING adapter, and the v1 tests install adapters independently of ENV on every leg; if its three-arm `case` is ever retired, the source should be FileLayer's own name→class table inverted with `.key(adapter.class)`, never the ENV reading.) Unset-in-dev still boots (the §14a-measured trap is not reopened); unset-in-production still refused; precompile stage unaffected; every shipped config names a valid value. Backend legs unaffected (test env skips the initializer).
- Validation: existing factory/required/check tests; rewrite the two "chunk mode" sentinel tests; new tests for `configured`, `boot!`, a static initializer-wiring pin, and NAMES-vs-factory arms, each with a named mutation.
- Confidence: high on the gap, medium-high on the remedy. Blast radius: boot path. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (schema pass, 2026-10-19): validation completed with the four mutations from audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S01.md:98-101 — (a) `configured` returns `raw` unvalidated → red; (b) revert `boot!` to `required_in_production!` + `check!` → the unknown-in-production case goes red; (c) restore the initializer's two old calls → the static wiring pin goes red; (d) drop an arm from `FileLayer.build`'s `case` → the NAMES-vs-factory test goes red. Dev-boot validation comes from test (c), not from `db:test:prepare` (see F-S26-2). Priority: P2 (defect-first: an unknown, typo'd or retired value passes both boot guards and then 500s every response through the CSP lambda; on Kamal destinations `/up` already stops it going live, so the gain is mainly compose/Unraid-template installs). Prerequisite for F-S17-1's optional `Demo.validate!` input and for F-S18-1's boot-time move of the `FileLayer.build` producer. Rewrite both §14a bullets in the same slice.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): Coordination — `config/application.rb:9-14,24` (S24); the three `LibraryBackend.name` test call sites in `chunk_remote/mutation_contract_test.rb:96`, `upload_test.rb:91` (S04) and `system/uploader_test.rb:49` (S21); CLAUDE.md (S30) under the 1,360-line ceiling (`docs_hygiene_test.rb:71`, S26 — §6). If F-S26-2's conservative option lands, the static initializer-wiring pin becomes the ONLY guard of `config/initializers/library_backend_check.rb`, so it is required.

### F-S11-1 — Stamp each `maker_addon_files` row with the state revision that wrote it, so a saved item's CURRENT output set is stored rather than guessed by a (position, id) tiebreak
- Authoritative row: S11. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S11.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `library_output.rb#upsert_file!` writes only role/content_type/position (and its own comment: "nothing on the row says which save that was"); `addon_state.rb:23` `has_many :files, -> { order(:position, :id) }`; `addons_controller.rb#latest_output_folder` orders `…position ASC, id ASC` so the OLDEST row wins a tie and `destination_from_outputs` reads `rows.first`; `#states` serialises `s.files` in that order while `docs/addon-api-v1.md:626-627` promises save order; `files/_meta.html.erb` and `projects/_generated_group.html.erb` print the state's CURRENT revision on every file; `addons_controller_test.rb` asserts the `[0, 0]` position tie on purpose; the sibling boxes bundle's `gallery.js` carries a workaround comment for exactly this.
- Current complexity / invalid state: the table conflates "every file this state ever produced" (needed for ownership/provenance) with "this state's current outputs" (needed by three readers); the second is approximated by a tiebreak that picks the stale row, so after a revise-under-new-name: "Edit in Boxes" starts in the old folder and a save there rewrites the OLD file; `#states` interleaves stale and current; the file page labels a v1 file "· v2". Three "cannot be test-pinned" caveats exist only because the row lacks this fact.
- Proposed representation: integer `revision` column on `maker_addon_files` (backfilled to the state's current revision; legacy rows keep today's order), stamped by the one writer `upsert_file!` inside the save transaction; order `revision DESC, position ASC, id ASC` in the association and in `latest_output_folder`. Optional additive: a per-file `revision` key in `#states`, and `AddonOrigin` carrying the row's revision so the labels are right.
- Smallest scope: one additive migration + backfill test; `library_output.rb#upsert_file!`; `addon_state.rb` association order; one ORDER BY line in `addons_controller.rb`; two doc lines. Stale rows are kept (ownership, provenance, never-delete-user-bytes all untouched). S23 CONSISTENCY VERDICT: fits (`integer, null: false, default: 1` matches `maker_addon_states.revision`; the correlated UPDATE via `execute` is the revisions migration's own shape; no index needed; not an EDGES column) — ONE TEST CAVEAT: the backfill test's state must sit at revision ≥ 2, otherwise "skip the UPDATE" leaves the default 1 equal to the expected value and the mutation stays green (§15's default-equals-expected vacuity); the revisions migration's refusal of a COUNT column is not contradicted (a per-row fact written once by the one writer).
- Risks: additive column, correlated UPDATE runs on SQLite and Postgres; `#states` order changes only in the stale case and toward what both shipped bundles already want (no bundle release needed, §14c); no port/adapter change so the three legs run identical assertions.
- Validation: existing destination/revise/origin tests; four new tests with mutations (drop `revision DESC` → red; restore `order(:position, :id)` → red; drop `revision:` from `update!` → red; skip the backfill UPDATE → red). Fixture rows must be created in an order that disagrees with the order under test.
- Confidence: medium-high (defect traced through SQL, association, bundle source and the API doc; exact column shape is a judgement call). Blast radius: addon save door + states JSON. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2). The cheaper alternative — touching `maker_addon_files.updated_at` on every upsert — fails (rows from one save would then order by write time and reverse `position`); +1 column is the only recoverable answer. Existing tests (named): `test/controllers/tools/addon_destination_test.rb`, `test/controllers/tools/addons_controller_test.rb:578-603`, `test/models/maker/addon_origin_test.rb`. Priority: P2. No dependency (additive migration); classify the column in `SchemaMap::EDGES` (F-S23-1); lands after F-X-1 (both edit `library_output.rb`).
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): the optional "AddonOrigin carries the row's revision" step must name the two views that print `state.revision` directly — `files/_meta.html.erb:16-17` (S10) and `projects/_generated_group.html.erb:49` (S15).

### F-S10-1 — One bounded content-read door for the two zip readers (GerberArchive, ThreeMfManifest): cache what the BYTES said, never what the PORT said [NARROWED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication]
- Authoritative row: S10. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S10.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `gerber_archive.rb#for` and `three_mf_manifest.rb#for` both call `Rails.cache.fetch([...], expires_in: CACHE_TTL) { read(...) }` where `read` rescues `FileLayer::FetchError`/`NotFound` to nil INSIDE the block; activesupport 8.1.3.1 `cache.rb:1123` writes a nil result unless `skip_nil` (not set anywhere); production is `:solid_cache_store` (`production.rb:109`), test is `:null_store` (so no test can see it); `addons/registry.rb:159-161` records the opposite, correct rule for its own fetch; GerberArchive's FetchError arm does NOT set `Current.library_unreachable` while ThreeMfManifest's does (already drifted); `project_intake.rb:531-534` reasons from "an unreachable library costs nothing", which is false once the nil is cached.
- Current complexity / invalid state: a cached nil conflates "the bytes say not a board / no manifest" (a content fact, correctly cached) with "the store did not answer" and "the path is not there right now" (request facts) under a key that does not change when the store comes back, for up to the TTL (30 days), surviving deploys in Solid Cache. Effects: a Gerber zip loses its board viewer; a 3MF loses slicer colours and its unit factor (inch/metre exports measure 25.4x/1000x short); ProjectIntake can file a board zip into the source-archive folder on the sweep after an outage. Two copies of the wrapper and its test helpers have already drifted once.
- Proposed representation: one `Maker::ContentRead.cached(namespace, user:, node:, limit:, ttl:) { |bytes| parse }` door that (a) gates on `node.size` and the content token, (b) stores ONLY the parser's answer (nil included), (c) rescues the port's two classes OUTSIDE the fetch (FetchError → banner + nil; NotFound → nil). Each reader keeps its name test, bound and pure parser. Minimal fallback if the shared door is declined: move each rescue outside its own `fetch` in place and add GerberArchive's missing banner arm (fixes the bug, keeps two copies).
- Smallest scope: new `app/services/maker/content_read.rb`; `gerber_archive.rb:83-97` and `three_mf_manifest.rb:86-107` → the door (delete both `self.read`); bump both cache namespaces so already-poisoned nils stop being served; prose in `current.rb:10-13`, both headers, `files-preview.md:171,518`. Call sites unchanged (files_controller, public/files_controller, models_controller, project_intake).
- Risks: during an outage each zip/3MF page view re-attempts one bounded read instead of serving a poisoned hit (one Transport failure per view on chunk_remote); zip pages start raising the unreachable banner (what §14d implies); the namespace bump costs one read per zip/3MF on first view. No schema/API change; leg-neutral.
- Validation: existing parser-nil cache tests stay green; new tests under a MemoryStore: adapter `read` raising FetchError → nil + banner, then unstubbed → real answer and a SECOND read counted; same for NotFound without banner. Mutation 1: move the rescue back inside the fetch → red. Mutation 2: delete the banner line → red (GerberArchive has no such assertion today). Optional end-to-end for S06: an outage sweep then a clean sweep files the board zip into pcb/.
- Confidence: high on the defect (read off the code and the gem source), high that one door beats two drifted copies, medium on placement/naming. Blast radius: two readers + cache keys. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): NARROWED and RAISED. The material part is the defect and needs no new module: move each `rescue → nil` OUTSIDE its own `Rails.cache.fetch` (`gerber_archive.rb:88-97`, `three_mf_manifest.rb:91-105`; activesupport writes a nil unless `skip_nil`), add GerberArchive's missing library-unreachable banner arm, bump both cache namespaces. The shared `Maker::ContentRead` door is an optional follow-up, justified only by the banner arm that has already drifted between the two copies. Priority RAISED to P1: an anonymous visitor reaches the poisoning through `Public::FilesController#view` (`:57-60`; §4's S14 note). Land before F-S10-2. Existing tests (named): `test/services/maker/gerber_archive_test.rb:259`, `test/services/maker/three_mf_manifest_test.rb:310`.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): Coordination — `app/models/current.rb:10-13` (S17). Add to scope: CLAUDE.md §14d's banner-setter list (`:938-940`) names Readme, RepoCard, GerberSets and ThreeMfManifest and omits GerberArchive — net-zero lines under the ceiling (§6).

### F-S10-2 — Resolve the file page's viewer ONCE in `FilePreview` as a typed `Viewer`, shared by the maker's and the visitor's file pages [NARROWED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication]
- Authoritative row: S10 (with S14 for the public half). Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S10.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `files_controller.rb#show:510,537-575` and `public/files_controller.rb#view:46,57-74` carry the same zip-override / layer-list / single-or-stackup / 3MF-manifest assembly line for line, differing only in user and URL builder; both templates (`files/show.html.erb:119,147-148`, `public/files/view.html.erb:73,82-83`) repeat the `colors`/`unit_factor` defaults; `@kind` is assigned twice and `#show:522` reads the provisional value (`@editable = @kind == :text …`) before the zip override at `:539`; `file_preview.rb:1-4` states the one-answer rule that the read-dependent half currently breaks; the public zip and 3MF cases have no test (grep of `public_*_test.rb` finds no zip/3MF/unit_factor coverage).
- Current complexity / invalid state: five ivars whose valid combinations are fixed by one rule but assembled twice; wrong-but-representable states (`:gerber` with nil layers; a provisional kind read mid-action) avoided only by convention; the public half is unpinned, so a change landing in one controller cannot be noticed by the suite.
- Proposed representation: `FilePreview::Viewer = Data.define(:kind, :gerber_layers, :gerber_single, :colors, :unit_factor)` built by one `viewer_for(user:, node:, url:)` in the concern; each controller makes one call with its URL lambda; templates read `@viewer.*` with defaults applied once; `preview_kind` stays name-only (files-preview.md:191); `gerber_layers_for`, `FilesHelper#gerber_archive_entry` and every viewer partial's strict locals unchanged.
- Smallest scope: `file_preview.rb` (+Viewer, +viewer_for); `files_controller.rb#show` (replace :510 and :537-575; `@editable` from `@viewer.kind`); `files/show.html.erb:109-182`; S14-owned: `public/files_controller.rb#view:46,57-74` (`enqueue_geometry` keys on `@viewer.kind`) and `public/files/view.html.erb:64-101`; `files-preview.md:191,200`.
- Risks: behaviour-preserving; no data/API change; no backend-leg difference; one extra `permits?` on a public zip page (constant). Needs S14's agreement; without it only the private copy consolidates and the drift risk remains.
- Validation: existing gerber_preview, public_file_view, files_flow, files_controller_preview_kind, page_query_budget tests; NEW public tests for a published Gerber zip (one archive entry, `single=false`, public named route) and a published 3MF (colours + unit factor). Mutation: drop the `archive ? :gerber :` override in `viewer_for` → zip tests on BOTH pages red (today only the private one could).
- Confidence: medium-high (mechanical consolidation, well pinned privately; ownership spans two rows). Blast radius: two controllers + two templates. Effort: small-medium. Dependency: none on F-S10-1, but land F-S10-1 first so the shared door is what `viewer_for` calls.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): NARROWED. Keep one concern method returning the only part that holds a decision — the archive / layer-list / single trio — shared by `files_controller.rb:537-574` and `public/files_controller.rb:57-73`, plus the missing public zip and 3MF tests (most of the value: public coverage today is Gerber layers only, `public_file_view_test.rb:112-122`). Drop the 5-field `Viewer` Data type and the `colors`/`unit_factor` default moves (line-count). The "provisional `@kind`" hazard is unreachable (a `.zip` never resolves to `:text`). Carry S14's refinement (§5): the zip-archive arm drops a nil URL (`filter_map`) like the other two layer arms, so files-preview.md:222-227's "absent on both arms or on neither" holds for all three; assert it on the public page. Priority: P3; lands after F-S10-1.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): the dependency on F-S10-1 is a PREFERENCE, not a code dependency — F-S10-1 keeps `GerberArchive.for`/`ThreeMfManifest.for` as the entry points `viewer_for` calls, so the two can run in parallel. The new public tests live in `public_file_view_test.rb` (S22 by tie-break); include S14's second mutation (pass the public side `url: ->(_) { nil }` → the new zip test and `:99` go red).

### F-S15-1 — One editing-aware answer to "which subfolders does this render draw no file cells for", read by BOTH the recent row and the thumbnail union (and add the Photos rows to the union directly)
- Authoritative row: S15. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S15.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `projects_controller.rb:196` feeds the recent row from `dirs_without_tiles` (`:633-644`: a HIDDEN or BROWSER-view panel → the whole dir; else nested repos), while the thumbnail union (`:402-406`) subtracts `hidden_in_repos = repo_subtree_ids - generated - unrolled`, which keys on `repo?` — so a hidden plain folder and a browsed plain folder ARE still backfilled, a repo nested inside a tiles-view repo is backfilled (`unrolled` takes the whole outer subtree), and an image inside a browsed repository is painted by Photos (`project_photos.rb:13`, `all_rows` images at any depth; `@photo_rows` computed at `:432` AFTER the union) but never backfilled — `_photos.html.erb:7-9` and `_folder_panel.html.erb:25-29` both claim otherwise. The comment at `:626-631` claims the two exclusions agree; they do not (`project_page_test.rb` records the recent rule being re-keyed off `repo?` in review).
- Current complexity / invalid state: two derivations of one fact (Dir objects vs file-id sets) disagree in four reachable cases: (a) hidden folder panel, (b) browsed plain folder, (c) nested repo in a tiles-view repo panel — all backfilled though unpainted, spending the 100-id BACKFILL_LIMIT budget ahead of visible tiles (name-ordered); (d) an image inside a browsed repo is painted but never backfilled → spinner then "Preview not supported", permanent on chunk_remote where only a backfill creates a row. The union is a hand-kept list of painted surfaces patched in one review at a time (`:396-427`).
- Proposed representation: make `dirs_without_tiles` editing-aware (`dropped = (!@editing && !visible?(key)) || view_for(key) == "browser"`), call it ONCE, derive `untiled = no_tiles.flat_map(&:subtree_file_ids).to_set - @generated_ids` for the union, compute `@photo_rows` BEFORE the union and add `@photo_rows.map(&:node_id)` as its own term. Deletes `unrolled`, `hidden_in_repos`, `ProjectFolderListing#repo_subtree_ids` (single caller) and two subsumed union terms (`@folder_images` ⊂ photo rows; live entry photos = the photo rows' entry half).
- Smallest scope: `ProjectsController#dirs_without_tiles` + `#show` (~15 lines), delete `project_folder_listing.rb#repo_subtree_ids`, rewrite comments `:385-422` and the stale claims in `_folder_panel`/`_photos` (the latter is S16's file).
- Risks: no data/API/query-count change (in-memory over the listing; still one `ThumbnailMap.for`). Intended behaviour changes: fewer preview rows/jobs for (a)-(c), new rows for (d). ONE product decision for the owner: in edit mode the recent row would include files of a ghosted hidden panel (the "on this render" reading). Leg-neutral; (d) only shows on chunk_remote.
- Validation: existing project_attachments ("a repository's files enqueue no thumbnails"), project_page (tiles-view repo; two recent-row tests), page_nodes, page_query_budget stay green. New request tests, red today: hidden/browsed plain folder enqueues no GeneratePreviewJob while an image in it still does (mutation: drop either `dropped` arm); nested repo in a tiles-view repo enqueues nothing (mutation: restore `unrolled`); a `.jpg` inside a browsed repo has a `maker_previews` row after GET (mutation: remove `@photo_rows` from the union); edit-mode hidden panel still backfills (mutation: drop `!@editing &&`).
- Confidence: high on the divergences; medium-high on edit-mode semantics (owner's call). Blast radius: one action. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2; defect-first — thumbnails backfilled for tiles never painted, repo images painted and never backfilled). Priority: P2. Sibling of F-S14-1 (same painted-set drift, different files); independent. The edit-mode reading of a hidden panel is the owner's call.

### F-S15-2 — The "browser draft is the whole truth of blocks and tabs" rule belongs in `LayoutDocument#merge` for `:draft`, not in the Save door alone
- Authoritative row: S15. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S15.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `layout_document.rb#merge:352` re-appends every saved entry the posted draft does not name (`(current.keys - listed.keys)`), blocks and tabs included, and runs the Publish-last note (`:353-357`), the block cap (`:364`), the duplicate-tab-name check (`:371`) and the tab cap (`:376`) on that MERGED set; `from_draft` (`:202-207`) always merges against the saved layout; only `Projects::PanelsController#replace` strips unlisted kinded entries afterwards (`held_by_browser`, `:55,87-90`), while `Projects::LayoutsController#yaml` (`:24-26`) and `#parse` (`:37-39`) use the raw merge; the browser's `draft()` emits every slot (`layout_editor_controller.js:83-84`, with the contract stated at `:32-33`) and the same draft rides to all three doors (`:453,:466`). The decision is recorded in the content-blocks spec §10.9 ("the two meet at the door"), whose own premise applies to all three doors.
- Current complexity / invalid state: a "merged draft" holds blocks/tabs the maker deleted, and it is what caps, duplicate checks, YAML export and Apply all see. Traced consequences: (a) the YAML drawer still lists a deleted block/tab; (b) Apply resurrects it (parse base re-adds it; `adopt`/`adoptBlock` refill it); (c) Save refuses VALID drafts at cap churn (38 saved, delete 3, add 4 → 42 counted) or when a deleted tab's name is reused; (d) every Save after a delete flashes "Publish is always last; it was moved to the end."; (e) in the (c) cases `parse` falls back to the saved layout as base, silently reverting every unsaved hide/width/title on Apply.
- Proposed representation: in `merge`, for `source == :draft`, do not carry over kinded (block/tab) entries the draft omits — one `reject` line; `:yaml` keeps merge-and-never-delete-by-omission; delete `held_by_browser` and let `replace` use `result.draft`. Panels and folders keep being carried.
- Smallest scope: `layout_document.rb#merge` (one line + header note); `panels_controller.rb#replace` (drop `held_by_browser`, ~10 lines); spec §10.9 note. No JS, view, column or YAML-grammar change.
- Risks: no data/API change. Save's outcome is identical (`held_by_browser`'s set equals "kinded entries listed"); yaml/parse change exactly in (a), (b), (e). A pasted `id:` naming a block deleted this session now creates a new block (the existing unknown-id rule) instead of reviving it. Leg-neutral.
- Validation: existing project_blocks "Save creates, updates and deletes…" and project_tabs "a deleted tab is gone" pin Save's deletion — deleting `held_by_browser` WITHOUT the merge line turns them red (proves the rule moved). New: unit `from_draft` of a draft omitting a saved block/tab drops it with no Publish note; integration `yaml` omits `id: <key>`; `parse` returns a draft without it; Save at block-cap churn and a reused tab name both save. Mutation: drop the `reject` line → all red.
- Confidence: high. Blast radius: one service + one controller. Effort: small. Note: revises a recorded design decision (spec §10.9); the argument for revising it is that the premise holds at all three doors.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2). It revises content-blocks spec §10.9 (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-09-content-blocks-design.md:234-241`) — a spec decision, not a §14/invariants rule, so revisable with a spec note; `from_draft` has exactly three callers, all browser drafts. Priority: P2; needs the owner's yes on §10.9. Net removal: one `reject`, and `held_by_browser` is deleted.

### F-S05-1 — The Reconciler's tombstone-revive lookups can use no index: a partial `(user_id, storage_path) WHERE deleted = true` index (plus one `deleted: true` term) makes a first scan or large import linear instead of quadratic
- Authoritative row: S05 (the migration/schema files are S23's; S23 told to defer to this entry). Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S05.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `reconciler.rb:536-537` (`where(storage_path:, deleted: true, kind: "file")`, run for EVERY new file) and `:605` (`where(storage_path:, kind: "dir")`, no `deleted` term, run for every new/changed dir); `db/schema.rb:507` is the ONLY `storage_path` index and is partial on `deleted = false`. Coordinator re-ran the plan check in an in-memory SQLite (no files written): both lookups → `SEARCH nodes USING INDEX index_nodes_on_user_id (user_id=?)` (a scan of the user's rows); with a `WHERE deleted = 1` partial index both → `USING INDEX … (user_id=? AND storage_path=?)`; the `present` lookup keeps its plan.
- Current complexity / invalid state: per new file the lookup visits every row the user has, so a first scan of N files costs ~N²/2 row visits (reviewer's probe ≈0.125 µs/row/lookup in memory: ~10 min at 100k files, past `ScanJob`'s 1-hour per-user semaphore near 240k — after which the 15-minute recurring tick can start a SECOND overlapping pass over the same library, the state the pairing logic assumes away — ~17 h at 1M). Negligible on today's real library (~5k rows), invisible to the suite (the query-COUNT guard stays linear; rows visited per query grow). S3 first scans hash nothing, so the create path IS their cost.
- Proposed representation: `add_index :nodes, [:user_id, :storage_path], where: "deleted = true"` (complementary partial index over exactly the rows these lookups ask for) and `deleted: true` added at `:605` (the dir lookup already means that: `row` nil ⇒ no present row at the path). Nothing in the Reconciler's control flow or state changes; F-07's "tombstone lookups stay live queries" stands; the two tombstone kinds stay distinguished (trash tombstones have NULL `storage_path` and still cannot match a path lookup). Non-partial alternative discussed in the report (serves PG without bound-parameter implication, but moved the no-op `#live` pluck onto it in the probe).
- Smallest scope: one migration + `db/schema.rb`; `reconciler.rb:605` (+2 comment edits); one EXPLAIN-plan test in `reconciler_test.rb`. S23 CONSISTENCY VERDICT: fits (custom `…_tombstones` name matches the schema's other custom names; the `deleted = true` literal mirrors the existing partial; tenant-first like every `nodes` index; the schema's first NON-unique partial index, which is fine; passes the prefix-index guard since a partial index is never read as a superset) — TWO CORRECTIONS: the guard's comment at `architecture_invariants_test.rb:1289-1291` ("Zero such pairs exist today") becomes stale and must be amended in the same slice; and "regenerate the schema map" is a NO-OP because the map carries no index list and `parse` drops `where:` (the page already misstates `storage_path` as unconditionally unique — a small accuracy fix inside S23's row).
- Risks: the `:605` edit differs only if a web `mkdir` creates a live dir at that path between the pluck and the line (today adopted; after: `RecordNotUnique` fails that one job, the next pass sees it). Postgres uses a partial index only with the bound `deleted` value visible (custom plans) — every scannable deployment is SQLite today; EXPLAIN on PG if share/s3 ever run there. Leg-neutral (+1 test on each).
- Validation: existing revive/move/kind-change/no-op-constancy tests stay green; new test captures the two revive statements WITH their binds and asserts the plan names the new index (trap: a bind-less EXPLAIN cannot prove a boolean partial-index predicate). Mutations: drop the index → both assertions red; drop `deleted: true` at `:605` → only the dir assertion red.
- Confidence: high on mechanism (plans verified twice); materiality scales with library size. Blast radius: one index + one query term. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT over the pass's DEMOTE, re-tiered. The pass is right that an index changes cost, not behaviour; it is kept because the brief names "a more appropriate collection or index" as an audit target and the plan was verified twice (no usable index for either revive lookup). Materiality scales with library size — negligible on the ~5k-row real library, minute-scale past ~100k files (an in-memory projection, not a deployment measurement), the 1-hour per-user scan semaphore overrun projected near 240k. Priority: P3 (a cheap scalability migration). Land before or with F-S05-2 (both touch `reconciler.rb:605`). Existing test (named): `test/services/library/reconciler_test.rb`.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): Coordination — `test/architecture_invariants_test.rb:1289-1291` (S26). Plan-claim note: if F-S02-1's `trashed` reads the new column, F-S02-1's own index serves it instead of this one (both are plan claims only).

### F-S05-2 — Give the Reconciler's pass state ONE owner: route its eight `nodes` writes through two `#live`-maintaining helpers, make `LiveRow` immutable, delete the redundant `@dir_ids`
- Authoritative row: S05. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S05.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `reconciler.rb:59-65` (`LiveRow` is a MUTABLE Struct "because the write sites re-stamp the fields they just wrote"); `:717-727` ("THE INVARIANT: this mirrors `present`. Every site … FIVE of them … Count them against the code, not against this sentence"); grep shows eight `live[...]=`/`live.delete` write sites and three verbatim 8-field `LiveRow.new` blocks (`:450`, `:552`, `:618`); `@dir_ids` (`:98`, stamped at `:595` and `:609`) duplicates what `live[dir]` already holds for every observed dir, and `parent_id_for` (`:686`) reads `@dir_ids[dir] || live[dir]&.node_id`; commit 0a160454 ("there are five") is the missed site that was found in review, and nothing tests the invariant.
- Current complexity / invalid state: three representations of overlapping facts (`present` in the DB, `@live`, `@dir_ids`) kept in sync by hand at ten sites; a missed site is silent (the stale key was "harmless today by luck"); adding a `LiveRow` column means editing four constructors where a `keyword_init` Struct silently defaults a forgotten key to nil; ~45 lines of obligation comments exist to remind writers of what the structure could discharge.
- Proposed representation: `LiveRow = Data.define(...)` with `.from(node)`; two private writers — `write!(node, **attrs)` (create/revive/move/re-stamp: update, evict the old path, set the new) and `tombstone!(path, node = nil)` (kind collisions, doomed files, vanished dirs) — as the ONLY ways a pass writes `nodes`; `parent_id_for` reads `live[dir]&.node_id` alone; `@dir_ids` deleted.
- Smallest scope: `app/services/library/reconciler.rb` only, all private; public surface (`new`, `#call`, `#vanished_paths`) unchanged.
- Risks: identical output on every input an observer can produce (traced site by site); differs only on contradictory input (a dir AND a file observation at one rel_path, which S3Observer stopped emitting in 083ce11 and a filesystem walk cannot produce) — same no-raise oscillation as today. Query count unchanged. No data/schema/API change; share and s3 run this path, chunk_remote never does.
- Validation: the existing reconciler/s3_observer tests that pin rename/edit/delete/revive/kind-change/tree-parenting/no-op constancy/dir+file survival; NEW property test — after each `call` of a fixture sequence that drives all eight sites, `@live` equals a fresh `present.where.not(storage_path: nil)` pluck through `LiveRow`. Mutations: drop `live.delete` from `tombstone!` → red (exactly 0a160454's stale key); drop `live.delete(was)` from `write!` → red on rename; drop the assignment → red on create; `parent_id_for` → nil → tree-parenting test red.
- Confidence: medium (behaviour preservation high; payoff is preventive — retires a bug class that has happened once, plus a redundant map). Blast radius: one file. Effort: small-medium. Independent of F-S05-1 (touches `:605` differently; land F-S05-1 first or together).
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T3; preventive, one recorded miss 0a160454). Do not touch the "present rows only" rule (`reconciler.rb:728-733`). Priority: P3; after F-S05-1.

### F-S06-1 — `ProjectIntake::Result` as ONE row per inbox file (`Filing`) instead of three parallel collections, a String|Symbol return pun, two derivable counters and a name-keyed side channel with a re-derivation fallback
- Authoritative row: S06. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S06.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `project_intake.rb:32` `Result = Data.define(:attached, :moved, :skipped, :skips, :plan, :status, :conflict_folders)` with `initialize(conflict_folders: {}, **)` so six construction sites need not change; `#file_all` (`:722-746`) keeps four accumulators plus a `processed` counter and returns the positional tuple `[moved, skips.size, skips, plan]`, rebuilt in its FetchError rescue; `#place` answers a String (folder) or a Symbol (skip reason) and `#file_all` dispatches on `is_a?(Symbol)` (`:683-687,:729`); the Collision arm writes `conflict_folders[node.name] = dest` into a lazily-built ivar from inside a rescue (`:849,:874-878`); `#call` builds `Result` four times, three spelling out zeros (`:428,:441,:455,:473`); the consumer `ProjectFolderIntake#conflict_folder` (`:203-206`) falls back to re-deriving the folder from the name — the exact re-derivation that shipped the wrong "archives/" flash for a board zip (commit 521bfb30, which added the side hash rather than giving the skip its folder).
- Current complexity / invalid state: the shape can represent `moved != plan.size`, `skipped != skips.size`, a `:conflict` skip with no folder, and a folder with no matching skip; production keeps them consistent only by construction in one method; the fallback MASKS a broken side channel (deleting `:849` reddens only the board-zip test); readers need three shapes to answer "what happened to file X".
- Proposed representation: `Filing = Data.define(:name, :folder, :skip)` (one row per inbox file; `folder` = where it was filed, or for `:conflict` where `#place` TRIED); `Result = Data.define(:attached, :status, :filings)` with `plan`/`skips`/`moved`/`skipped` as derived readers KEEPING their current shapes (so the rake task, the job log, the scan notice and ~60 assertions read unchanged) and `conflict_folder(name)`; `#place` always returns a `Filing`; `#file_all` becomes one `each` with the FetchError rescue appending `:unavailable` Filings for `inbox[filings.size..]`; the concern drops its fallback. New names must stay off `ENGINE_ASSOCIATIONS` (never `conflicts`).
- Smallest scope: `project_intake.rb` (Filing, Result, `#place`/`#skip`/`#file_all`/`#call`, delete the memo and four defending comments); `project_folder_intake.rb` (`#left_unfiled_by_conflict?`, `#conflict_folder`); six hand-built Results in `project_scan_button_test.rb`; one assertion in `project_intake_test.rb:512`; `project-intake.md:383-393`.
- Risks: no schema or wire change (FilesController's JSON carries the sentence, not the Result); backend-neutral Ruby; a missed hand-built `Result.new(moved: …)` raises loudly (test-only); the fallback's removal changes behaviour only for Results built without a folder (test doubles); the FetchError abort must keep marking exactly the unanswered files (`inbox[filings.size..]` ≡ today's `inbox[processed..]`).
- Validation: existing project_intake (conflicts, bad names, refusals, README, working tree, dry run, FetchError cases), project_folder_intake (board zip → `pcb/`; exact dup.stl sentence), project_upload_attach, project_scan_button, addon save-door conflict tests, project_intake_job. New: "a conflict's Filing carries the folder #place tried". Mutation: record `folder: nil` in the Collision arm → EVERY conflict-sentence test goes red (today the equivalent mutation reddens one).
- Confidence: medium (behaviour-preserving with high confidence; the payoff is local — a side channel, a fallback, a type pun, a positional tuple and two derivable counters removed). Blast radius: one service + one concern. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T3; local and preventive — the invalid combinations are unreachable in production; the shape shipped one wrong flash, 521bfb30). Priority: P3. Independent (F-S24-1, which would have edited `ProjectIntakeJob`, is demoted).
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): ride-along — correct `project_intake.rb:599-602`, which says inbox order decides which files a capped run files; both caps are all-or-nothing (`:266-273`, `:320-326`) and order matters only for the FetchError abort this entry rewrites.

### F-S08-1 — One owner for "does this preview row need a job?": the STALLED_AFTER heal lives only inside `Enqueue.call`, and every door that renders a wedged row pre-filters on coordinates and never reaches it
- Authoritative row: S08. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S08.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `enqueue.rb:91-98` computes `stalled` (pending/processing older than STALLED_AFTER) and returns early otherwise; but `ThumbnailMap.backfill!` skips `map.current?(node_id)` (coordinate-current, status-blind: `thumbnail_map.rb:140,161`), `FilesController#current_thumbnail` (`:1231-1234`) returns a coordinate-current row before calling `Enqueue`, `#geometry` (`:479-483`) enqueues only `if preview.nil?`, `Public::FilesController#enqueue_geometry` (`:161-163`) returns early on a current row, and `MissingBackfill` (`:89-109`) compares a status-blind `(node_id, version_id, renderer_revision)` triple. `GeneratePreviewJob`'s `retry_on FetchError … attempts: 5` leaves the row at `processing` by design (`:65-70`) and its start write `update!(status: "processing")` (`:90`) is not compare-and-set. The documentation in `enqueue.rb:46-57` and `measure-view.md:320-333` claims the read doors reach the heal; they do not (and did not at c3bc2f57 either).
- Current complexity / invalid state: `pending`/`processing` are unverified assertions ("a job is coming"); the verifier exists but only `call` holds it, while five doors re-decide "does this row need a job?" with a weaker copy that drops `stalled` (and, on purpose, `failed`). A row wedged on current content — an unmounted share, an S3 blip, a down sync server, a lost job — stays wedged until its content or renderer revision moves: grids spin 40 polls then show "Preview not supported", the file page sits on "Generating preview…", `rake previews:backfill_missing` reports 0. The read doors' "never retry a failed render" policy exists only as a side-effect of those pre-filters.
- Proposed representation: `Enqueue.needs_job?(preview, version_id:, revision:, retry_failed:)` as THE transition predicate (new/missing → true; coordinates moved → true; `failed` → `retry_failed`; in-flight past STALLED_AFTER → true), used by `call` and by every door that already holds a row, with `retry_failed: false` at the read doors. `current_preview?` stays status-blind (the recorded §14h rule is untouched).
- Smallest scope: `enqueue.rb` (extract `needs_job?`, add `retry_failed:` default true = behaviour-neutral for all callers); `thumbnail_map.rb#backfill!`; `missing_backfill.rb` (load the batch's rows instead of the hand-kept triple); call sites `files_controller.rb#current_thumbnail`/`#geometry` (S10's file) and `public/files_controller.rb#enqueue_geometry`; correct the factual claims in `enqueue.rb:43-63` and `measure-view.md:320-333`.
- Risks: writes-on-GET grow only for rows already ≥15 min in flight, restamped so ≤1 job per row per window per door; polled doors (`#thumbnails`, public geometry poll) stay read-only; `retry_failed: false` must be passed at every read door or a `failed` row re-renders per view (pinned for the inspector and the public page). No schema/wire/UI change; identical on all three legs.
- Validation: existing enqueue window/restamp tests, inspector "failed never re-enqueued", maker_page_thumbnails, thumbnail_map query constancy, missing_backfill, v1 renderer-revision test. NEW request-level tests (red today): a Files page / file page re-enqueues a coordinate-current row created `processing` 3 days ago; `MissingBackfill.call` counts it; a row made `processing` now is NOT re-enqueued. Mutations: delete the stall line → red; drop `retry_failed: false` at `current_thumbnail` → inspector test red; drop the restamp → a second page load enqueues again.
- Confidence: high (every pre-filter read at HEAD and at the introducing commit; the only door-level stall coverage is the v1 API). Blast radius: the preview read doors. Effort: small. Prerequisite for F-S08-2's stall heal of `rebuilding` rows.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T1; defect-first — the documented `STALLED_AFTER` heal is unreachable from every read door; wedged rows observed in production). `current_preview?` stays status-blind as §14h requires. Effort corrected to small-medium (six files in three rows + three request tests). Priority: P1. Prerequisite for F-S08-2.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): Coordination — `public/files_controller.rb#enqueue_geometry` (S14). Extend the prose step to `enqueue.rb:65-67`'s caller list, which omits `Public::FilesController#enqueue_geometry` and names a nonexistent `V1::CommitsController`; coordinate with F-S21-2, which merges the two upload-door callers — whichever lands last fixes that comment.

### F-S08-2 — `ready` means two things during a renderer rebuild: give that window its own status (`rebuilding`) and name the three status classes once on `Maker::Preview`
- Authoritative row: S08. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S08.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `enqueue.rb:100-117` stamps the NEW `renderer_revision` on a renderer-only rebuild and leaves `status` at `ready` (deliberately, so the picture stays visible); `FilesController#geometry` (`:479-492`) answers `case preview.status when "ready" → url_for(preview.asset)` for any coordinate-current row, so on the client's second poll (after `Enqueue` stamped the revision but before the job ran) it serves the OLD build's geometry payload — the door's own comment says reading status "would hand back the stale blob URL — exactly the bug the revision check exists to stop"; `Public::FilesController#view` enqueues at render so the visitor's FIRST poll can serve the older build; `GeneratePreviewJob:90` flips the row to `processing` during the render anyway (contradicting "the old artifact stays visible until the job swaps it"); `preview.rb` has `STATUSES` and no classification; 15 literal `"ready"` checks and 5 `pending processing` literals across views/helpers/controllers (grep count verified). Geometry FORMAT_REVISION has been bumped four times (d5c662c6, cc2340ff, 5ce97ac0, f55a7f45), each re-opening the window.
- Current complexity / invalid state: one string column encodes both "what is in flight" and "what the attached artifact is", so "artifact is an older build of these coordinates, rebuild queued" is indistinguishable from "artifact matches these coordinates". The one reader that must refuse an older build (Measure, both doors) cannot tell; a lost rebuild is never healed (`ready` is not in the stall set); the picture is blanked during the render despite the rule; the classification is re-derived ~20 times.
- Proposed representation: `STATUSES += rebuilding`; `IN_FLIGHT = pending processing rebuilding` (stall set); `showable?` (ready|rebuilding with asset — a picture OF THIS CONTENT, any build) and `current_build?` (ready with asset — what a data consumer needs). Enqueue: renderer-only rebuild → `rebuilding` if showable, else `pending`; job start transition `pending → processing` only, through `commit_if_current`; picture surfaces ask `showable?`, data surfaces `current_build?`; both geometry doors' existing `case … when "ready"` become correct with no code change; v1 `preview_json` maps `rebuilding` → `"ready"` to keep the wire pinned by previews_renderer_revision_test.
- Smallest scope (in order): `preview.rb` (status, IN_FLIGHT, three predicates — neutral while nothing writes `rebuilding`); move the ~20 consumers onto the predicates (application_helper, seven partials, `files_controller#thumbnails`, `public/files_controller#preview`, v1 previews_controller) — still neutral; then `enqueue.rb` writes `rebuilding` and `generate_preview_job.rb`'s start transition; optional: gate `show.html.erb:188`'s settings panel on `showable?`; docs `enqueue.rb:102-116`, `measure-view.md:278-291`.
- Risks: ordering — a consumer still comparing `status == "ready"` when `rebuilding` ships shows a wrong terminal label (not data loss): do the consumer step first and add a grep/template guard refusing raw `"ready"` comparisons outside the model; v1 clients keep seeing `ready` (check tinkerex-client before exposing the new value); no migration (plain string + model inclusion); backend-neutral.
- Validation: update four representation pins that assert the raw string (enqueue_test:93, thumbnail_map_test:244, lbrn_geometry_test:215, previews_renderer_revision_test:96, keeping :97's wire `"ready"`), each paired with a behavioural assertion; NEW (red today): a stale-revision `ready` geometry row → GET enqueues → GET again without performing jobs answers `"pending"` (today `"ready"` + the old URL), and the public twin; a `rebuilding` row stays `showable?` while rendering; a `rebuilding` row past STALLED_AFTER is re-enqueued. Mutations: revert the `rebuilding` branch → red; drop `rebuilding` from `IN_FLIGHT` → red.
- Confidence: medium-high on the defect (traced, not run), medium on the exact shape. Blast radius: the preview status vocabulary across ~20 consumers. Effort: medium. Dependency: F-S08-1 first (cheaper, no status change, and it makes the `rebuilding` stall-heal reachable).
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2; adds state, justified — the lighter fixes either break measure-view.md:286-291 or need a migration). Counts corrected: 12 `status == "ready"` comparisons (+2 emitters, 1 comment) and 4 `%w[pending processing]` literals in views + 1 in `enqueue.rb:95`. Its first, neutral step (predicates on `Maker::Preview`) is useful alone and absorbs S10's repeated ready-and-attached check. Priority: P2; after F-S08-1.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): Coordination — the consumer step is a six-row edit: `application_helper.rb` (S20), `files/viewers/{_gerber,_model3d,_static}` (S22), `_measure` (S09), `files/_thumb` + `files_controller#thumbnails` (S10), `public/_project_card`, `public/projects/_page`, `public#preview` (S14), `v1/maker/previews_controller.rb` (S18). Prepend to the model step: delete the unused `scope :ready` (`preview.rb:30`) — this entry changes what `ready` means, so the dead scope goes here, and the demoted F-S23-2 ticket then drops only the `(user_id, status)` index, after F-S08-1/2. The ten `status == "ready" && …asset.attached?` sites are exactly this entry's consumer list (overlap pass re-counted).

### F-S03-1 — Give an S3 trash entry ONE shape (`.trash/<root id>/<root name>/<root-relative path>`, Share's layout) and drive `delete` and `undelete` from one subtree walk — three live bugs on the deployed s3 backend sit behind the two layouts
- Authoritative row: S03. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S03.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `s3.rb#delete` (`:480-517`) writes a file entry at `trash_key(node)` = `.trash/<node id>/<name>` (`:842-844`) while `#delete_directory!` (`:528-575`) writes each file at `.trash/<ROOT id>/<file's FULL storage_path>` (`:545-546`) — the column the same method then NULLs; its object set `user.nodes.where(node_id: ids).where.not(storage_path: nil).where(kind: "file")` (`:532`) has no `present` filter (scan tombstones KEEP their path and so re-enter the copy set) and excludes the `<key>/` marker objects `#mkdir` creates (`:417,:425`), which are therefore never copied or deleted; `#undelete` (`:704-786`) knows only the file layout (`src_key = trash_key(node)`, `destination_vacant!(… kind: "file")`), has no directory branch, and its own comment (`:721-724`) describes a layout (`<path under the dir>`) that the code does not write; Share has `revive_subtree!` (`share.rb:694-714`) pinned by share_test, S3 has no folder-restore code and no folder-restore test (grep of s3_test finds file-undelete tests only); `adapter_mutation_contract.rb` has no folder-restore case.
- Current complexity / invalid state (each traced statement by statement; FakeS3 models every wire step): (a) a folder holding any scan-tombstoned file (removed out-of-band, or an `etag:`-rung object renamed out-of-band) becomes PERMANENTLY undeletable — the copy of the missing source fails on every retry ("Couldn't reach your library. Try again."), and the phase-2 comment's recovery claim (`:552-555`) is false because scan tombstones keep their paths; (b) a deleted folder COMES BACK empty under new node_ids within 15 minutes, because its markers stay in the bucket, S3Observer reads a marker as a directory, and `Reconciler#ensure_dirs` cannot revive a NULL-path tombstone — this hits every app-made folder (project folders, "New folder"); (c) a deleted folder CANNOT be restored (`undelete(dir)` HEADs an object nothing writes → NotFound "Couldn't restore that item") and every child answers "restore the folder first" — a dead end until the purge job removes the bytes, while the Trash page (`trash_controller.rb:121-122`), the help page and CLAUDE.md §14a ("a refusal is recoverable — restore the folder, then the file") all promise the restore. Nested folders additionally depend on the root's FORMER path, which the cascade NULLs.
- Proposed representation: one entry rule — `entry_key(root, rel)` = `.trash/<root id>/<root name>[/<rel>]` with `rel` computed from node names along the parent edge (the walk `revive_subtree!` already uses), never from `storage_path`; the object set = the subtree's LIVE nodes' keys plus each directory's marker where one exists (existence by HEAD, never inferred from a failed COPY — SeaweedFS answers InvalidArgument); a file root is the degenerate walk of one (byte-identical keys to today; root-level folder entries also identical, so existing key assertions hold); `undelete` reads exactly what `delete` wrote and revives top-down as Share does, with a compensating delete on partial failure.
- Smallest scope: `s3.rb` only, in two shippable steps — (1) delete side (`.present` + markers in the object set, root-relative destination keys, markers deleted in phase 2) fixes (a) and (b) with no interface change; (2) undelete side (directory walk, prefix vacancy check, top-down revive mirroring `share.rb:694-714`) fixes (c); then cleanup (fold the file branch, delete the dead `trashable_file!` and the stale "BOTH verbs refuse" block, correct `:721-724`). `TrashPurgeJob` (groups by the first segment after `.trash/`), the observer, the controllers, `trashed` and the port signature are untouched.
- Risks: old-layout entries for NESTED folders are missed by the new restore (unrestorable today anyway; age out under LIBRARY_TRASH_DAYS); billing: one HEAD per directory plus copy/delete per marker on delete, HEAD+COPY+DELETE per object on restore, bounded by `DIR_DELETE_MAX_OBJECTS` (keep the cap counting files); one explicit decision — apply `copy_source_if_match` uniformly (a folder whose child changed since the last scan refuses until the next scan, the single-file path's existing behaviour); ordering invariants preserved (copy → delete → tombstone LAST; copy → revive → sweep last); the residual is a compensation that itself fails, the same class as the scan race already accepted at `:700-703`. Every S3 test injects FakeS3, so the three legs run them identically. Semantics: Share revives the whole subtree; chunk_remote's journey restores the child with a second POST; a shared contract must accept both.
- Validation: existing s3_test delete/undelete/folder-delete/cap suites, the mutation contract, s3_write_test, s3_library_test, trash_purge_job_test. NEW in s3_test, each red today: "a folder holding a file the scan tombstoned still deletes" (mutation: drop `.present` → red); "a deleted folder stays deleted across a scan" (mutation: drop markers → red); "restoring a directory returns the subtree with the same node ids and the same bytes" + "an empty directory deletes and restores too" ported from share_test (mutation: send a dir root down the file-only branch → red); "a nested folder's entry is root-relative"; "a folder restore whose copy fails partway revives nothing and leaves no copied-back object" via `fault_after!` (mutation: drop the compensation → red). Optional (S01): a backend-agnostic folder delete+restore contract case.
- Confidence: high that (a)-(c) are real and reachable on the deployed s3 backend; medium-high on the design (no S3 atomicity; a narrow compensation residual). Blast radius: the S3 trash keyspace and two verbs. Effort: medium. Priority: high (data-facing defects on a live deployment; a false claim in §14a).
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T1; one slice with F-S02-1). SUPERSEDED IN PART by F-S02-1 (§5): the undelete MEMBERSHIP rule as written ("revives top-down as Share does", mirroring `share.rb:694-712`) is the existence-at-rebuilt-key inference §5 supersedes — membership comes from `nodes.trash_root_node_id`; this entry keeps the S3 ENTRY-KEY SHAPE, the marker handling and the one subtree walk. Step 1 (the delete side) is narrow and ships on its own. Confidence reworded: high through FakeS3's wire model, unprobed on the live SeaweedFS bucket (marker listing, COPY answering InvalidArgument) — assert the WIRE key shape in FakeS3 (§14e). Priority: P1.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): the tombstone-set half is superseded too — audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S03.md:94 keeps `Subtree.node_ids` for the S3 cascade's `update_all` (`s3.rb:568-569`); F-S02-1 narrows that UPDATE to live rows and both adapters follow. Step 2 (the folder restore) never ships before slice (B): its existence-at-key membership reproduces defect (b) on S3; and the blank-ETag hardening (the demoted F-S03-2 ticket) lands before step 2, because a folder restore reaches `#restored_stat` (`s3.rb:999-1006`) once per member. Slicing: (A) step 1 alone, no migration; (B) with F-S02-1's column.

### F-S03-2 — One rule for the facts an S3 store response carries: `ObjectIdentity.for_reported` owns the blank-ETag answer at the adapter's three refresh sites, and FakeS3's copy result carries `LastModified` as the real wire does [DEMOTED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication; not ranked in §6]
- Authoritative row: S03. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S03.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — three hand-rolled guards answer "the store gave no usable ETag": `s3.rb#move:675-676` (`if copied_etag.present?`), `#restored_stat:1002` (`if head.etag.present?`, rescue covers only ServiceError/NetworkingError), `#store_identity:1120-1129` (`present?` + `rescue ArgumentError`); the review fix b3741f76 that found the literally quoted-empty `""` passes `present?` but still raises in `normalize_etag` reached `store_identity` ONLY, and its comment calls skipping "the direction #move and #restored_stat already take" — those two still use the insufficient `present?` test, and they are the two sites whose failure the code calls the worst (after `delete_object` removed the source; after the copy-back and before the revive). FakeS3's `do_copy` returns `{ copy_object_result: { etag: } }` with no `last_modified` (`fake_s3.rb:205`) while aws-sdk-s3's `CopyObjectResult` carries `:last_modified` (types.rb, verified); `move`'s comment (`:669-673`) cites the fake's omission to justify not refreshing mtime — §14e's "a fake that shares the code's misunderstanding confirms it".
- Current complexity / invalid state: one decision has three spellings and only one handles `""`; a store answering a copy or HEAD with `""` makes `move` raise with the bytes only at the new key and the row naming the old one, and makes `undelete` strand a restored object behind a trashed row (the next scan mints a new node_id). After every move the row keeps the source's mtime until the next scan rewrites it (one UPDATE + one preview enqueue per moved file).
- Proposed representation: `ObjectIdentity::BlankEtag < ArgumentError` raised by `normalize_etag`; `ObjectIdentity.for_reported(etag:)` → identity or nil (rescuing only `BlankEtag`) for the adapter's three REFRESH sites (the observer keeps `for`, whose raise is §14a's totality guard); FakeS3's copy result and the `retag_copies!` test helper carry `last_modified:`; `move` takes the mtime from the copy result when present, presence-guarded like the ETag. `write`'s unguarded `ObjectIdentity.for(etag: put.etag)` stays raising (a created node needs an identity).
- Smallest scope: `object_identity.rb` (+BlankEtag, +for_reported); `s3.rb:669-676, 999-1006, 1120-1129`; `fake_s3.rb:205`; `s3_test.rb:1080-1090`.
- Risks: no data/API change; observer path untouched (totality holds; `Reconciler#plan_moves`'s nil-drop stays unreachable); the mtime half depends on stores filling `CopyObjectResult.LastModified` — keep the presence guard and compare precision with the listing's on the self-host SeaweedFS before relying on it; FakeS3-only test change, so the three legs behave identically.
- Validation: existing F-04 refresh tests and blank-ETag tests in s3_test, object_identity_test. New: `for_reported` returns nil for nil/`""`/quoted-empty/whitespace and equals `for` otherwise (mutation: plain `for` → red on quoted-empty); a move with `retag_copies!('""')` keeps the previous identity and lands (today raises; mutation: restore `present?` → red); an undelete whose HEAD answers `'""'` completes; a FakeS3 self-test that a copy result carries `last_modified` (mutation: drop the field → red); a move sets the row's mtime to the copy's (mutation: drop the refresh → red).
- Confidence: high on the facts (code and SDK read), medium on value (the quoted-empty case needs a misbehaving store; the mtime half is cosmetic and heals at the next scan). Blast radius: three adapter sites + the fake. Effort: small. Priority: low.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): DEMOTED → §4 hardening ticket. The drift is real (`present?` at `s3.rb:675-676,1002` vs the `ArgumentError` rescue only in `store_identity` `:1120-1129`; FakeS3's copy result omits `last_modified`), but the trigger needs a store that sends blank ETags on COPY/HEAD while sending real ones on LIST — no such store is in evidence (b3741f76 added the guard in review, not from an observed store) — and the mtime half heals at the next scan. Two-line hardening + a fake-fidelity fix; `BlankEtag`/`for_reported` would be two names for a hypothetical input. Not ranked in §6.

### F-S09-1 — The Measure view's phase has two owners (a CSS class and ten controller fields): give it one — a load generation, a shared in-flight fetch and rest-on-connect — as the Gerber and 3D viewers already do
- Authoritative row: S09. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S09.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `measure_controller.js#load` (`:125-135`) is unguarded (no generation, no shared promise; only `this.aborted`, set in `disconnect`), `#connect` (`:95-118`) builds fresh `marked`/`nodeByShape` and never reconciles with the element it attaches to, `#showPoster` (`:141-150`) cannot cancel a load in flight, `#draw` adds `measure-view--ready` (`:235`) and `preview.css:234-248` derives launch/actions visibility from that class alone, `restingStatus()` dereferences `this.allShapes.length` unguarded (`:695`), and four comments argue correctness from "the button is display:none" (`:340-346`, `:742-746`, `refreshPrintBar`, `measure_print_test.rb:3110-3117`). The sibling controllers hold the fix: `gerber_controller.js` keeps `this.generation`, shares one in-flight `this.pending`, adopts a result only if it still owns the stage, bumps on `showPoster`/`disconnect` and calls `rest()` on connect with an explicit Turbo-snapshot rationale (`:78-120,:195-200`); `modelviewer_controller.js` bumps `generation` on `showPoster` and checks it after `boot` (`:80-95,:216-220`); commit ba5d6119 found and pinned the Gerber race. Turbo Drive is on app-wide (`application.js`) with no cache-control exemption in any view.
- Current complexity / invalid state (each follows from the cited lines): (1) a second Measure click during "Generating geometry…" runs two poll loops, two payload downloads (up to ~1.3 MB gzipped) and two `draw()`s — the later draw resets the viewBox and the selection made on the first SVG survives in `marked`, so the bar says "Print 2 parts" over an SVG with nothing highlighted; (2) ✕ Image while a second load is in flight — the late `draw()` re-adds `--ready` and reopens the dismissed view; (3) Back to a file page whose Measure was open — Turbo restores a `--ready` snapshot (SVG, marks, "Print 1 part") onto a fresh controller with `allShapes`/`svg`/`panZoom` undefined: contours, pan and zoom are dead, Print/Download/Save silently no-op, Reset throws `TypeError` — the state the tests call unreachable.
- Proposed representation: `this.generation` + `this.pending` in `load()` (a second click joins the first fetch; a superseded load paints nothing and reports no error); `rest()` (today's `showPoster` body) called from `connect()` and `showPoster()`, which also bumps the generation; `draw()` creates `marked`/`nodeByShape`/`hovered` fresh. Optional: bundle the per-draw fields into one `this.view` and resolve hover through `nodeByShape` so the second shape→element resolution goes away. DOM becomes derived from the controller instead of a second owner.
- Smallest scope: `measure_controller.js` only (~20 lines; same ERB targets/values, CSS, wire format; `shape_svg`/`print_sheet`/`export_svg`/`svg_pan_zoom` untouched); rewrite the four "display:none so unreachable" prose sites to argue from controller state.
- Risks: client-only, no data/API/backend-leg difference (same JSON on all three legs; identical run counts); after Back the user sees the poster (the Gerber behaviour); a superseded load's rejection must not overwrite the owner's status (the `gen ===` check in `catch`); keep `draw()`'s documented ordering (no `await` between `replaceChildren` and `bindInteractions`; `populatePaper` after binding; `refreshPrintBar` last). If `this.view` is bundled, ~7 probe sites in `measure_print_test.rb` change.
- Validation: existing "✕ Image drops the selection", "Reset returns the view home", "marking an open contour", "printing before the geometry has loaded", "the controller holds what the printer will need", plus measure_units/export/formats and lbrn_measure. NEW system tests: (1) "a second Measure click while generating joins the first fetch and draws once" using the `gerber_test.rb:331-374` recipe (delayed counting `fetch` stub + a `draw` counter; mutations: drop `pending ||=` → 2 fetches; drop the generation check → 2 draws); (2) "Back to a file page whose Measure view was open rests at the poster" — set a body-dataset probe, navigate away via a Turbo link, `go_back`, assert the probe FIRST (proves the snapshot path; no system test drives Back today), then no `--ready`, launch visible, "Print all", and Measure → Reset with no console errors (mutation: drop `rest()` from `connect()` → the restored stage survives and Reset logs the TypeError).
- Confidence: high on the mechanism (every state follows from cited lines; the siblings document and fix the identical hazards; the Gerber race was measured and pinned), medium on frequency. Blast radius: one controller. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2; defect-first — Turbo Back restores a dead Measure view, a double click runs two loads). Applies files-preview.md:277-278's reset-on-connect rule with the Gerber sibling's mechanism; leave the optional `this.view` bundling out of the first slice. Sibling of F-S22-1: share `test/system/support/web_helpers.rb`'s Turbo-Back helper — whichever lands first adds it. Priority: P2.

### F-S02-1 — Record which trash entry a tombstone belongs to (`nodes.trash_root_node_id`) instead of inferring it from four signals: the folder cascade rewrites tombstones it does not own, and folder restore guesses which rows to revive
- Authoritative row: S02 (with S03 changed in the SAME slice — see the amendment under F-S03-1 in §5). Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S02.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `share.rb:422-424` cascades `update_all(deleted: true, storage_path: nil, updated_at: now)` over `Subtree.node_ids`, which includes tombstones by design (`subtree.rb:17-18`: "so `undelete` has the full set to revive"); `revive_subtree!` (`:694-712`) revives any walked row whose rebuilt path exists on disk, writes `size`/`mtime` but never `content_hash`, and its candidate query has no ORDER; `trashed` is `where(deleted: true, storage_path: nil)` (`:81-82`); `undelete`'s membership test is the PARENT row's shape (`:554-558`); the scan tombstone KEEPS its path (`reconciler.rb:161-162`); the partial unique index on `(user_id, storage_path) WHERE deleted = false` is unvalidated in `Node`, and `TrashController` rescues NotFound/UnsupportedOperation/FetchError but not `RecordNotUnique` (`:36-67`); the purge job removes `.trash` DIRECTORIES by age and never a row (`trash_purge_job.rb:98-102`); the design premise "no column needed" predates the cascade (phase-2b plan vs ae996173). The S03 reviewer independently traced the same root cause on S3 (`s3.rb:530-532` assumes a scan-tombstoned descendant has a NULL path; it does not).
- Current complexity / invalid state (traced, not run; no test puts a tombstone inside a later-deleted folder): (a) a file removed in Finder (scan tombstone, path kept) inside a folder later deleted in the browser is converted into a TRASH tombstone, listed in /trash with a Restore that can never work, and stays listed forever after the folder is restored; (b) the ordinary replace workflow (delete `x.stl` → trash tombstone A; upload corrected `x.stl` → live B; later delete F, restore F) makes `revive_subtree!` reach both A and B, the second `update!` violates the partial unique index → 500 mid-restore (bytes already renamed back, F live), rows the BFS had not reached keep NULL paths over bytes back on disk, and the next scan mints NEW node_ids for them — every attachment, BOM line and tag orphaned silently (§14a's landmine); or A goes live over B's bytes with A's OLD `content_hash` and the scan's (mtime,size) skip never re-hashes it; (c) every older tombstone inside F has `updated_at` re-stamped, restarting `Maker::DeadNodes.reapable?`'s 30-day grace; (d) `undelete` spends 28 lines arguing which proxy separates "inside another entry" from "destination folder trashed".
- Proposed representation: one nullable `nodes.trash_root_node_id` (NULL = live or scan tombstone; == own node_id = an entry root; other = moved with that root), written by `delete` for `user.nodes.present` rows only and read back by `undelete` (`trash_root_node_id.nil?` → NotFound "removed outside the app"; `!= node_id` → "restore the folder first"; members revived from `where(trash_root_node_id: root)` by the existing top-down BFS). Trash rows KEEP a NULL `storage_path`, so §14a's two-kinds rule and revive-by-path's inability to fire hold by construction. Coordinator note: `trash_root_node_id IS NOT NULL ⇔ deleted AND storage_path IS NULL` must be asserted (a guard test, or `trashed` reads the column) so the two spellings of "trash" cannot drift. A no-migration first step (`.present` on the cascade + `revive_subtree!` skipping rows that kept a path or own a `.trash/<id>` entry) closes (a), (c) and half of (b) but NOT the purged-entry case ("replace a file, then delete and restore the folder months later"), which is what forces a recorded fact.
- Smallest scope: migration (+column, +index, backfill each `deleted AND storage_path IS NULL` row to the topmost such ancestor, depth-capped; chunk_remote rows stay NULL). S23 CONSISTENCY VERDICT: fits structurally with THREE convention fixes before it lands — (1) NAME it `trash_root_node_id`: all 20 columns holding a `nodes.node_id` are `node_id`/`*_node_id`, a bare `*_id` means an integer `id`, and `nodes` has both, so `trash_root_node_id` reads as `nodes.id`; (2) make the index the schema's existing partial shape `(user_id, trash_root_node_id) WHERE trash_root_node_id IS NOT NULL` (precedents in the auto-catalog and folder-claim migrations) so live-row inserts pay nothing; (3) draw it in `SchemaMap::EDGES` (`nodes.trash_root_node_id → nodes.node_id`, share/s3 only) and make the backfill self-contained (a migration-local model, never `Node` or `FileLayer::Subtree`, per the rederive migrations' rule). The column records MEMBERSHIP, not existence (after the purge the members still name a gone entry — today's "purged?" NotFound, fine); `share.rb` `delete`/`undelete`/`revive_subtree!` (+`trashed` may read the field); `s3.rb` in the same change (`delete`, `delete_directory!` live rows only, `trashed`, the orphan check); optional `TrashController#restore_refusal_message` naming the entry root.
- Risks: rows already converted by (a) cannot be told apart by the backfill and stay listed; `TrashEntry` and every port signature unchanged; chunk_remote must never read the column (its trash is the server's); /trash keeps listing folder members minus converted scan tombstones; do NOT narrow `Subtree.node_ids` itself (the walk is still right) — narrow the adapters' UPDATE. New tests live in share_test/s3_test, which run on all three legs (identical run counts).
- Validation: existing share_test/share_trash_test/mutation-contract/s3_test folder tests stay green.
