NEW: "a file deleted on the NAS inside a later-deleted folder is never offered for restore" (mutation: drop `.present` → red); "restoring a folder revives exactly what its delete moved" with A's entry purged (mutation: revert to walk + `File.exist?` → RecordNotUnique or A revived → red); "a folder delete does not re-stamp an older tombstone" using `travel_to(3.days.ago)` (never `.to_i`); the s3 mirror.
- Confidence: high that (a)-(c) are live defects; medium-high that one column is the minimal exact representation. Blast radius: both writable adapters' trash + one migration. Effort: medium. Priority: high (data-facing; same slice as F-S03-1).
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T1; same slice as F-S03-1). Column named `trash_root_node_id` throughout (its own S23 verdict; the `*_node_id` convention). Confidence reworded: high on the mechanism (traced statement by statement), unreproduced — no test puts a tombstone inside a later-deleted folder, which the new tests add. The column is a second spelling of "trash tombstone" beside the NULL path (§14a's two kinds), so the equivalence guard is REQUIRED. Ship the one-word `.present` change on the cascade first; it is correct on its own. Classify the column in `SchemaMap::EDGES` (F-S23-1 lands before or with this). Priority: P1.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): Coordination — optional `TrashController#restore_refusal_message` (S10); rewrite `subtree.rb:17-18` (S01's file: the walk keeps tombstones so none is left live by omission; membership is `trash_root_node_id`, NOT the walk — its second clause becomes false). §5 refined: the trash work is TWO consistent slices — (A) no migration: the `.present` cascade here + F-S03-1 step 1 with a live-rows-only object set AND tombstone UPDATE (S3's `update_all` at `s3.rb:568-569` today walks `Subtree.node_ids`, tombstones included, turning scan tombstones into trash tombstones exactly as defect (a)/(c) does on Share); (B) migration: this column on both adapters + F-S03-1 step 2 (the S3 folder restore reading it). S03 rejected this column BY NAME (audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S03.md:205) arguing root-relative keys make the entry LOCATION derivable — that does not answer MEMBERSHIP (which rows a given delete moved), which defect (b) turns on; the column stands over that rejection.

### F-S02-2 — A directory move rewrites its descendants' `storage_path` in the same call, so the path and the parent edge never disagree after a port write [DEMOTED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication; not ranked in §6]
- Authoritative row: S02. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S02.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `share.rb:499` updates only the moved row; the adapter's own comment (`:440-452`) calls cascading the rewrite "the right end state … NOT in this phase's plan"; `FilesController` refuses folder rename/move with `FOLDERS_NOT_YET` (`:1013-1022`) "until Share#move rewrites the subtree's paths in the same call"; `subtree.rb:12-15` gives the same staleness as the reason the walk is by edge; `share_test.rb:860-886` pins the stale descendant path; `revive_subtree!` already rebuilds descendant paths top-down from the parent edge.
- Current complexity / invalid state: `move` is the one port write that leaves LIVE rows whose `storage_path` contradicts `parent.storage_path/name`; until the next scan every descendant reads as NotFound and `write(replace: true)`/`mkdir` inside the moved folder mint a second node; at the scan every nested directory gets a new node_id (dir ids carry project and model `folder_node_id`), the whole subtree is re-hashed, and a file unreadable at that moment is tombstoned and re-created. No production door reaches it today (FOLDERS_NOT_YET; `AttachToProject#refusal_for` `:not_a_file`; `ProjectIntake#place` files only), so the present cost is a known-bad mode in a port verb plus a blocked browser feature.
- Proposed representation: after the rename, one `rebase_descendants!` statement over the parent-edge walk (`Subtree.node_ids(...).drop(1)`) rewriting only rows that still carry the OLD prefix (`||`/`substr`, identical on SQLite and Postgres; a descendant already stale from an unscanned Finder change is left for the scan), plus an explicit refusal of a move into the folder's own subtree (today rename(2) EINVAL surfaces as FetchError "try again"). Alternative: lift `revive_subtree!`'s BFS into one path-rebuild walk used by both `move` and `undelete`.
- Smallest scope: `share.rb#move` + helper + refusal; rewrite `share_test.rb:860-886`; update the two comments. Separate slice outside this row: lift FOLDERS_NOT_YET where the port accepts a directory (s3 still refuses via `movable_file!` → 422).
- Risks: a stale LIVE row under the destination prefix hits the partial unique index (the moved row already has this exposure) — atomic, descendants stay as stale as today and the scan heals; scan tombstones inside the folder move with it (their kept path is rebased), so revive-by-path fires at the new location — what a Finder rename means; no data migration; no leg divergence.
- Validation: existing share move/mutation/write-contract tests. NEW: every descendant (incl. a nested sub-folder's file) reads back immediately; across the next scan the nested sub-folder keeps its node_id and no file is re-hashed (spy `Digest::SHA256.file` via the house `#replacing` helper); `write(replace: true)` into the moved folder reuses the node; a move into its own subtree is UnsupportedOperation; an already-stale descendant is left alone. Mutations: delete the `rebase_descendants!` call → first three red; drop the prefix guard → stale-descendant test red; drop the refusal → FetchError instead.
- Confidence: medium (mechanics high and in-file; payoff lands when FOLDERS_NOT_YET is lifted). Blast radius: one verb. Effort: small. Dependency: none; enables a web feature.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): DEMOTED → §4, attached to the FOLDERS_NOT_YET follow-up. No production door can move a directory today (`files_controller.rb:1022,1046` refuses with `FOLDERS_NOT_YET` and already names this port change as "its own slice", `:1018-1021`; S3 refuses directory moves), so the invalid state is unreachable at HEAD. The design (a prefix-guarded rebase of descendants' `storage_path` in the same call; refuse moving a folder into its own subtree) is recorded for that slice. Not ranked in §6.

### F-S14-1 — One "shown" answer for a visitor's project page: hiding decided by ANCESTRY against the saved layout, one `shown_rows` list for every derived reader, and a thumbnail map built from what the page paints (the /u/:handle card included)
- Authoritative row: S14. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S14.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `public_project.rb` answers four page-assembly questions on four bases: LISTED is the capped listing (`project_folder_listing.rb:117,127`, FILE_CAP 300 name-ordered); DELIVERABLE is `permits?`/`claimed?` by ancestry-or-attachment (`:456-481`, "never by the capped listing"); HIDDEN is two sets built from the CAPPED tree (`hidden_file_ids:147-150`, `hidden_dir_ids:154-157`) applied one reader at a time in seven spellings (`:123,:299,:313,:328,:342,:365,:381`), with only the comment at `:139-146` telling a new reader to filter ("the first two surfaces that forgot … were found by review"); PAINTED is `thumbs` = `listing.node_ids + page_nodes.keys` (`:429-431`), filtered by neither. `permitted_cover_ids` (`:491-513`) asks `claimed?` and `visible` and never reads a layout; `bundle_files` rejects backups (`:298`) while `bundle_path_for` uses `listing.row_for` (`:315`); `bundle_files`' comment (`:294-296`) contradicts its own code and test.
- Current complexity / invalid state (traced, not reproduced): (a) /u/:handle shows a HIDDEN folder's picture as a project card's cover while the project page's hero refuses the same node; (b) past FILE_CAP/DIR_CAP a hidden folder's unlisted files are not in `hidden_file_ids`, so `cover_node` (`find_by`) and `block_image_node` (`page_nodes`) can advertise them — an inline image renders from its bytes as the hero, "the Photos leak in its biggest frame"; (c) on an anonymous GET `thumbs` backfills (queues renders for) backups, hidden folders' files and BOM/release targets the page never paints, in name-ordered budget walk, while OMITTING a cover attached from outside the folder (not in the listing nor `page_nodes`), so a non-inline Linked cover's hero stays blank forever even with a ready row; (d) a BOM line pointing at a `_backup` gets a zip path in the CSV for an entry the zip never carries.
- Proposed representation: `hidden_dir?(dir_id)` decided from the lineage `claimed?` already walks (`depth_one_dir` → `layout.hidden?(folder_key)`), memoised; `shown?(node) = permits?(node) && !hidden?(node)` for `cover_node`/`block_image_node`; `shown_rows = listing.all_rows.reject { backup? || hidden? }` as the ONE start for every DERIVED reader (bundle, CSV path, KiCad rows, photos, repo cards); AUTHORED prose (README/block links, BOM/release links) keeps reading `listing` + `permits?` (decision 2: hidden is not unpermitted); `thumbs` = `[cover] + shown_rows + live entry photos + live block images`; `permitted_cover_ids` adds one layout check over the `panels` column the batch already loaded. Deletes `hidden_folder_ids`/`hidden_file_ids`/`hidden_dir_ids` and the seven inline filters.
- Smallest scope: `public_project.rb` (predicates, `shown_rows`, re-point seven readers, `thumbs`, `permitted_cover_ids`, two comment rewrites); `_page.html.erb:124-128` comment; `folder-panels-design.md:61-64`. No schema/route/manifest/CSV-shape change; `ProjectPhotos` untouched.
- Risks: the intended behaviour changes are exactly (a)-(d); the CARD change (a) is the one change to an existing surface and needs the owner's yes (spec decision 10 names "the hero", not the card); everything is in memory over the tree/listing/page_nodes already loaded and `ThumbnailMap.for` costs the same constant queries for one more id (the four public budgets keep their shape); a broken chain reads as not hidden (same as today for anything unlisted); fewer backfill jobs per anonymous GET; the owner's preview changes identically; leg-neutral.
- Validation: existing public_project/public_bundle/bom_csv/page_query_budget tests stay green. NEW (each red today): card hides a hidden folder's cover (mutation: delete the `next if`); cap — FILE_CAP rows sorting before a hidden `z.jpg` set as cover → no hero and no image block (mutation: decide hidden from the capped subtree); painted — a project-attached STL outside the folder as cover with a ready row renders the hero (mutation: drop cover from the union); backfill — an anonymous GET creates no `maker_previews` row for a hidden folder's renderable file or a root `_backup.lbrn2` but does for a visible model (stamp `content_hash` so the test is not vacuous; mutation: restore `listing.node_ids + page_nodes.keys`); CSV — a BOM line at a backup gets its bare name (mutation: `bundle_path_for` reads `listing.row_for`).
- Confidence: high on (a)-(d), medium-high on the shape. Blast radius: one service. Effort: small-medium. Sibling of F-S15-1 (the maker's page has the same "what does this page paint" drift); the two can share the "one painted set feeds the thumbnail map" idea but touch different files.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2; defect-first). Confidence reworded: high on the mechanism (traced), not reproduced — (b)'s past-FILE_CAP leak was traced, not driven. `permits?` stays "the ONE answer" (§14d) because "hidden is not unpermitted" is kept; the /u/:handle card change needs the owner's yes. Priority: P2. Sibling of F-S15-1.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): S14 considered and rejected one shared viewer-chain partial for the eight-branch chain spelled twice (`files/show.html.erb:109-182`, `public/files/view.html.erb:64-102`): it contradicts the recorded per-page decision at `files/show.html.erb:103-108` — the duplication stays a §4 note.

### F-S13-1 — The commit box computes each path's row ONCE: one per-request `Staging` row set feeds both `#show` and `#create`, `binary` moves onto `LocalRepo::Row`, and node ids are returned instead of a side-channel ivar
- Authoritative row: S13. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S13.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `repo_commits_controller.rb#show:28-37`: `reconcile` builds a `Row` per pending path via `status_for` (a full content read + HEAD blob lookup each, `local_repo.rb:255-270`), then `full_status` builds a `Row` for EVERY dirty path again (`:103`), `rest` throws away the pending twins, and `binary_paths` (`:200-212`) re-opens every listed modified/added/ignored file for an 8 kB sniff; `reconcile` (`:121-131`) returns `[keep, dropped]` AND writes `@node_id_by_path`, which `#create`'s `node_ids_for` (`:213`) reads by call order; `#create` (`:52-53`) calls `reconcile` then `committable_paths`, which calls `full_status` a second time (`:151-153`); the authority ("what the page offered") is defined twice — implicitly by #show + the view's `checked:`/`committable?`, explicitly by `committable_paths` — and `:45-50` records that the two already disagreed once and shipped ("the commit answered Nothing selected").
- Current complexity / invalid state: each dirty pending file is read in full twice by `row_for`, once more by libgit2's status hash, then sniffed again; HEAD blobs looked up twice; every other listed file costs one extra open; four parallel path-keyed structures (Row, the view's literal groups, `@binary_paths`, `@node_id_by_path`) must agree by key; `node_ids_for` depends on call order. Cost estimated from the codebase's own ~15 ms/object CIFS figure (`local_repo.rb:84-87`, git-editing.md:531-535): seconds at the 500-path cap. Not measured.
- Proposed representation: `Row = Data.define(:path, :state, :additions, :deletions, :binary)` with `binary` decided in `row_for` while the bytes are in hand (`BINARY_SNIFF_BYTES` moves to LocalRepo); one `staging` builder returning `Staging(pending, other, clutter, pending_node_ids, dropped, truncated_count)` with `offered_paths` = the committable rows of all three groups — `#create` intersects `params[:paths]` with THAT, so the authority IS the render; `full_status` rows are reused for pending paths and `status_for` is asked only for pending rows `full_status` did not compute (`.gitignore`'d-untracked, past the cap, unchanged, missing). Deletes `binary_paths`, `committable_paths`, `node_ids_for`, `@binary_paths`, the ivar write. The safety-critical `checked:` literal stays in the view.
- Smallest scope: `local_repo.rb` (Row + sniff), `repo_commits_controller.rb` (builder), `repo_commits/show.html.erb` (read `@staging` + `row.binary`), git-editing.md:585 pointer. `status_for`/`full_status` have no other callers; no schema change.
- Risks: the box renders only where `local_worktree?` (s3/chunk_remote see the pinned redirect); `#create` now pays an 8 kB read for rare `:ignored` rows; within one request a pending row and its full_status twin come from one moment. Do NOT move `checked:` out of the view.
- Validation: the nine existing membership/checked/binary/gitignore/dropped/truncated/other-commits/deletion/ignored-path pins in `repo_edit_flow_test.rb`, `local_repo_test.rb`, system `repo_staging`/`repo_editor`. NEW: "a NUL in the first 8 kB marks a row binary; text and a NUL after 8 kB do not" (mutation: sniff the whole file / never sniff); "a ticked OS-clutter path commits" (POST `paths: [".DS_Store"]` — no test posts a clutter path today; mutation: drop clutter from the lists → red with the membership test); optional per-path `File.binread` count ≤ 1 via the house `replacing` helper (mutation: restore the separate sniff).
- Confidence: medium-high (duplicate reads, side channel and two definitions read off the code; drift shipped once; I/O saving estimated). Blast radius: one controller + one service + one view. Effort: small-medium.
- Coordinator adjudication (schema pass, 2026-10-19): the bare "(`:103`)" after the `local_repo.rb` citation means `local_repo.rb:103` (`full_status`). KEPT (T2; the authority defined twice shipped wrong once). The I/O saving is estimated from the ~15 ms/object CIFS figure, not measured. Priority: P2. Independent of F-S13-2 (different files).

### F-S13-2 — `CommitPaths` decides each selected path ONCE (`read_entry` → `{oid, mode} | nil`), and writes both the tree and the index from that one read, so the write window is the two operations the spec names
- Authoritative row: S13. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S13.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `commit_paths.rb#call:46-53`: `paths.each { stage }` (each leaf reads/hashes/stats the file: `Rugged::Blob.from_workdir` + `filemode_for`, `:91-92`; `File.exist?` is re-asked at every recursion level despite "ASKED ONCE" at `:86`), then `Rugged::Commit.create(… update_ref:)` moves the ref, then `refresh_index` (`:158-166`) asks `File.exist?`, `Blob.from_workdir` and `filemode_for` AGAIN after the ref moved; the class header (`:22-24`), git-editing.md:183-185 and spec part 8 rule 3 all say only the ref update and the index write belong in that window.
- Current complexity / invalid state: each committed path is read, hashed and stat-checked twice (≈ d+3 stats and two reads per path of depth d); existence/bytes/mode are decided twice, the second time after the commit landed; if the maker's editor or desktop sync writes the file over SMB between `stage` and `refresh_index`, the index records a blob, mode or deletion the commit did not — `git status` then shows the path staged-and-different or as a staged deletion nobody asked for, silently, which is the "the app half-did something" state the class exists to prevent; two explanatory comments (`:69-75`, `:106-108`) exist only because `stage` reads mid-recursion.
- Proposed representation: `entries = paths.to_h { |rel| [rel, read_entry(rel)] }` (THE one place a selected path is read: nil when gone, else `{oid:, mode:}`); `stage(builder, segments, entry)` (no `rel`, no reads — symmetric with `remove`); `refresh_index(entries)` does in-memory index edits + `index.write` with no file I/O after the ref moved; `remove` and the `index[rel]` guard unchanged.
- Smallest scope: `commit_paths.rb` (`call`, `stage`, `refresh_index`, +`read_entry`), `commit_paths_test.rb`, git-editing.md:609-610. Public interface and its only caller unchanged.
- Risks: identical behaviour without a concurrent write; every git-editing.md invariant holds (no `index.clear`; unselected deletions survive; emptied dirs pruned; HEAD-seeded builder); all reads now happen before any tree object is written, so an unreadable file aborts earlier, never after. Commits run only where `local_worktree?`; no leg/schema impact.
- Validation: all 16 `commit_paths_test.rb` cases + `repo_edit_flow_test` commit cases + `repo_staging_test`. NEW: "the index records exactly the blob the commit recorded, even if the file changes after it was read" — wrap `Rugged::Commit.create` with the house `replacing` helper to write the file after the commit; assert `index["a.txt"][:oid] == head.target.tree.path("a.txt")[:oid]` (RED today; mutation: restore the re-read in `refresh_index`); the deletion twin (`File.delete` in the wrapper — today the index loses the entry, after: it keeps the committed oid and the worktree shows an unstaged deletion); optional `Blob.from_workdir` call count == `paths.size`.
- Confidence: high on correctness/behaviour preservation; medium on materiality (narrow race; I/O estimated). Blast radius: one class. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T3; a narrow race needing a concurrent SMB write during one commit; no new types, one local hash). Priority: P3. Independent of F-S13-1.

### F-S16-1 — Sheet autosave saves on five NAMED grid events, so sorts, column renames/resizes, row drags and undo/redo never reach the database: decide "is the grid different from what the server last stored?" by comparison, re-checked after every event
- Authoritative row: S16. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S16.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `sheet_controller.js:77-81` registers exactly `onchange`/`oninsertrow`/`ondeleterow`/`oninsertcolumn`/`ondeletecolumn` → `scheduleSave()`; `:58-76` never sets `columnSorting`, `allowRenameColumn`, `columnResize`, `rowDrag`/`columnDrag`, and the vendored bundle gates each as `0!=options.X` (grep confirms `options.columnSorting` gates and the `config.onevent` dispatcher plus `ignoreEvents=!0` during undo); a save sends the WHOLE grid (`:127-132`: headers+widths and `getData(false,false)`); the status label changes only in `scheduleSave` (`:104`); `sheet_grid_test.rb` exercises cell edits only.
- Current complexity / invalid state: the grid and the row disagree while the label says "Saved" (Ctrl+Z on a saved value reverts after reload; a header double-click sorts on screen but not in the row; a column rename/resize is gone after reload); whether such a change survives is luck (the next unrelated cell edit persists it with the whole grid); the list fails silently for any of the bundle's 42 event names the controller does not list, including future ones; there is no "unsaved" state — `disconnect()` clears the pending timer, so a cell edit followed within 1 s by the Rename form's Turbo submit is dropped and the redrawn grid shows stored data.
- Proposed representation: one catch-all `onevent: () => { this.scheduleSave() }` (BLOCK BODY on purpose — the dispatcher returns the hook's value to events without their own handler, and `onbeforechange` substitutes a non-null result for the typed value); `this.stored = snapshot()` set in `onload`; `save()` compares `snapshot()` to `this.stored` and sends nothing when equal, setting "Saving…" only when a PATCH is actually sent. `this.stored !== this.snapshot()` becomes an explicit unsaved flag usable later for `beforeunload`/save-before-Rename (out of scope).
- Smallest scope: `sheet_controller.js` (~20 lines; drop five handlers, add `onevent`/`snapshot`/`stored`, early return, move the label); `sheet_grid_test.rb`. No server/view/route/data/wire change (same PATCH JSON).
- Risks: the hook MUST return undefined; OWNER DECISION — should a sort/row drag persist (now saved) or be switched off (`columnSorting: false`, `rowDrag`/`columnDrag: false`)? Either removes the disagreement; rename/resize/undo need the value check regardless. Selection/focus events re-arm the debounce (a save waits for a 1 s pause in any interaction; a deny-list of read-only events can tune it and fails safe); the label lags ≤1 s; one `JSON.stringify` of a ≤1 MB grid per expiry; same on every backend (app-database rows).
- Validation: existing formula-persistence test and the controller/integration tests stay green. NEW system tests: (a) undo reaches the row (red today); (b) a header double-click sort reaches the row (red today); (c) selecting cells sends nothing (`update_columns(updated_at: 2.days.ago)`, click three cells, wait past the debounce, assert still > 1 day old — separates the times instead of `.to_i`). Mutations: restore the five handlers → (a),(b) red; delete the early return → (c) red; `onevent: () => true` → `onbeforechange` stores `true` and the formula test reds.
- Confidence: high on the defect (each gesture traced to its dispatch site in the bundle), medium-high on the design. Blast radius: one controller. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2; defect-first — sorts, renames, resizes, drags and undo/redo never saved under a "Saved" label). Priority: P1 for the defect. Owner decision first: persist sorts/drags, or switch those grid features off; otherwise independent.

### F-S16-2 — A stock BOM line's unit is stored twice, and only the web "add line" door keeps the copies in agreement: move the rule into `Maker::BomLine` (+ an `InventoryItem` unit-change hook, S19's file) and backfill
- Authoritative row: S16 (with S19 for the item hook). Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S16.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `projects/bom_lines_controller.rb:37-42` alone applies "A STOCK LINE IS COUNTED IN ITS ITEM'S UNIT" (`attrs[:unit] = item.unit`, "found in review"); `v1/maker/bom_lines_controller.rb:9-34` permits `:unit` and saves it verbatim (an omitted unit takes the column default "each"); the shipped desktop composer sends `unit: $("blUnit").value` for every kind including stock (`../tinkerex-client/client/maker-app/src/projects.js:424,432`); `InventoryItem` has no unit hook (`inventory_item.rb:25` declares only `before_destroy :release_bom_lines`) while the web form permits `:unit`; within ONE row `_bom_lines.html.erb:64` prints `line.unit` in Qty but `:74` and Consume's flash use `item.unit`, and the public table, CSV and API print `line.unit`.
- Current complexity / invalid state: a stock line's unit can disagree with its item's (via the desktop app, any v1 POST/PATCH, or an item unit edit after lines exist) although Consume, the short check and the cost estimate all treat `line.quantity` as being in the ITEM's unit — "2 each" in Qty beside a Consume button that takes 2 kg; the public table/CSV publish the wrong unit; after `release_bom_lines` converts the line to free text the stale unit is permanent.
- Proposed representation: `before_validation :count_in_item_unit` on `Maker::BomLine` (`self.unit = item.unit` when `inventory_item_id` resolves to this user's item; a dangling/foreign id is left to the validations — no §14d lock); `after_update :recount_bom_lines, if: :saved_change_to_unit?` on `InventoryItem` (`update_all` over its lines); one correlated-UPDATE backfill (SQLite + Postgres); delete the door's lookup-and-copy. Alternative weighed and rejected: a reader override returning the item's unit (validation would check the reader, not the column; `release_bom_lines` would have to copy; v1 index needs an `includes`).
- Smallest scope: `bom_line.rb`, a data migration, `inventory_item.rb` (S19's agreement), `projects/bom_lines_controller.rb#create` (delete the copy, keep the blank-item refusal).
- Risks: v1 behaviour changes (a unit sent for a stock line is replaced by the item's and the response shows it — the rule the web door already applies; the desktop app needs no change); the backfill rewrites units on disagreeing rows to the unit Consume already used (no balance/ledger change); an item unit edit now relabels its lines (as the item's own balance already relabels); `release_bom_lines` unaffected; one extra item load per stock-line save (write path only); same on every backend.
- Validation: `project_bom_test.rb:229-234` ("a stock line takes its item's unit, whatever the composer sent") must stay green after the door code is deleted — the mutation "delete the door code without the callback" reds it, proving the rule moved. NEW: model test (`create!` with `unit: "each"` on a kg item stores "kg"); v1 POST answers `"unit":"kg"` (red today); item `update!(unit: "g")` relabels its stock line and leaves a free-text line alone; conversion — v1-create on a kg item, destroy the item, the converted line reads "kg" (red today). Mutations named per test.
- Confidence: high on the disagreement (every path traced incl. the shipped composer), medium-high on the shape. Blast radius: one model + one hook + one migration. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19; credits S18's independent co-sign): KEPT, shape amended. The proposed hook pair keeps the duplicate and adds two hooks plus a data migration to hold it in step; S18's shape — a stock line's unit IS its item's unit (derived), copied into the row only by `release_bom_lines` when the item goes, exactly as `part_name` is (`inventory_item.rb:61-69`) — removes the duplicate's authority and is the audit's recommended default. The callback shape stays the fallback if the owner wants a stored copy (then scope its `update_all` to the user). Both respect §14d's dangling-id rule. Needs S19's agreement (`inventory_item.rb`). Priority: P2; lands after F-S19-1 (both touch `Maker::BomLine`).
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): Coordination — the data migration (if the stored shape is chosen) is S23's; this is the only migration-bearing entry without an S23 consistency verdict — obtain one before cutting the slice. `bom_lines_controller.rb` actions are disjoint from F-S19-1's (`#create` vs `#consume`).

### F-S19-1 — Let `Maker::InventoryTransaction` own what a postable ledger row is (delta finite, non-zero, in-column; project id scoped to the item's owner), instead of one of its four doors
- Authoritative row: S19. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S19.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `inventory_transaction.rb:21` validates `delta` with `numericality: true` only (ActiveModel 8.1 counts "Infinity"/"-Infinity"/"NaN"/"1e400" and 0 as numbers) while `:54-57` keeps `quantity_cached == SUM(delta)` by an SQL increment; the web controller alone holds `MAX_DELTA`/`parse_delta` (finite, non-zero, `abs <= 999999999.999`, `inventory_items_controller.rb:123-138`), re-checks `REASONS` (`:104-107`) and DROPS a foreign project id (`:109-114`), and its comment claims it mirrors v1 "field-for-field"; `v1/maker/inventory_items_controller.rb:33-41` passes `delta`, `project_id` and `note` RAW into `apply!`; BOM consume posts `-line.quantity` validated only `greater_than: 0` (`bom_line.rb:12`), which Infinity passes; commit 499ceddf measured the corruption through this same `apply!` (an Infinity row stored while the balance never moved; the immutable ledger leaves deleting the item as the only repair; NaN → NULL → 500) and fixed only the two WEB doors; `belongs_to :project, optional: true` is unscoped, `#show` preloads it (`:51`) and `show.html.erb:88` renders `link_to(t.project.name, project_path(t.project))` — the model comment's "a foreign … project renders as —" is false for one that still exists.
- Current complexity / invalid state: the definition of a valid post is split three ways (model: reason; web: delta validity + project tenancy + reason again; v1 and BOM consume: nothing), permitting states the model's own comments deny — an immutable Infinity/1e400 row with a permanently disagreeing cached balance (the running-balance walk then prints garbage), a NaN request that 500s, zero-delta no-op rows (v1), and ledger rows pointing at ANOTHER TENANT's project, whose private name and slug the poster's own ledger page then discloses. Two of four doors did not copy the private controller method.
- Proposed representation: `MAX_DELTA` moves to the model; `validates :delta, presence: true, numericality: { other_than: 0, in: -MAX_DELTA..MAX_DELTA }` (`in:` → `Range#include?`, false for ±Infinity/NaN; "1e400" parses to Infinity); `apply!` normalises `project_id` to `Maker::Project.where(id:, user_id: locked.user_id).pick(:id)` (dropped, not refused, at every door — the rule the web door applied alone; a normalisation, so no §14d `if: fk.present?` lock) and `note.presence`. Every door becomes a translator of `RecordInvalid`: web `adjust` rescues into its existing alert; v1 needs NO edit (`V1::BaseController` already maps `RecordInvalid` → 422).
- Smallest scope: `inventory_transaction.rb` (validation, `MAX_DELTA`, scoping in `apply!`, correct the false "—" sentence); `inventory_items_controller.rb` (`adjust` and `post_initial_quantity!` rescue `RecordInvalid`; delete `parse_delta`/`MAX_DELTA`/the REASONS re-check/the project lookup, ~30 lines; fix the stale "field-for-field" comment); S16 follow-up: `Projects::BomLinesController#consume` rescues `RecordInvalid` into its flash; optional one-off census nulling foreign `project_id`s via `update_all` (non-finite rows can only be reported).
- Risks: v1 `adjust` answers 422 instead of 201 for 0/±Infinity/NaN/1e400/out-of-column and stores a foreign `project_id` as nil — the desktop client sends `delta: i64` and never `project_id`, so the only observable change there is a zero delta; `inventory_transaction_test.rb:57` must use a valid delta (today `update!(delta: 0)` would fail validation before `before_update` and red for the wrong reason); three "Enter a non-zero" flash regexes need a custom message or new patterns; one extra PK lookup when `project_id` is present (nets zero for the web door); rows already posted through v1 with a foreign project keep disclosing until the census runs (an optional read-side `t.project.user_id == @item.user_id` guard is belt-and-braces); backend-neutral.
- Validation: existing inventory_test posts/zero/non-numeric/non-finite/foreign-project/bad-reason pins, initial-quantity pins, model tests, behavioral_edges, structural_orphan, v1 inventory tests. NEW: (a) model — each of `%w[Infinity -Infinity NaN 1e400 0]` makes `apply!` raise `RecordInvalid` with ledger count and balance unchanged (mutations: drop `in:` → ±Infinity/NaN/1e400 red; drop `other_than: 0` → "0" red); (b) v1 — the same five strings answer 422 and leave the ledger unchanged — RUN FIRST, it should be RED on today's code (the probe for the gap this audit could only read); (c) v1 adjust with another user's project answers 201, stores nil, and the poster's item page does not contain the foreign name (mutation: drop `user_id: locked.user_id` → both red); (d) S16 — consuming a stock line planted with an Infinity quantity posts no ledger row.
- Confidence: high on the divergence and the ledger corruption; medium-high on the cross-tenant disclosure (follows directly from the unscoped association + preload + view; test (c) is the probe). Blast radius: one model + one controller. Effort: small. Priority: high (data integrity + a tenancy leak reachable through the device API).
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19; credits S18's co-sign): KEPT (T1; security track first). Adopt S18's `on: :create` refinement — validate `delta` on create so the immutability test (`inventory_transaction_test.rb:57`) keeps raising `ReadOnlyRecord` unchanged — instead of rewriting that test. Confirm the cross-tenant disclosure with one request and fix it on its own track first (§4); the model move removes the door-only rule (net removal). Priority: P1.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): Coordination — validation (b)/(c) are v1 request tests in `test/integration/v1/*` (S18). Unchecked by any pass: whether one of the five inputs reaches an `ArgumentError` on the v1 path — if one does, F-S18-1 would turn that 422 into a 500, so run this entry's (b) after F-S18-1 or confirm none does.

### F-S20-1 — Define the icon-only rail ONCE, as a container query on `.rail`, instead of a body-class copy plus an unscoped viewport copy that leaks onto every `.brand` outside the rail
- Authoritative row: S20. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S20.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `shell.css:71-82` (the `.rail--collapsed`-prefixed copy, emitted only inside the shell per `shell_helper.rb:98-101`) and `:160-168` (the `@media (max-width: 1099px)` copy, UNPREFIXED) hold the same rules; the unprefixed copy hides `.brand-name` for every `.brand` on the page, which `shell.css:22-29` (`.public-header .brand-name { display: inline }`, "THE OVERRIDE IS NOT TIDINESS") and `pages/landing.css:17-21` each undo; the auth card renders `.brand`/`.brand-name` (`users/sessions/new.html.erb:2` and five sibling Devise views) and NO rule anywhere names `.auth-card .brand-name` (grep verified); `shell_helper.rb:99-101` records fixing the hidden-wordmark defect for the COOKIE path only; `test/system/shell_test.rb:77-79` says its second browser test exists because "shell.css carries a SECOND copy". Four commits edited both copies together.
- Current complexity / invalid state: one UI state ("the rail is icons only") with two triggers (cookie, viewport), each with its own hand-synced copy scoped differently; every rail edit lands twice and needs two browser tests; the viewport copy styles elements OUTSIDE the rail so every surface reusing `.brand` must remember an override — one forgot: the self-host front door's sign-in card (what `/` shows on every self-hosted box) loses its wordmark below 1100px (inferred from the cascade, not observed in a browser).
- Proposed representation: `.rail { container: rail / inline-size }` and ONE `@container rail (max-width: 120px) { … }` block holding the icon-rail rules (the content-box width is 39px collapsed vs 199px expanded, so any threshold between works); the cookie and the media query keep doing the one thing they already do — set the grid track width. Scoping is built in (a container query matches only the container's descendants), so the two overrides are deleted.
- Smallest scope: `shell.css` (add `container`, one `@container` block, reduce `:71-82` to the grid rule, delete `:162-168` and `:29`, rewrite the `:17-25` comment); `pages/landing.css:17-21` (drop the two restoring declarations); comment-only edits in `shell_helper.rb:99-102` and `shell_test.rb:77-79`; design-system.md rule 8 (the icon rail is one container query; the rail's width must stay the grid track's).
- Risks: `container-type: inline-size` applies inline-size/layout/style containment to `.rail` — its width can no longer come from content (fine while the track is a fixed length; record it), and it becomes the containing block for `position: fixed` descendants (none today); size container queries need Chrome 105 / Safari 16 / Firefox 110, inside `allow_browser :modern` (Chrome 120 / Safari 17.2 / Firefox 121); CSS only, leg-neutral. Visible changes: the auth card keeps its wordmark below 1100px (the intended state); the public header keeps its 8px brand padding at narrow widths.
- Validation: existing `shell_test` (:23, :47, :80, :107, the 375px sweep), `shell_layout_test` (:93, :127, :217), `landing_test`, `phone_topbar_test`, `css_hygiene_test` (its parser records `@container` as an at-rule inside `@layer shell`). NEW (a) "below 1100px the sign-in card and the visitor's header keep their wordmark" — CDP viewport 900/375 signed out; computed `display` of `.auth-card .brand-name` ≠ none, same for `.public-header` and `.landing-nav` (RED today; mutation: restore the unprefixed `.brand-name { display: none }`); (b) "the two triggers draw one rail" — equal computed-style fingerprints for cookie@1440 vs expanded@900 (mutations: drop `.rail-label` from the block → both arms red; delete the collapsed grid rule → cookie arm red).
- Confidence: medium-high (both blocks diffed; the grep shows no auth-card rule; the symptom is inferred). Blast radius: one stylesheet. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2; the sign-in card's lost wordmark is read from the cascade, not observed in a browser — observe it first). Priority: P2; independent.

### F-S20-2 — Help says where files come from through the shared `shared/_where_files_come_from` partial, not a second copy of the capability branch (modest) [DEMOTED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication; not ranked in §6]
- Authoritative row: S20. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S20.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `help/show.html.erb:19-22` asks for exactly this ("Once both have merged, render that partial in place of…") and both halves merged on 2026-10-13; the inline copy remains at `:31-41` and `:45-50`; the partial (`:3-5`) claims to be "ONE definition … so the three cannot drift"; they HAVE drifted: commit 8124afbf touched only the partial (it names the library folder via `library_folder_label`; Help still says "a folder on this server"), and the partial links "Library" page where Help links "Files" (the rail calls it Library). The tests are duplicated too (`help_page_test.rb:83-106` vs `where_files_come_from_test.rb`).
- Current complexity / invalid state: two encodings of one per-deployment fact (what fills the library, how fast, where Rescan lives), gated on the same three capabilities, edited and tested separately; diverged twice within five days.
- Proposed representation: `<%= render "shared/where_files_come_from", projects: false %>` replaces `:31-41` and `:45-50`; the Help-only sentences (rename, versions, Trash) stay inline.
- Smallest scope: `help/show.html.erb`; `help_page_test.rb` (assert `main .file-sources` renders under each capability set, "Rescan library" appears exactly once on a scannable library; drop its copies of the partial's wording). The partial (S06's) is untouched.
- Risks: /help's wording changes (the folder sentence names the `LIBRARY_HOST_PATH` folder as Recent/Library/Projects already do; link text becomes "Library page"); the partial's `<strong>` lead-ins inside `.prose` are cosmetic; the rule that maker sections name no setting still holds; leg-neutral.
- Validation: `where_files_come_from_test` already pins the arms; rewritten `help_page_test`; mutations: re-adding an inline folder paragraph → the count assertion red; deleting the render → `.file-sources` red.
- Confidence: medium (safe, requested by the file itself; payoff is copy drift, not behaviour). Blast radius: one view. Effort: trivial.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): DEMOTED → housekeeping ticket. The copies have drifted (`help/show.html.erb:31-50` vs `shared/_where_files_come_from.html.erb`) and the Help file itself asks for the swap (`:19-22`) — a recorded TODO about user-facing copy, removing no state or decision the app acts on. Not ranked in §6.

### F-X-1 (cross-cutting, FINAL — S21 reported no contrary evidence; owner S01) — Dead port keyword arguments: `begin_upload(device:)` and `finalize_upload(broadcast:)` are read by NO backend, yet two callers create a `devices` row just to supply `device:`, and CLAUDE.md §14a's "finalize_upload broadcasts by DEFAULT, with exactly one explicit opt-out" describes a parameter nothing reads
- Authoritative row: S01 (call sites S21, S11, S20). Status fixed 2026-10-19 by the schema pass: S01's row now lists F-X-1.
- Raised by: S20 (cross-note). Authoritative owner proposed: S01 (the port signature), with S21 (`FilesController#begin_upload`), S11 (`LibraryOutput#addon_device`) and S20 (`ApplicationController#web_device`) as the call sites. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `chunk_remote.rb:321-325` ("`device:` IS ACCEPTED AND UNUSED, DELIBERATELY … the server derives the device from the bearer token") and `:374-378` ("`broadcast:` IS ACCEPTED AND UNUSED … the SERVER broadcasts"); `share.rb:317` and `s3.rb:395-402` raise `NotImplementedError` for both verbs; the only callers are `files_controller.rb:922-925` (passes `device: web_device`) and `library_output.rb:193-194,210` (passes `device: addon_device(user)` and `broadcast: false`); `ApplicationController#web_device` (`:63-67`) and `LibraryOutput#addon_device` (`:216-218`) exist to mint/find a device row for that argument.
- Current complexity / invalid state: a port vocabulary carried over from the retired embedded engine — every upload through the web or an addon save performs a `devices` find-or-create whose result no backend reads, and the recorded §14a broadcast rule governs nothing.
- Proposed representation: drop both keyword arguments from the port and the three adapters, delete `web_device` (and its `helper_method`) and `addon_device`, amend §14a's bullet; optionally remove `web_device` from `architecture_invariants_test.rb:1195`.
- Smallest scope: `file_layer/{chunk_remote,share,s3}.rb` signatures, `files_controller.rb:922-925`, `library_output.rb:193-194,210,216-218`, `application_controller.rb:48,58-67`, CLAUDE.md §14a, the LibraryOutput tests that assert `broadcast: false`. Check `test/services/file_layer/chunk_remote/upload_test.rb` and the adapter contracts for the kwargs.
- Risks: a device row is still minted by `POST /v1/devices/login` for real devices (unaffected); the web session no longer creates a "web-session" device — any reader of that row (none found in app code; verify in the sibling sync server, which never sees this app's `devices` table) must be checked; behaviour-neutral on all three legs.
- Validation: existing upload tests on all three legs; the mutation is restoring the kwarg (nothing goes red — which is the point: a parameter with no reader has no test that can see it, so the guard is the signature itself).
- Confidence: high on the facts. Effort: small. S21's review of the upload doors raised nothing against it; S21 additionally notes no client reads `version_id`/`seq` from `/files/finalize_upload` (`chunk_upload.js:42-43`), weakening `upload_finalized.rb:4-9`'s only argument for keeping them outside `info` — a follow-on for S01, not part of this finding.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2; it corrects a false §14a statement and stops minting `devices` rows — the API's only credential table — for nothing). Scope corrected: `broadcast:` is accepted ONLY by `ChunkRemote#finalize_upload` (`share.rb:322` and `s3.rb:400` are `def finalize_upload(_user, commit_id: nil, verify: true)`), so drop `device:` from all three `begin_upload`s and `broadcast:` from ChunkRemote alone; the scope item "LibraryOutput tests that assert `broadcast: false`" is struck — no such tests exist (grep: prose only). Validation (replaces "nothing goes red"): NEW — a web `begin_upload`/`finalize_upload` on chunk_remote and an addon save leave `Device.count` unchanged; mutation: restore `device: web_device` / `addon_device(user)` → red. Blast radius: the port signature (3 adapters) + 2 callers + `ApplicationController` + CLAUDE.md §14a. Effort: small. Priority: P3; no dependency; lands before F-S11-1 (same `library_output.rb`).
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): Coordination — `share.rb:317` (S02), `s3.rb:395` (S03), `chunk_remote.rb:325,378` (S04) and `architecture_invariants_test.rb:1195` (S26); S01 owns none of the files but owns the port's verb contract (§1 Interfaces), so it coordinates. CLAUDE.md §14a `:489` edit falls under the ceiling rule (§6).

### F-S17-1 — One predicate for "can this deployment keep the demo's promise for this account" (`DemoReset.host_refusal`), asked by the door AND by the reset, retiring `accounts_sharing_library`'s `syncable?` stand-in
- Authoritative row: S17. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S17.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `demo.rb:48-50` (`available?` = account present AND `accounts_sharing_library(except:).zero?`; its comment claims "the button, the links and POST /demo all ask this and nothing else"); `demo_sessions_controller.rb:28-46` re-derives `enabled?`, the account and the co-tenant count with its own sentence; `demo_reset.rb:68-88` (`refusal`) has a PORT arm (`is_a?(Share) || is_a?(S3)`, else refuse) the door never asks; `accounts_sharing_library` (`:93-97`) returns 0 when `port.capabilities.syncable?`, and `FileLayer::ChunkRemote` declares `syncable: true` (`chunk_remote.rb:55-56`); `_demo_banner.html.erb:12` promises "It resets every night at 04:00 UTC"; the demo spec (`2026-10-14-h-public-demo-design.md:64`) expected chunk_remote demos to work ("every account has its own library and the demo is just one of them").
- Current complexity / invalid state: three modules compose the admission rule; they agree on the co-tenant arm (one helper, the sentence written twice) and disagree on the port arm, so on chunk_remote with an account at the demo address the door OPENS (button, link, POST /demo sign visitors in) while `DemoResetJob` refuses every night ("the reset refused and changed nothing") and the banner promises a reset — a contradictory lifecycle state with no end date. "Every account indexes one library" is spelled as `!syncable?`, the capability-coincidence §14b warns about (syncable means a sync server is behind the port, not per-account libraries).
- Proposed representation: `DemoReset.host_refusal(except:, port:)` → nil or a sentence (the file's idiom): the port arm first (share/s3 only), then the co-tenant COUNT; `refusal` = `seed_refusal || host_refusal`; `Demo.available?` asks `host_refusal(except: account).nil?`; POST /demo replaces `:37-46` with one call and logs the sentence. `accounts_sharing_library` and its `syncable?` proxy disappear.
- Smallest scope: `demo_reset.rb` (split `refusal`, delete `accounts_sharing_library`), `demo.rb:42-51` (point + fix the false comment), `demo_sessions_controller.rb:37-46`; optional prose in `demo_reset_job.rb:7-9` and `tinkerex.rake:41`; optional follow-on once F-S01-1 lands: `Demo.validate!` refuses `DEMO_ACCOUNT_EMAIL` at boot on a non-share/s3 backend (the `Registration.validate!` shape).
- Risks: the ONLY behaviour change is on chunk_remote — the button/link disappear and POST /demo becomes a logged 404; this follows the direction the reset and `setup` already took, but the spec expected the opposite, so the OWNER picks: shut the door there, or teach the reset chunk_remote (seed through a LibraryOutput-style upload, no trash purge) — either way the decision lands in `host_refusal`'s one port arm. share/s3 (OVH included) unchanged; existing log-substring assertions survive; no schema/data/API change; leg-neutral (the new test pins ChunkRemote explicitly).
- Validation: existing demo_session (off → 404; no account → 404 + log; co-tenant → no button/404/log; locked demo unlocked), landing, demo_reset (co-tenant refusal; non-share/s3 refused before the first delete), demo_reset_job and tinkerex_tasks pins. NEW (a) "on a backend the reset cannot rebuild, there is no button and POST /demo is a logged 404" with `FileLayer.current = FileLayer::ChunkRemote.new` (mutation: restore the co-tenant-only body → the button renders and the POST signs in → RED); (b) unit `available?` false with ChunkRemote, true with Share and no co-tenant; single-predicate proof: deleting `host_refusal`'s co-tenant arm must red `demo_session_test:133` AND `demo_reset_test:163` together.
- Confidence: medium (the disagreement is verified; three small edits; the direction needs the owner's call; reachable only once an account exists at the demo address on chunk_remote). Blast radius: the demo door. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T3; config-gated — reachable only when an operator sets `DEMO_ACCOUNT_EMAIL` on chunk_remote, which no shipped config does). Scope label corrected: `tinkerex.rake:41` is CODE (the demo_email lambda's raw ENV read), the nearby prose is `:36-38`. Priority: P3; the direction is the owner's; the optional `Demo.validate!` input follows F-S01-1.

### F-S07-1 — The model catalog's "is this folder claimed?" includes discovery's POSITIONAL claim (an uncapped public reader on `ProjectDiscovery`), so its answer stops depending on job order, the 50-folder cap, or pull timing on any backend
- Authoritative row: S07 (with S06 for the public reader in `project_discovery.rb`). Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S07.md, Opportunity 1. Resolves the convergent S01/S06 cross-cutting pattern (see §4/§5). Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `model_intake.rb:145-150` `claimed_folders` = import-model `folder_node_id` ∪ project `folder_node_id` (claims that EXIST), and its comment (`:137-138`) delegates correctness to "Library::ScanJob already runs discovery before this sweep"; `ProjectDiscovery::MAX_PROJECTS = 50` (`:58`) caps discovery even inside ScanJob's own sequence and `adoptable` is `private_class_method` (`:188`); on chunk_remote the three jobs are independent 15-minute entries (verified earlier) with the 2-minute changelog pull firing at the same instants; `model_intake_defaults_test.rb:22,51,81` pins "desk-lamp is a model" (the hole, as seeded behaviour); `attach_all` (`:152-172`) only inserts.
- Current complexity / invalid state: in time a `Projects/<x>` folder has three states — claimed (row exists), PENDING (discovery will adopt it), unclaimed — and only the first and third are represented; "pending" is handled by sequencing, which fails three ways (capped on every backend; absent on chunk_remote; described as correctness-critical in five places and backed by two tests whose mutation claims predate the 2026-09-02 claim rule). The wrong row ("project AND model") lasts until someone runs `rake models:prune APPLY=1`.
- Proposed representation: `ProjectDiscovery.positional_claims(user)` — every folder discovery owns by position, UNCAPPED (direct children of the `Projects/` root passing `adoptable_name?`), made public, with `adoptable` defined through it (one rule); `ModelIntake.claimed_folders` unions it. A positional claim always becomes a real one (archiving keeps the claim; a v1 hard delete is re-adopted), so "pending" means "claimed later" and discovery-before-catalog drops from correctness to latency. S06's suggested fix is the right shape on two conditions: (a) it must be discovery's own reader, never a path test inside ModelIntake (if S06's "contains-claimed" refusal lands, the reader follows it); (b) it prevents NEW wrong rows only — legacy rows need F-S07-2 or Prune. Better than the "one sequenced pipeline" alternative, which cannot close the cap hole and would multiply the catalog's per-tick cost by 7.5 at the pull's 2-minute cadence. Optional: consult the claim set for every folder between a file and its nearest shelf (`owner_key`'s chain), closing the hand-adopted-three-levels-down swallow.
- Smallest scope: `project_discovery.rb` (+`positional_claims`, `adoptable` through it — S06's file), `model_intake.rb:127-150`, tests, prose in `model-intake.md:88-98,205-211`, `project-intake.md:134-146`, `scan_job.rb:239-249,320-333`, `recurring.yml:162-166`, `scan_job_test.rb:378-397,643-660`, `model_intake_test.rb:98-104`.
- Risks: deliberate behaviour change — an unadopted `Projects/<x>` is never a folder model even in the window before adoption (`model_intake_defaults_test.rb:51,80-81` change; the doc already answers "no"); +2 constant queries per sweep; `rake models:prune APPLY=1` would also destroy auto rows over not-yet-adopted `Projects/<x>`; fail-closed if discovery were wedged (no wider than discovery's own window); rows only, no migration, identical on all three legs.
- Validation: existing model_intake claim/archived/stop-accreting/import pins, prune test, `project_adoptions_test:350-371`, DiscoveryOrderTest. NEW: (a) a folder discovery will adopt is not catalogued before adoption (mutation: drop the union → red); (b) a dot-folder under Projects/ is still catalogued (mutation: replace discovery's reader with "parent is the Projects root" → red — pins that discovery's rule decides); (c) a capped discovery (`cap: 1`) leaves no auto model for the rest (mutation: cap the reader → red); (d) the same through `Library::ScanJob` with `PROJECT_DISCOVERY_MAX=1`. Re-point DiscoveryOrderTest's swallow assertion (ordering mutations can no longer fail it, §15) and fix the two stale mutation comments.
- Confidence: high (short code paths read end to end; the cap hole is deterministic; the chunk_remote race is reasoned). Blast radius: two services. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T1; a deterministic cap hole on any first scan with more than 50 project folders, plus the chunk_remote race). Priority: P1. PREREQUISITE for F-S07-2 (narrowed) — and formerly for F-S24-1, now demoted.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): Coordination — `scan_job.rb:239-249,320-333` + `scan_job_test.rb` (S05); `recurring.yml:162-166` (S24); `project-intake.md:134-146`, `model-intake.md:88-98,205-211` (editing owners S06/S07, §1). With F-S24-1 demoted this entry makes the truth fixes to those paragraphs itself.

### F-S07-2 — An auto model's attachment rows are a function of the plan: let the sweep RECONCILE them (add and remove on live files) and retire `ModelIntake::Prune` [REJECTED AS WRITTEN, NARROWED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication]
- Authoritative row: S07. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S07.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — the sweep only inserts (`model_intake.rb:152-172`, one `insert_all … unique_by`); Prune exists only because of that and its sole premise (`prune.rb:3-13`, `model-intake.md:177-192`) is that a deleting sweep "would also delete what a user attached BY HAND through `Models::AttachmentsController#create`"; that door's only caller is `importer_controller.js:122` posting to the `attach_url` `ModelsController#create` returns for a just-created IMPORT (`models_controller.rb:130`), no view references the model-attachment paths, the detach link left in fdaa2660 a month before auto models existed, the v1 door cannot name a Model, and `AttachToProject` writes only Project rows; Prune's guard 3 (`:57-60`) assumes "the sweep can only ever have attached from inside [the folder]", which a MOVE breaks (node_id survives), and Prune skips one-file models entirely (`:28-32`); `AttachToProject#model_owning` (`:226-230`) takes the oldest row, dismissed or stale alike.
- Current complexity / invalid state: four actors own an auto model's rows (sweep inserts; Prune detaches/destroys on demand behind four guards; the reaper destroys rows on dead nodes; the adoption door destroys the model) and none handles "the file is live but the plan no longer gives it to this model" — reachable stale states: a file in two models (moved between model folders), ghost one-file models, auto models over claimed folders that stay listed, and stale rows read as membership (attaching a moved mesh to a project drags in the OLD model's bundle). Steady state re-sends one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` carrying every owned file every tick — a write statement taking SQLite's write lock to write nothing (`ProjectIntake` avoids exactly this with pluck-and-reject).
- Proposed representation: make the premise structural — `Models::AttachmentsController#create` refuses an auto model (422; the importer never targets one), so `maker_models.origin` IS the "which door wrote this row" discriminator `model-intake.md:182` says is missing; the sweep reconciles rows for non-dismissed auto models only: desired = `plan.owners` by key for unclaimed keys; delete rows whose node is LIVE and not desired; KEEP rows on tombstones (the reaper's grace) and on broken-chain files (`classify` must tell `:broken` from `:no_shelf`); insert only missing pairs. Never touch import or dismissed models, never destroy a model; an emptied row hides by the existing rule. Deletes `prune.rb`, the rake task and its test; steady state becomes one SELECT and zero writes.
- Smallest scope: `models/attachments_controller.rb` (guard; optionally delete the UI-less `#destroy` and route), `model_intake.rb` (`classify` returns live + broken sets; `for_user` loads all non-dismissed autos; `attach_all` → `reconcile`; `RunResult#detached`), `model_intake_job.rb` (log), delete `prune.rb` + `models.rake:12-30` + `model_intake_prune_test.rb`, prose (`model-intake.md:177-192,160-162`, `model_folder.rb:18-19`, `adoptions_controller.rb:203-218`).
- Risks: REVERSES a recorded decision (`model-intake.md:177-192`) — needs the OWNER's sign-off; introduces unattended row deletion (rows only, pure functions of the plan, re-derived by the next correct sweep; no model is ever destroyed); the first post-deploy run applies Prune's detach for every user plus the rows guard 3 wrongly protected — MEASURE FIRST with a read-only count of the would-delete set on each live database (the 2026-09-02 change was measured: 2441 rows on 6 folder models); visible: ghost one-file models and claimed-folder autos leave `/files/models`, `AttachToProject` bundles shrink to current membership, a derived cover can move; constant query count, writes only on change, identical on all legs.
- Validation: existing model_intake (no-op re-sweep, dismissed, claimed, imports, rename keeps one model, constancy), classify, models_catalog hide/unhide, model_folder, project_adoptions pins. NEW (a)-(h) in the report, each with its mutation: a moved mesh moves its row (restore insert-only → red); a one-file model whose file moved empties and leaves the list; tombstoned rows survive (compute live without `.present` → red); broken-chain rows survive (fold `:broken` into `:no_shelf` → red); dismissed and import rows untouched; a no-op sweep issues zero INSERT/DELETE on `sql.active_record`; the door 422s for auto / 201s for import; an auto over a claimed folder is emptied not destroyed and refills on hard delete.
- Confidence: medium (mechanics straightforward; the hand-attach premise demonstrably unreachable; but it reverses a written decision and its first run is unmeasured). Blast radius: the model catalog's rows. Effort: medium. Dependency: F-S07-1 first (then ScanJob's post-reconcile order is purely a latency choice).
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): REJECTED AS WRITTEN; NARROWED. As proposed it reverses `model-intake.md:177-192` ("`Maker::ModelIntake::Prune` IS THE REPAIR HALF, AND IT IS NOT IN THE SWEEP ON PURPOSE") and its first run's delete volume is unmeasured; a recorded invariant is reopened by the OWNER with evidence, not reversed by this audit. The evidence stands (the hand-attach premise is unreachable at HEAD: the attach door's only caller is the importer posting to a just-created import, `importer_controller.js:122`, `models_controller.rb:130`) and is filed in §4 as an owner's decision. What remains as the finding, inside the rule: (a) DEFECT — Prune's guard 3 (`prune.rb:57-60`) breaks when a file moves: teach it that a moved node belongs to the plan's model; (b) the steady-state sweep re-sends one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` carrying every owned file every tick (`model_intake.rb:152-172`) — a write transaction on SQLite's single writer lock for nothing — insert only the missing pairs (pluck-and-reject, as `ProjectIntake` does) so a no-op tick issues zero writes (mutation: restore the unconditional insert → the zero-writes test on `sql.active_record` goes red); (c) OWNER'S CALL — make the attach door refuse an auto model so `maker_models.origin` becomes the door discriminator. Confidence: medium. Priority: P3. Dependency: F-S07-1 first.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): Coordination — `projects/adoptions_controller.rb:203-218` prose (S06).

### F-S21-1 — Retire `Projects::UploadsController`'s project-target arm (unrouted-in-practice since redesign slice 4), leaving one upload door per backend for "upload into a project" and a build-log-photo-only controller
- Authoritative row: S21. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S21.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `config/routes.rb:257` still declares `resources :uploads, only: :create, module: :projects` under projects; grep of `app/` finds NO caller of `project_uploads_path` (only `build_log_entry_uploads_path` in `_log_entry.html.erb`); the controller branches on target type in `attach_target`/`project_of`/`upload_parent`/`role_for` (`:111-139`), carries a 12-line create-before-intake ordering invariant that is "about the PROJECT case" (`:69-80`), and says the project-target route "stays, answering exactly as before" after slice 4 moved the hub's Upload to the top bar (`:97-99`); the slice-4 plan keeps the controller "for the build-log entries' own uploads"; five days after it lost its last UI caller, f275f0e3 had to add the demo cap to it.
- Current complexity / invalid state: on share/s3 "upload a file into this project" has two server doors that disagree (folder resolution: `ensure_library_folder!` on POST vs the listing's folder; attach: explicit `create!` before the sweep vs the sweep alone; landing page; refusal wording) and only one is reachable; the unreachable one still carries a two-type target, the ordering invariant, an entry in `project-intake.md:69-75`, ~10 tests duplicating the top bar's coverage, and every new cross-cutting guard.
- Proposed representation: the controller serves one target (the build-log entry): find the entry, `write_batch(files, parent_node_id: nil)` yielding `entry.file_attachments.create!(role: "image")`, redirect; `project_of`/`upload_parent`/`role_for` and the branch disappear; the intake call can go (a file at the library root has no owning project — pinned inert by `project_folder_intake_test.rb:210-219`).
- Smallest scope: delete the nested route (keep the build-log route `:307-310`); collapse the controller; delete the project-target tests already pinned through `files_path` (`project_upload_attach_test.rb:22-35,125-152,157-163`, `project_folder_intake_test.rb:192-205`, `projects_flow_test.rb:595-608`, `demo_guards_test.rb:252-255`) and PORT the batch + redirect_url tests to `build_log_entry_uploads_path`; docs (`project-intake.md:69-75,497-503`, the concern headers, the arc record).
- Risks: UI none (no rendered form/link/Stimulus value targets the route); a hand-made POST gets 404 instead of a write; one unreachable behaviour goes (uploading into a project whose folder is trashed landed at the root and attached — today the hub's top bar shows the chooser instead); port_writable-gated so chunk_remote untouched; the deleted tests pin Share themselves, so all three legs lose the same count.

- Confidence: high that the arm is unreachable and everything it does is pinned through the top bar; medium on intent ("stays, answering exactly as before" reads as slice-4 scope control, not a reason). Blast radius: one controller + one route. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2; an unreachable second door that disagrees with the top-bar door; `port_writable`-gated so chunk_remote is untouched). ### F-S21-2 — `UploadBatch` owns the request's files and EVERY per-file refusal (one filtered list, one refusal table that includes `FileLayer::NotFound`, one stat-then-enqueue) — a mid-batch reserved name or oversize file today aborts the batch with "File not found." or a 500 [NARROWED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication]
- Authoritative row: S21. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S21.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `upload_batch.rb:54-64` rescues only `UnsupportedOperation` and `FetchError`, while both writable adapters' `write` raise `NotFound` for single-file conditions (Share: over `MAX_BYTES` in `read_capped!` `:829-831`, called from `write` at `:256` (`:101` is `#read`'s cap), path escape `:732`, non-leaf `:742`, reserved/OS-clutter names; S3 equivalents), and nothing caps an upload before the port; `FilesController#create` answers "File not found." via `rescue_from` (earlier files written, intake nudge and notice never run, later files never tried) and `Projects::UploadsController` has NO `NotFound` arm (`:30-36`) and ApplicationController no `rescue_from` → 500 after earlier files were written and attached (a single build-log video over 256 MB also 500s); the concern calls `enqueue_preview` it does not define (`:51`) and each includer carries a private copy (`files_controller.rb:1240-1251`, `uploads_controller.rb:141-148`) while `library_output.rb:220-234` is "THE ONE STAT-THEN-ENQUEUE"; `errors` exists only so each caller can copy `raise errors.first if uploads.size == 1 && errors.any?` (`files_controller.rb:828`, `uploads_controller.rb:63`); "which params entries are files" is filtered in three places and single-vs-batch is decided on the raw count.
- Current complexity / invalid state: the batch's idea of "this file was refused" is an exception allowlist that does not match what the port refuses per file, permitting "files 1-2 written, file 3 refused, files 4-5 never tried" answered with "File not found." or a 500 — the property the concern's own header forbids; "how many files" has two answers (`["", real]` takes the batch branch); `refused` (Hash by name) and `errors` (Array) can disagree; a new includer must remember a private `enqueue_preview` or `NoMethodError` abandons the batch after a successful write.
- Proposed representation: `REFUSED_BECAUSE = { UnsupportedOperation => "a file of that name already exists", FetchError => "the library could not be reached", NotFound => "the library cannot store it" }` (wording: product call); `uploaded_files` as the ONE filtered list feeding the demo cap, the loop and the single/batch answer; `write_batch(files, parent_node_id:)` returning `[written, refused]`, re-raising inside the loop when `files.one?` (the single-file rule in one place; `errors` and both copied `raise` lines go); previews through `LibraryOutput.enqueue_preview`; a `NotFound` `rescue_from` on the build-log controller.
- Smallest scope: `upload_batch.rb`; `files_controller.rb#create`/`#refuse_oversized_demo_upload!`/`#finalize_upload` (+ delete `#enqueue_preview`); `projects/uploads_controller.rb#create`/demo cap (+ delete `#enqueue_preview`, + `rescue_from NotFound`); optional cohesion move of `refused_sentence`/`batch_upload_notice` into UploadBatch.
- Risks: share/s3 only (both doors port_writable-gated); on chunk_remote only `finalize_upload`'s enqueue helper changes (same stat/guard/rescue list); a mid-batch `NotFound` now refuses that file and continues (new reason string; no test pins the old sentence for a batch); a vanished parent reports each file with the NotFound reason (single file unchanged); `["", file]` gets the single-file answer; clobber/fetch reasons, single-file JSON shapes and the 413 demo body byte-identical; no schema/wire change.
- Validation: existing `upload_batch_test` (11), `project_upload_attach_test:44-78`, `demo_guards_test:202-262`, the ShareTest single-file sentences, `files_flow_test:60-65,674-698`, `s3_write_test:70-90`. NEW: (1) a reserved name mid-selection (`["a.stl", ".DS_Store", "b.stl"]`) refuses only that file and the rest land — RED today (mutation: drop `NotFound` from the table → red); (2) a build-log upload of `._photo.jpg` answers an alert, never a 500 (mutation: remove the new arm → 500); (3) tighten `upload_batch_test:76-82` to `assert_redirected_to file_path(node)` (mutation: decide on the raw param count → red).
- Confidence: high on the `NotFound` gap (traced through both adapters, the rescue list and the missing arm), medium on shape. Blast radius: one concern + two controllers. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): NARROWED; citation corrected (above). Share's write-side refusals that reach the batch as `NotFound` are `share.rb:742` (not a leaf name), `:788` (reserved path), `:821` (parent without `storage_path`) and the size cap in `read_capped!` `:829-831`. Smallest fix that removes the invalid state: (1) refuse `NotFound` per file inside the loop (`upload_batch.rb:49-65`); (2) a `NotFound` `rescue_from` on the build-log door (`projects/uploads_controller.rb:30-36`, which answers 500 today); (3) one stat-then-enqueue — the concern calls an `enqueue_preview` each includer defines as a near-duplicate (`files_controller.rb:1244`, `projects/uploads_controller.rb:142`; `project_folder_intake.rb:16` already says so): route both through `Maker::Addons::LibraryOutput.enqueue_preview` (`text_save.rb:42` calls it "the ONE stat-then-enqueue"). The `REFUSED_BECAUSE` map and the `uploaded_files` single/batch rework are optional tidy-up. Priority: P2 (pure defect). Lands after F-S21-1.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): both this and F-S21-1 rewrite `Projects::UploadsController#create`, its demo cap, its `rescue_from` block and `#enqueue_preview`; after F-S21-1 the `NotFound` arm serves the build-log door alone. `LibraryOutput.enqueue_preview` is already public via `module_function` (`library_output.rb:27`), so step (3) needs no change in S11's file.

### F-S18-1 — Retire `rescue_from ArgumentError` from `V1::BaseController`: an engine-era entry whose every intended producer left with the sync engine, which now maps only programming errors and misconfiguration to 422 responses carrying the interpreter's message
- Authoritative row: S18. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S18.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `v1/base_controller.rb:37` `rescue_from ArgumentError, with: :unprocessable`, whose handler (`:44`) renders `e.message` to the client; history: 50e11933 added it for three sync-engine raisers (malformed manifest hash, move cycle, foreign conflict version), 161d3b20 moved the rescues into the engine's base, 9658a573 (Arc A) copied them back without the rationale, and Arc D Part 2 removed every endpoint the rationale named; there is no `enum` and no `raise ArgumentError` in `app/models` or `Maker::Previews` (grep verified); the one explicit producer left is `FileLayer.build`'s "unknown LIBRARY_BACKEND" ArgumentError, reachable through `DevicesController#library_info` (`FileLayer.current`) — so a misconfigured deployment answers POST /v1/devices/login with 422 `{"error":"unknown LIBRARY_BACKEND …"}` while a missing `SYNC_PUBLIC_URL` on chunk_remote answers 500 (two misconfigurations, two answers); Ruby's own arity/keyword errors come back as 422s; the house rule (`move_node.rb:58-64`: "lets bare ArgumentError 500, which is what a programming error should do"; `attach_to_project.rb:381-387`; `architecture_invariants_test.rb:957`) says this must not be rescued; the only other ArgumentError rescue in `app/controllers` is a local one around a BigDecimal parse.
- Current complexity / invalid state: the five-entry map has four entries with live producers (RecordNotFound, ParameterMissing, RecordInvalid, RecordNotUnique) and one whose producers are gone; its only effect is that a bug or misconfiguration in any of the 15 controllers reads as "your request was invalid", sends internals to the client and never reaches error tracking as a 500 — a programming error gets two answers across the app (500 web, 422 v1).
- Proposed representation: delete the line; a future v1 action with a genuine client-input ArgumentError gets a named subclass and its own `rescue_from` (the `MoveNode::InvalidName` precedent).
- Smallest scope: one line in `v1/base_controller.rb` + one test; after F-S01-1 the `FileLayer.build` producer moves to boot as well. Optional static pin (S26) that no controller declares a bare `rescue_from ArgumentError`, with a negative case for a named subclass so a broader regex is not vacuous.
- Risks: requests that hit an ArgumentError today get 500 instead of 422 — the intent; the Rust client maps any non-2xx maker call to `Error::Server` and `with_retry` wraps only chunk PUT/GET, so no maker call starts retrying; Rails already converts input-caused ArgumentErrors upstream (bad encoding → 400; datetime/decimal casts rescue); no backend-leg effect.
- Validation: every existing v1 422 test (all RecordInvalid-based) stays green on all three legs. NEW: make `FileLayer.current` raise ArgumentError (stub adapter, or `LIBRARY_BACKEND=bogus` + `reset!`), log in, `assert_raises(ArgumentError)` under the test env's `show_exceptions = :rescuable` (red when line 37 is restored).
- Confidence: high on the facts (history and producers read directly); medium-high that no hidden producer exists. Blast radius: one line. Effort: trivial.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T3; one vestigial line that gives one error class a second answer and sends `e.message` to the client). Existing test (named): `test/integration/v1/maker/inventory_items_test.rb` (every v1 422 test is `RecordInvalid`-based). Priority: P3; after F-S01-1 the `FileLayer.build` producer moves to boot; independent otherwise. (No edge from F-S19-1: `InventoryTransaction.apply!` raises no `ArgumentError` — coordinator-verified.)

### F-S22-1 — The 3D viewer's lifecycle on the Gerber viewer's model: ONE engine record, ONE staleness token, rest on connect, and one import per engine (the rule the vendored engine itself requires)
- Authoritative row: S22. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S22.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `modelviewer_controller.js:35-40` `connect()` sets `disposed = false`, RESETS `generation = 0` and never rests the element (vs `gerber_controller.js:83` `this.rest()` with its Turbo-snapshot rationale); `:117-118` routes the engine's `onModelLoaded`/`onModelLoadFailed` through controller fields (`this.modelLoaded?.()`) that `:93-97` reassigns per load and `teardown()` (`:56-65`) nulls; `teardown()` calls `viewer.Destroy()`, which in the vendored engine is `Destroy(){this.RevokeObjectUrls(),this.importer=null}` — it does NOT stop a running import; the engine's `ThreeModelLoader.LoadModel` is guarded by `this.inProgress||(…)` (a second request while one runs is silently dropped) and `EmbeddedViewer.LoadModelFromInputFiles` nulls/removes `progressDiv` before calling it, after which the running import's `onImportStart: () => { this.progressDiv.innerHTML = … }` dereferences null (all three patterns grep-verified in `vendor/javascript/o3dv.js`); the controller's `:51-55` comment assumes the opposite ("`LoadModelFromUrlList` clears the viewer itself"); two tokens answer one question (`disposed` and `generation`); no test under `test/` calls `go_back` (grep verified), so Gerber's rest-on-connect is pinned by nothing either.
- Current complexity / invalid state (each follows from the cited lines; (b)-(d) read from the engine, not driven): (a) Back to a file page whose 3D viewer was open restores a `--ready` snapshot with old dims over a blank cloned canvas onto a controller with no engine — launch hidden, Reset a no-op, Fullscreen a blank box; on the model page a switch then boots a second engine whose canvas is appended below the first; (b) switching the model page's source while an import runs (including arrowing through the switcher, which the code says it handles) is silently dropped after the running import's progress element was thrown away, that import's next callback throws, `inProgress` never resets and the engine ignores every later request until ✕ Image; (c) ✕ Image mid-fetch, pick B, View in 3D — A's late failure reaches `this.modelFailed`, now B's, and tears down B's engine as "failed"; (d) leaving during an import logs an uncaught NotFoundError (cosmetic).
- Proposed representation: `initialize() { this.generation = 0 }` (never re-issued); `this.engine` = one record `{viewer, observer, importing, settle}` whose constructor callbacks close over THAT record; `load()` awaits `engine.importing` before asking for another import, then checks the generation; `release()` (today's teardown, on the record, rejecting a queued stale load); `connect()` → `rest()`; `disconnect`/`showPoster` bump the generation. Deletes `disposed`, the per-load callback reassignment, and the separate `viewer`/`resizeObserver` fields. Unchanged on purpose: one engine per open (the window-resize-listener reason), `--ready` before construction, colours/unit/dims/up-axis/named URL.
- Smallest scope: `modelviewer_controller.js` (~30 lines net; no new values/targets/actions, no markup/CSS/route change); tests in `o3dv_test.rb` + `gerber_test.rb` + a shared Turbo-Back helper in `test/system/support/web_helpers.rb`; docs `files-preview.md:541-545` and the controller header/`:51-55` comment. Optional: a per-engine mount `<div>` for (d) (never retarget `.model-viewer-stage > div`, one of the 52 addon-shared classes); drop the always-true `lazy` and the log-only `format` values.
