- Risks: client-only, identical markup and run counts on all three legs; Back shows the poster (Gerber's behaviour and what files-preview.md:277 promises for the board); a switch during an import briefly shows the superseded model before the latest replaces it; a switch during a hung import waits for the XHR error (✕ Image still escapes); `o3dv_test.rb:147`'s probe reads `c.viewer` — keep a getter.
- Validation: existing o3dv (12 cases), files_flow, models_flow, public_file_view, lazy_bundles. NEW: (1) Back rests at the poster (snapshot probe FIRST, then no `--ready`/state/canvas, then View in 3D → one canvas + right dims; mutations: drop `rest()` → red on `--ready`; rest without clearing → red on canvas count); (2) a switch while importing shows the latest choice (delay the first `/blob/` XHR — the engine uses XMLHttpRequest, not fetch — then PLY then GLB → GLB's dims, one canvas, no console errors; mutation: drop the `await engine.importing` → red with the SEVERE TypeError); (3) a released engine's late failure does not fail the next load (mutation: route callbacks through a controller field → red on `failed`); (4) the Gerber Back twin pinning `gerber_controller.js:83`. Run (1)-(3) against today's code first: they turn (a)-(c) from read to measured.
- Confidence: high on (a) (code, Turbo's source and Gerber's measured precedent af536baa agree) and on the mechanism of (b)/(c) (read from the engine); medium on frequency. Blast radius: one controller. Effort: small. Sibling of F-S09-1 (share the Turbo-Back test helper).
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2; defect-first — Turbo Back restores a dead 3D viewer; switching models during an import wedges the engine). Count corrected: `o3dv_test.rb` has 12 cases. Cases (b)-(d) are read from the vendored engine, not driven — run the new tests against today's code first. Sibling of F-S09-1 (shared Turbo-Back helper). Priority: P2.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): Coordination — `test/system/support/web_helpers.rb` (S27) for the shared Turbo-Back helper.

### F-S23-1 — Make `SchemaMap::EDGES` complete by CHECK, not by memory: classify every `*_id` column of the primary schema as drawn (EDGES) or deliberately-not-a-join (`NOT_EDGES`, with its reason), so a reference nobody draws can no longer slip past both guards that derive from EDGES
- Authoritative row: S23. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S23.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `lib/schema_map.rb:104` `EDGES` is hand-authored and `:25-32` states the hole ("a NEW app-level reference that nobody adds to EDGES") justified by "no naming convention to key on" — a premise that died with the engine tables (the cited `manifest_id`/`chunk_hash` lived in them); every column holding a node id is named `node_id` or `*_node_id` (grep: 10 + 11, verified) and every other reference ends in `_id`; `StructuralOrphanTest` derives its case list from EDGES, so an undrawn pointer is never considered; `#problems` checks only that AUTHORED edges name real columns. The reviewer's hand classification of the 53 reference-shaped columns: 48 drawn, 4 deliberate non-joins documented only as prose, 1 live omission (`maker_releases.repo_node_id`, written by `Maker::ReleaseSnapshot`, no arrow on the map).
- Current complexity / invalid state: add a new `*_id` column today and TenantCleanupTest (table-level), StructuralOrphanTest (inherits EDGES) and `problems` are all silent; the stale-page test only forces regeneration; no `dependent:` decision is forced — re-opening §14d's dangling-id lock, the class Supplier, Location, Release and Project→transactions each paid a recorded fix for. The "not a join" knowledge lives as prose in five places plus a dedicated test whose own comment ("`versions.version_id` is a real column") stopped being true when `versions` was dropped. F-S02-1's new column would pass every guard undrawn today.
- Proposed representation: `NOT_EDGES = { "nodes.current_version_id" => …, "remote_manifest_chunks.version_id" => …, "maker_previews.version_id" => …, "maker_file_attachments.version_id" => … }`; a rule in `#problems` that every primary-schema `*_id` column (except `user_id` and edge TARGET keys) is drawn XOR listed, plus "NOT_EDGES names X, which does not exist"; the missing `maker_releases.repo_node_id → nodes.node_id` arrow; the caveat shrinks to "a reference whose column name does not end in `_id` — none exists; name new ones that way". `lib/tasks/schema_map.rake` already aborts regeneration while `problems` is non-empty, so the rule stops a developer at exactly the step the stale-page test sends them to.
- Smallest scope: `lib/schema_map.rb` (NOT_EDGES, the rule as a unit-testable helper, one EDGES line, rewrite `:25-32`, refresh stale prose at `:16,:46-48,:269`); `test/schema_map_test.rb` (one rule test on synthetic input; retire or keep `:87-100`); regenerate `docs/schema-map.html`; optionally point CLAUDE.md §5 at NOT_EDGES.
- Risks: no data/API/UI/leg effect (the tool reads the dumps as text; +1 test per leg); the only new failure mode is a false positive on a non-reference `*_id` column (none exists; the remedy is the point).
- Validation: `SchemaMapTest` and `StructuralOrphanTest` stay green. NEW: (a) the rule on a synthetic `maker_widgets.project_id` returns one sentence (mutation: delete the rule → red); (b) non-empty first (§14e): the classified set is ≥ 40 so an inverted `db` filter cannot pass vacuously (mutation: flip the filter → red); on the real dumps: delete the `repo_node_id` line → red; delete one NOT_EDGES entry → red; re-add `maker_previews.version_id → versions.version_id` → red twice.
- Confidence: high on the gap and mechanics; medium on payoff (preventive for the structural case that costs data, plus one documentary omission). Blast radius: the map tool. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T3; guard gap — `StructuralOrphanTest` derives its cases from `EDGES`, so an undrawn pointer is never checked; `maker_releases.repo_node_id` is undrawn today). Priority: P2 as a GUARD to land FIRST: F-S02-1 (`trash_root_node_id`), F-S11-1 and F-S16-2 each add a column the map must classify.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): when the demoted F-S23-2 ticket's column half is ever taken, this entry's drawn-XOR-listed rule retires `schema_map_test.rb:87-100` and the `maker_file_attachments.version_id` NOT_EDGES entry is deleted rather than a test narrowed; both edit CLAUDE.md §5 (`:149`) under the ceiling (§6).

### F-S23-2 — Retire schema state nothing maintains: `nodes.updated_seq` + its index, `maker_file_attachments.version_id`, and the readerless `maker_previews (user_id, status)` index (in the repo's own two-release retirement shape) [DEMOTED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication; not ranked in §6]
- Authoritative row: S23. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S23.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `updated_seq` has ZERO readers or writers in `app/`/`lib/`/`config/` (grep: two comments only, one of them wrong), `ChangelogSync#upsert` does not write it, so chunk_remote does not use it either (contrary to this row's own premise), every row holds 0; `maker_file_attachments.version_id` is written by NO web door (AttachToProject, ProjectIntake, ModelIntake, the three web controllers write node_id/role only) and only by the v1 permit list (`v1/maker/file_attachments_controller.rb:33`), which the shipped desktop client never sends; its only readers are three JSON echoes; yet CLAUDE.md §5, `file_info.rb:4-8`, `schema_map.rb:122-139` and `schema_map_test.rb:87-100` defend how to read it; `index_maker_previews_on_user_id_and_status` has no status-filtered query in `app/`/`lib/` (grep) and `Maker::Preview.ready` has no caller; each render rewrites the entry 2-3 times.
- Current complexity / invalid state: two columns assert facts nobody keeps ("this node's last changelog position is 0" on every row; "this attachment is pinned to version X" where no read path honours a pin); ~25 lines of prose plus one test defend the interpretation of a column nothing writes; two indexes with no reader cost one extra B-tree insert per `nodes` row and 2-3 rewrites per preview render.
- Proposed representation: release 1 — drop both indexes (plain DROP INDEX on both adapters; pass columns so it rolls back) and `ignored_columns` for the two columns; drop `:version_id` from the v1 permit list and the three serializers (emit nil for one release, or drop the key — the client's field is `#[serde(default)] Option`); delete the unused `ready` scope; release 2 — `remove_column` both. Afterwards each stored content token has one column per meaning and NOT_EDGES shrinks to three.
- Smallest scope: migration A, two `ignored_columns`, three v1 files, the scope, comments (CLAUDE.md §5, `file_info.rb`, `schema_map.rb`, `project_intake.rb:566-568`), regenerate schema + map, narrow `schema_map_test.rb:87-100`; migration B next release. The index half can be taken alone.
- Risks: SQLite `remove_column` rebuilds `nodes` once (the cost tinkerex#152 weighed against FKs, paid once and deferrable; Postgres metadata-only); an API caller posting `version_id` has it ignored instead of echoed (`script/maker_smoke.py:161-164` posts one and asserts only the 201); PRODUCT QUESTION FIRST: if version-pinned attachments are planned, keep the column and give it a reader instead; F-S08-1/2 add no per-user status query (recheck at implementation); EXPLAIN-spot-check plans that land on the `updated_seq` index for a bare `user_id = ?`.
- Validation: v1 file_attachments/projects/items tests, preview/Enqueue tests, page_query_budget, the prefix-index guard, SchemaMapTest. NEW: v1 POST with `version_id: "x"` answers 201 and neither row nor JSON carries it (mutation: re-permit → red); extend `legacy_columns_dropped_test.rb` with the two columns and two index names absent (mutation: revert → red).
- Confidence: high on the facts; medium-low on payoff (representational honesty + write-amplification hygiene, no live defect). Blast radius: two tables + v1 JSON. Effort: small. Priority: low.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): DEMOTED → hygiene ticket for the index/scope half; the column half waits on a product answer. The state is dead (`nodes.updated_seq` has no reader or writer in app/lib/config; `Maker::Preview.ready` and the `(user_id, status)` index have no reader) — take the two DROP INDEX statements and the dead scope as hygiene. Dropping `maker_file_attachments.version_id` changes the v1 wire (`v1/maker/file_attachments_controller.rb:33,37`) and rewrites CLAUDE.md §5 (`:149-151`): "small ×2 releases", owner's call. Not ranked in §6.

### F-S24-1 — State the post-catalog sequence (discover → file → catalogue) ONCE for every backend: a `Maker::LibrarySweepJob` with an ordered `STEPS` list, run inline by `ScanJob` on share/s3 and by ONE recurring entry on chunk_remote, replacing three schedule entries, four inverse gates and `#chain_intake` [DEMOTED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication; not ranked in §6]
- Authoritative row: S24 (with S05/S06/S07 for the step jobs). Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S24.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `scan_job.rb:96-98` runs the three steps inline with ~110 lines of per-step wrappers; `recurring.yml:93-95,158-160,192-194` are three independent `every 15 minutes` entries (fugit turns that into `*/15`, so all three fire at the same instants on the one 3-thread worker, `queue.yml`); three copies of `return if FileLayer.current.capabilities.scannable?` plus a fourth guarding `ProjectDiscoveryJob#chain_intake` (`:80-87`), which chains discovery→intake only and only when discovery created something; discovery→catalogue has no chunk_remote counterpart; solid_queue 1.7.0 supports `limits_concurrency(group:)` (verified in the gem). The history (2ce07c95, 8cb9ffbb) shows the split was copied "matching its siblings", not argued.
- RELATION TO F-S07-1 (coordinator adjudication): F-S07-1 removes the model catalog's CORRECTNESS dependency on order (positional claims) and is the fix for the cap hole, which a sequence cannot close; F-S24-1 is complementary — it removes the DUPLICATED SCHEDULING REPRESENTATION (five places, four gates, a chain) and gives chunk_remote the sequence for the steps whose order still matters (project intake's own ordering per `project-intake.md`, and latency: a discovered project is filed in the same run instead of one enqueue later). Land F-S07-1 first; then F-S24-1's STEPS list is where F-S07-1's reader runs in order anyway.
- Current complexity / invalid state: "what runs after the catalog changes, in what order, on which backend" is written in five places; ~110 lines of recurring.yml comment exist mainly to explain why each entry declines on share/s3 and how the three relate; deleting the gate in `#chain_intake` re-arms §14f's terminal-404 race; readers need three files plus ScanJob plus the scan door to answer "what happens after a sync".
- Proposed representation: `Maker::LibrarySweepJob` with `STEPS = [ProjectDiscoveryJob, ProjectIntakeJob, ModelIntakeJob]` ("THE ORDER IS THE RULE"), per-step rescue (a later step still runs; a `Failed` raise at the end names the failed steps), `limits_concurrency` sharing a `group: "maker_library_sweep"` with `ProjectIntakeJob` (whose only remaining `perform_later` is ChangelogSync's nudge) so a nudge and a sweep never file one user's folders at once, and ONE inverse gate in `sweep_all!`; `ScanJob#perform` calls `perform_now` inline after the reconcile (§14f's "same job, after the reconcile" kept); `recurring.yml` gets one `library_sweep` entry; `Projects::ScansController`'s chunk_remote branch enqueues the sweep when its eager discovery created something (replacing the reliance on `#chain_intake`). Optional (S04/S06 to decide): enqueue the sweep from `ChangelogSync` whenever a pull applied anything, bringing filing+cataloguing to ~2.5 minutes on chunk_remote.
- Smallest scope (in order): new `app/jobs/maker/library_sweep_job.rb`; `project_intake_job.rb` (+`group:`, −`sweep_all!`); `project_discovery_job.rb` (−`sweep_all!`, −`chain_intake`, its `limits_concurrency` then has no `perform_later` caller — the partial unique index is the durable guard); `model_intake_job.rb` (−`sweep_all!`); `scan_job.rb:96-98,239-349` → one `sweep(user)` + one narrow rescue; `scans_controller.rb` chunk_remote branch; `recurring.yml:75-194` → one entry; docs (`project-intake.md:141-146,217-231`, `model-intake.md:91-93,205-211`).
- Risks: no data/schema change (Solid Queue deletes static recurring tasks that leave the file); share/s3 behaviour unchanged (inline, same order, same semaphore; one rescue replaces three); chunk_remote intended changes: the three steps run in order on one thread (per-user latency = the sum of three CAPPED steps), a discovered project is filed in the same run, a step failure yields one failed job naming the steps; CONCURRENCY CORNER: a device-push nudge arriving during a sweep is discarded (today the same discard happens against the scheduled intake; the window is now the whole sweep) — the shared `group:` is REQUIRED, not optional (without it the inline intake and a nudged intake could double-file); not closed: discovery's cap (F-S07-1). Gate tests move rather than multiply; leg counts stay identical.
- Validation: existing DiscoveryOrderTest, IntakeOrderTest, the three failure-isolation tests (log regexes may need the per-step message), discovery perform/cap tests, scan-button chunk_remote tests, recurring_schedule, boot_scan. NEW: (a) behavioural order through the chunk_remote vehicle (port DiscoveryOrderTest's model-role case to `LibrarySweepJob.perform_now`; mutation: move ModelIntakeJob first in STEPS → red); (b) `concurrency_limits_test`: `ProjectIntakeJob.new(user_id: a).concurrency_key == LibrarySweepJob.new(user_id: a).concurrency_key` and differs for user b (mutation: drop `group:` → red — the only semaphore property assertable without a live worker); (c) failure isolation inside the sweep; (d) partition: for Share/S3/ChunkRemote exactly one of `ScanJob.scan_all!` and `LibrarySweepJob.sweep_all!` enqueues (mutation: invert the gate → red); (e) no recurring entry names a STEPS job directly (mutation: re-add `project_folder_intake` → red); (f) the scan door enqueues the sweep iff discovery created something.
- Confidence: medium-high on the split representation; medium on how often the wrong-model race fires (reasoned); medium on the semaphore detail (only a live worker proves the shared semaphore blocks). Blast radius: four jobs + ScanJob + the schedule. Effort: medium. Dependency: after F-S07-1.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): DEMOTED → §4 latency note; §5's "both land" is withdrawn. Its correctness motive is exactly what F-S07-1 removes (after positional claims, a file under `Projects/<x>` keys to a claimed folder before intake moves it — `owner_key`, `model_intake.rb:255-269`, `Projects` a shelf — so discover → file → catalogue order on chunk_remote is latency only; `chain_intake` already runs intake after a productive discovery, `project_discovery_job.rb:80-87`). What remains — three recurring entries and four one-line gates consolidated into `LibrarySweepJob` — must add a concurrency `group:` shared between `ProjectIntakeJob` and the new job because an inline intake bypasses `ProjectIntakeJob`'s own semaphore (the entry's own "CONCURRENCY CORNER", rated "only a live worker proves"): ~110 lines of per-step wrappers move rather than disappear and the coupling gets subtler. Revisit only if chunk_remote filing latency is reported. Not ranked in §6.

### F-S24-2 — Retire the Solid Cable database: a fourth store that is provisioned, migrated on every boot, backed up nightly and documented, with no writer, no reader and no endpoint [DEMOTED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication; not ranked in §6]
- Authoritative row: S24. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S24.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — wired in `Gemfile:50`, `config/cable.yml` (production `adapter: solid_cable`, `connects_to: writing: cable`), both production branches of `config/database.yml` (`:116-119`, `:132-135`), `db/cable_schema.rb`, `lib/schema_map.rb`, `ops/backup-db.sh:229`, `docs/INSTALL.md:268-269,383` ("the derived databases must exist"); nothing reads or writes it: `config/application.rb` sets `mount_path = nil` ("NO CABLE ENDPOINT"), `test/cable_absent_test.rb` pins "THIS APP IS NOT A BROADCASTER", CLAUDE.md §4 acknowledges the mismatch, and no `broadcast_*`/`turbo_stream_from`/`ActionCable.server`/`createConsumer` exists in app/lib/config/JS.
- Current complexity / invalid state: every production `db:prepare` creates and migrates a database the app never touches (a file in the SQLite volume; on managed Postgres a `<db>_cable` database the operator must provision or grant CREATEDB for); the nightly backup snapshots it; the schema map, INSTALL.md and §4 each describe it. Persistent state with no owner.
- Proposed representation: production `adapter: async` (the in-process adapter development already uses; ActionCable stays loaded by `rails/all`, unused, `mount_path = nil` unchanged); delete the `cable:` role from both production branches, `db/cable_schema.rb`, `gem "solid_cable"`; `lib/schema_map.rb` SOURCES/LAYOUT (S23) + regenerate; INSTALL.md and CLAUDE.md §4; optional `ops/backup-db.sh:229` (its `[ -f … ] || continue` already tolerates absence). §14e becomes true by construction instead of by caveat; re-adding live push later is the deliberate act `cable_absent_test.rb:18-20` asks for.
- Risks: existing deployments keep a stale `production_cable.sqlite3`/`_cable` database (harmless; delete by hand; the backup loop copies it until `:229` is edited); turbo-rails' `Turbo::StreamsChannel` still eager-loads and nothing broadcasts; no route/API/UI/data change; the test env uses `adapter: test`, so all three legs are unaffected.
- Validation: `cable_absent_test` unchanged; `schema_map_test` red until regenerated (intended). NEW: assert the production cable adapter resolves to `async` and `database_configuration["production"]` has no `cable` key, read from the parsed config (mutation: restore either → red). Manual: `db:prepare` in a production-env container creates three databases on SQLite and on a Postgres URL.
- Confidence: medium (facts high; payoff is operational surface — provisioning, migrations, backups, docs — not code structure; housekeeping-grade). Blast radius: deployment config. Effort: small. Priority: low.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): DEMOTED → operational housekeeping ticket (the entry's own "housekeeping-grade"). Smallest scope, recorded for the ticket: `config/cable.yml` (production → `adapter: async`); `config/database.yml:116-119,132-135`; delete `db/cable_schema.rb`; `Gemfile:50`; `lib/schema_map.rb` SOURCES/LAYOUT + regenerate; `INSTALL.md:268-269,383`; CLAUDE.md §4; optional `ops/backup-db.sh:229` (after F-S25-2, same file). Not ranked in §6.

### F-S25-1 — ONE secret-site walk and ONE destination list (`lib/tinkerex/deploy_secrets.rb`), shared by `bin/secrets-preflight` and `test/deploy_hygiene_test.rb` — today the copy that actually gates a deploy is the one with no test
- Authoritative row: S25. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S25.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `bin/secrets-preflight:70-120` and `test/deploy_hygiene_test.rb:639-688` are IDENTICAL once comments and indentation are stripped (coordinator re-ran the diff: empty); both say "widen both together" by hand; commit e2fafea1 ("secrets-preflight checked 2 of the 9 places kamal reads a secret") records both copies holding the same wrong 2-of-9 list; NO test runs the preflight (grep of `test/` finds only comments and an assertion message); the mutation-checked oracle (`:690-731`) exercises the test's own copy; the destination list exists three times (preflight `DESTINATIONS`, test `SECRETS_FILES`, the explicit pin at `:879`); the stated reason for two copies ("the test cannot execute a script whose job is to hit the vault") does not apply to the walk, which reads config only; the repo's own pattern for this is `bin/importmap-audit` → `ImportmapAudit.verdict` and `Tinkerex::Sidecars` ("THREE READERS, ONE ANSWER").
- Current complexity / invalid state: delete the accessory `env.secret` arm from the preflight and `bin/ci` stays GREEN while the preflight prints "Safe to deploy." with `SECRET_KEY_BASE` unchecked on every destination and `SYNC_ADMIN_TOKEN` unchecked on three of four — the silent-empty deploy §14g exists to prevent (`preflight:27-31` records exactly this as measured). Adding or folding a destination means editing three lists in two files (folding `remote` broke the test's list).
- Proposed representation: a checkout-only `Tinkerex::DeploySecrets` module holding `DESTINATIONS`, `at`, `names(raw)` (the nine arms moved verbatim with their kamal file:line comments) and `required(destination, config_file:)` (lazily `require "kamal"`); the preflight `require_relative`s it; the test's synthetic oracle calls `names`; optionally `SECRETS_FILES` becomes `Kamal::Secrets#secrets_files` (public, vault-free) so kamal's file selection is reflected rather than re-implemented. Variant: derive DESTINATIONS from the `config/deploy.*.yml` overlays present (needs the dead `deploy.r2.example.yml` deleted or excluded).
- Smallest scope: new `lib/tinkerex/deploy_secrets.rb`; `bin/secrets-preflight` (−`:44-46`, −`:55-120`, header rewrite); `test/deploy_hygiene_test.rb` (−`:623-632`, −`:653-688`, point `:690-731` at the module, keep the pin at `:879`). No doc-rule change (the guard stays static and vault-free).
- Risks: Zeitwerk — the file must define exactly `Tinkerex::DeploySecrets` (caught by CI's eager load); keep kamal OUT of the web process (no top-level require; kamal is `require: false`); the preflight runs without Rails, so a wrong `require_relative` breaks it (the smoke test catches that); no behaviour/output change; no leg/product impact (+1 run on each leg).
- Validation: existing `:690`, `:733`, `:747`, `:871/:879` pins. NEW smoke test: `bin/secrets-preflight nonesuch` via Open3 exits non-zero and its "Known:" line equals the module's labels (vault-free — the script aborts on an unknown destination before building `Kamal::Secrets`; mutation: give the preflight back a local DESTINATIONS without "ovh" → red). Main-claim mutation: delete the accessory arm from the MODULE → `:690` and `:747` red; make the same deletion in today's preflight → `bin/ci` stays green — that difference is the evidence. Optional reverse guard: every key a `.kamal/secrets.<dest>` defines is required by that destination (would have caught the dead `CHUNK_*` tombstone lines).
- Confidence: high (identity measured; drift already happened; the house pattern exists twice; the static property is untouched). Blast radius: one script + one test. Effort: small. Priority: high (it is the gate that prevents a silent-empty deploy).
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T1; the deploy-gating copy of the walk has no test, so losing an arm ships an empty secret silently — the failure deploy-secrets.md:10 exists to prevent). Keep `kamal` lazily required (Zeitwerk eager-loads lib/tinkerex in production). Priority: P1; independent. Lands before F-S12-2 (same test file).

### F-S25-2 — ONE backup-destination table in `ops/backup-db.sh` (`--list`/`--describe`) that the argument guard, the ping lookup and `bin/restore-db` all read; the ping whitelist disappears
- Authoritative row: S25. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S25.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `ops/backup-db.sh` enumerates `{chunk, share, s3, sync}` at `:40` (argument case), `:109-112` (the `PING_*` whitelist), `container_for` (`:191`) and `volume_for` (`:208-216`); the file's own comment (`:98-105`) names the whitelist's price ("Nothing catches a missing arm … `sync` shipped in exactly that state"); commits 39c29535 (whose message wrongly said the script "already DERIVES PING_<DEST>") and cb50a9f0 ("`sync` never pinged its monitor in either direction") record the defect; `bin/restore-db:51-61` carries a full second copy of the FILTER/VOLUME rows (verified); the destination set or its default appears in `bin/backup-db`, `bin/backup-status` and `bin/backup-cron-install` too; nothing in `test/` reads the backup scripts (§14i).
- Current complexity / invalid state: a destination can be backed up but never pinged (it happened); a restore can write to a different volume from the one the backup read if restore-db's table drifts (it `docker volume create`s the stale name — an empty volume — restores into it, restarts the container on its real volume, and the rehearsal verifies the wrong one, silently); adding a destination is a five-file edit with no gate, and the installer's own checklist ("THREE THINGS BY HAND") undercounts (~ten sites).
- Proposed representation (bash 3.2-safe): one `DEST_TABLE` (name, container filter, volume) with `dest_row`/`dest_names`, `--list` and `--describe NAME` modes; `ping_url` reads the one `PING_<UPPER>=` line literally from `ping.env` at ping time (parsed, never sourced; last occurrence wins; wrapped so a failed lookup can never fail the backup) — the whitelist is not generated, it disappears; `container_for`/`volume_for` become one row read; `bin/restore-db:51-61` becomes `ops/backup-db.sh --describe "$DEST"`; optionally backup-db/backup-cron-install/backup-status consume `--list`.
- Smallest scope: `ops/backup-db.sh` (table, modes, `ping_url`, one lookup; every single-site comment stays beside its line), `bin/restore-db:51-61`; optional the three callers; comments/docs (`backup-db.sh:98-105`, `backup-cron-install:194-204`, `DEPLOY.md:673,715`). `backup-restore.md` needs no rule change.
- Risks: ping semantics must stay exact (only a line beginning `PING_<UPPER>=`, value literal, query-string strip unchanged, unknown keys ignored); Mac bash 3.2 (no associative arrays, `${x,,}` or mapfile; awk/tr are on cron's PATH); `set -euo pipefail` (dest_row returns 1 for an unknown name — callers use if-blocks); the installed copy lags until `bin/backup-cron-install` is re-run (names unchanged, old crontab still parses); the closed set must hold for `backup-status`'s crontab parse; no Rails/data/API/UI/leg impact.
- Validation: nothing in `bin/ci` covers this today (§14i). NEW static test (`test/ops_backup_table_test.rb`, no vault, no docker): `--list` equals the defaults in backup-cron-install/backup-status; for every row the `--describe` volume equals the volume the merged Kamal config mounts (at `/rails/storage` for base/share/s3, `/rails/host/storage` on the sync accessory) read through `Kamal::Configuration.create_from` (mutation: misspell share's volume → red). Ping path: the stubbed-box rehearsal §14i prescribes — `PING_FILE` holding only `PING_SYNC` with its query string, `--to-volume … sync` against stub docker/curl, exactly one `status=up` request (mutation: restore the enumerated whitelist without the sync arm → no request, the 2026-08-26 bug); the script resets PATH, so PATH-shim stubs need the harness. The first `bin/ci` test over `ops/` makes §14i's "NOTHING in bin/ci can go red" sentence untrue — touch the doc.
- Confidence: medium (the duplication and its recorded defect are high; the scripts have no harness and the ping parser is security-adjacent; the ping half's payoff depends on the rehearsal being run). Blast radius: the backup scripts. Effort: small-medium.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2; five enumerations of `{chunk, share, s3, sync}` incl. the restore volume mapping; the script records the price itself). Validation is structural: no existing gate can cover `ops/` (§14i), and that absence IS the finding; the new `test/ops_backup_table_test.rb` copies `test/deploy_hygiene_test.rb`'s pattern (it already reads the merged config through `Kamal::Configuration.create_from`). Must keep backup-restore.md's rules (ping.env parsed, never sourced; argument guards exit before any ping). Priority: P2; independent of F-S25-1; F-S24-2's ticket lands after it.

### F-S26-1 — `bin/ci` compares the three backend legs' RUN counts itself: the §15 rule that only a person checks becomes a gate with one owner in `config/ci.rb`
- Authoritative row: S26. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S26.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — CLAUDE.md §15 and §13 say the legs must report identical run counts and that "`bin/ci` PRINTS the three counts and never COMPARES them"; `config/ci.rb:276-278` runs the gates and nothing after them; only the chunk_remote leg tees its output (`:209,:212`), `test` and `test-s3` capture nothing; the human step is restated in `execute-plan.md:85-87`, `finish-branch.md:28-30` (verified: names a non-existent `test-share` gate, omits `test-chunk-remote`, and asks for identical "run/assertion" counts — assertions ALWAYS differ, so that instruction fails every run) and ~20 plan files. On the tolerance question: the tinkerex#131 flap is in assertions (±2) and skips (±1), never runs; the memory note of 17 logged runs shows runs always identical; no test defines itself conditionally on the backend — so runs can be compared EXACTLY.
- Current complexity / invalid state: three independent leg results can all be green while they disagree — the exact condition §15 calls a real bug — and enforcement belongs to nobody; the copy `/finish-branch` actually loads is the wrong one; in GitHub CI the legs are separate jobs and no comparison happens anywhere.
- Proposed representation: `BACKEND_LEGS = %w[test test-s3 test-chunk-remote]`; each leg tees its own `tmp/ci/<gate>.log` under `set -uo pipefail` (the `PIPESTATUS` trap `config/ci.rb:138-145` already records); after the gates, a ten-line `LegRuns.parse` reads the last `^(\d+) runs, …` line per leg and `CI.run` fails with a table when the runs differ or a summary is missing (stale logs `rm_f`'d up front so "no log" reads as disagreement). Assertions and skips deliberately NOT compared. The human step and its restatements in two command files and two CLAUDE.md passages go away (CLAUDE.md sits at its line ceiling; this removes lines — lower the ratchet). Optional step 2: a `legs-agree` job in GitHub CI (must be a gate in `config/ci.rb` because `ci_hygiene_test.rb:134-155` asserts set equality).
- Smallest scope: new `lib/ci/leg_runs.rb` + unit test; `config/ci.rb` (`test`/`test-s3` → the `bash -c` heredoc form with tee; repoint the chunk_remote tee; `BACKEND_LEGS` + the post-run comparison); docs (CLAUDE.md §15/§13, `execute-plan.md:85-87`, `finish-branch.md:28-30` → "every gate `bin/ci --list-ci` names; `bin/ci` compares the legs", and its stale `paths:` allowlist paragraph at `:96-101`).
- Risks: behind `tee` the `test`/`test-s3` output loses TTY colour (a Minitest reporter writing the count to a file avoids it but touches `test_helper`, S27); dropping `pipefail` would report green over a red suite; no data/API/UI change; the new unit test is backend-agnostic (+same runs on all legs). Land with F-S26-2 (same three commands).
- Validation: existing `ci_hygiene_test.rb:134-155`, `importmap_audit_test.rb:109`, `bin_sync_server_test.rb:67` unchanged. NEW unit tests: equal runs with unequal assertions/skips agree (mutation: parse assertions → red); a log with no summary is a disagreement (mutation: nil as 0 → red); the last of two summaries wins (mutation: first match → red). End-to-end once: add `TEST=test/models` to `test-s3` and run `bin/ci test test-s3` → red naming both counts; plant a failing test under `test` → the step is red (proves tee swallows nothing).
- Confidence: high (the rule text, the flap's location, the absence of backend-conditional tests and of any comparison are all verified). Blast radius: `config/ci.rb` + docs. Effort: small. Priority: high (it is the repo's stated definition of "green").
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T1; the repo's stated definition of green gets one owner, and the close-out command's wrong instruction goes). Compare RUN counts only (assertion counts always differ between legs). Risk added (schema pass): `lib/ci/leg_runs.rb` sits under Zeitwerk's lib root (`config/application.rb:24` ignores only assets/tasks/generators/library_backend.rb; no custom inflections) so it must define `Ci::LegRuns` — a top-level `LegRuns` is a `Zeitwerk::NameError` under CI's eager load (`config/environments/test.rb:40`); F-S25-1 records the identical risk. Priority: P1 as a GUARD to land first; before F-S26-2 (same `config/ci.rb`).

### F-S26-2 — One definition per gate: `ci.yml` jobs carry runner plumbing only, never gate behaviour (`RAILS_ENV: test` ×4, the two `ci/ensure-resvg.sh` pre-steps and `SYNC_PORT` move out), and the contract test checks that
- Authoritative row: S26. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S26.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `ci.yml:222,235,252,280` set `RAILS_ENV: test` on the four test-shaped jobs while `config/ci.rb` does not; `ci/ensure-resvg.sh` runs before `test` and `test-s3` (`:219,:232`) but not before `test-chunk-remote` (the same full suite) or `system`, and on the Linux image it is a no-op (resvg is baked at `ci/runner/Dockerfile:162`; its brew/cargo fallbacks cannot work on the runner); `ci.yml:254` restates `SYNC_PORT`'s default; `ci_hygiene_test.rb:140-146` only counts `bin/ci <gate>` invocations. Mechanism: `db:test:prepare` boots the full app in the current `RAILS_ENV` (activerecord `databases.rake:22,553`) and `config/initializers/library_backend_check.rb` runs the boot guard "unless Rails.env.test?" — so LOCAL `bin/ci` boots development and runs the guard against each leg's configuration, while GitHub CI boots test and never runs it.
- Current complexity / invalid state: a gate's real definition is three things (the `config/ci.rb` command, `ci.yml`'s `env:`, `ci.yml`'s pre-steps); local and CI run different boot sequences under one gate name — a knob added to `CHUNK_REMOTE_REQUIRED` without `bin/sync-server` emitting it turns local `bin/ci` red and leaves CI green, invisible from either side; the resvg precondition covers 2 of 3 full-suite legs and is a third pin of resvg 0.47.0; the split already misled one reader in THIS audit (F-S01-1's validation note assumed CI does a development boot).
- Proposed representation: a gate job = checkout(s) + `bundle install --quiet` + `bin/ci <gate>` + only the runner-plumbing env (`TINKEREX_SYNC_DIR`, `CARGO_*`, `RUSTUP_HOME`, `RUBOCOP_CACHE_ROOT`) and cache/artifact `uses:` steps; decide the environment ONCE in `config/ci.rb` (conservative: add `RAILS_ENV=test` to the four commands so local matches CI; stronger: drop it everywhere so CI also runs the guard per leg — needs one measured CI run first); extend the contract test: every `run:` in a gate job is `bundle install --quiet` or exactly one `bin/ci <gate>`, and job/step `env` keys ⊆ an argued `RUNNER_PLUMBING_ENV` ("exception as data", like `LOCAL_ONLY_GATES`).
- Smallest scope: `config/ci.rb` (the four commands), `ci.yml` (−`RAILS_ENV` ×4, −`SYNC_PORT`, −two `ensure-resvg` steps), `test/ci_hygiene_test.rb` (extend `:134-155` + a planted-fixture test in the shape of `:324-380`); hand `ci/ensure-resvg.sh` to S29 (delete, or keep as a manual Mac helper).
- Risks: the direction choice; without the `ensure-resvg` step a future image that lost resvg writes no step-summary warning (today it covers two legs and its install paths cannot succeed on this fleet — the renderer-parity guard below is the better net); F-S01-1's dev-boot validation must then come from a test; no data/API/UI change; run counts unaffected.
- Validation: existing `ci_hygiene_test.rb:134-155,168-186,188-197` unaffected. NEW fixture assertions: a gate job with `env: RAILS_ENV: test` is an offender; an extra `run: ci/ensure-resvg.sh` is an offender; negative controls (`TINKEREX_SYNC_DIR`/`CARGO_HOME` on a gate job, `DEPENDENCIES_HASH` on a `uses:` step) stay green. Mutations: re-add `RAILS_ENV: test` to one real job → red; re-add the resvg step → red; delete an entry from `RUNNER_PLUMBING_ENV` → the chunk_remote job red.
- Confidence: medium (facts verified against the gem sources and the initializer; no shipped bug yet, but a live local/CI split in the subsystem's central claim). Blast radius: `ci.yml` + `config/ci.rb` + one test. Effort: small. Land with F-S26-1.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T3; a duplicated gate definition with a narrow live effect — locally `db:test:prepare` boots development and runs the boot guard, in CI it does not). Owns the two `ci.yml` resvg steps; F-S29-2 owns the script (§5) — the "keep as a manual Mac helper" option is withdrawn. F-S01-1's new test (c), a static initializer pin, already supplies the dev-boot check this entry asks for. Decide the direction with one measured CI run; land with F-S26-1 (after it). Priority: P3.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): order — the resvg-step removal lands WITH or AFTER F-S29-2's pin-equality assertion, so there is no window with neither the step-summary warning nor a red gate (`ci/ensure-resvg.sh` cannot be deleted before its two `ci.yml:219,232` steps go — the priority pass makes the same point). F-S26-1's optional `legs-agree` job needs artifact upload/download steps, which this entry's contract already allows.

### F-S28-1 — Retire `bin/migrate-record`: a dead second Kaneo client that is also a second WRITE door (with DELETE verbs) onto the canonical record, whose own header and plan scheduled its deletion
- Authoritative row: S28. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S28.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `bin/migrate-record:4` "ONE-TIME MIGRATION TOOL: Forgejo -> Kaneo. Deleted once the cutover is verified"; its plan's Task 21 (`git rm bin/migrate-record`) never ran; it carries a `Net::HTTP::Delete` client arm and `delete_project`/task deletes (`:73`, `:372`, `:451`); its documented recovery ("delete the project, re-run from Task 7", `:189-191`) would now destroy the ~190 issues filed after the import; `git grep migrate-record` finds only the plan (no caller, no test); it hand-copies `bin/log`'s Kaneo wire model (workspace resolution, board walk, label setup, LABELS, statuses, Bearer client); CLAUDE.md §11/§13: "write it with `bin/log` and nothing else … the SINGLE write door"; precedent: `bin/split-status` was deleted with the migration it served (8ec505c1).
- Current complexity / invalid state: two owners of one wire model, one of which nothing exercises (drifts silently while reading as maintained); a committed second write door with destructive verbs `bin/log` deliberately lacks; destructive documented recovery.
- Proposed representation: `git rm bin/migrate-record`; nothing else references it (the plan stays as history; the file comes back from git if a re-import is ever wanted, which would also need Forgejo and the off-box export). The one non-obvious rule it holds ("(reserved #N)" placeholders are never deleted) survives in each placeholder's description and the plan.
- Smallest scope: one `git rm`. Optional guard turning §11's "nothing else" into a check: a hygiene test that no tracked file except `bin/log` (and `test/`) addresses Kaneo's write endpoints (`/api/task`, `/api/label`, `/api/comment`), made non-vacuous by asserting `bin/log` itself matches (mutation: restore the file → red).
- Risks: none at runtime, in CI, in tests or on any leg; `ci_hygiene_test.rb:562`'s `Dir.glob("bin/*")` surface stays non-empty.
- Validation: `git grep -n migrate-record` returns only the plan; bare `bin/ci`.
- Confidence: high. Blast radius: one file. Effort: trivial. Priority: medium (removes a loaded gun aimed at the canonical record).
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2; pure removal of a dead second write door with DELETE verbs against the canonical record, contradicting §11/§13). Validation (was FAIL): Existing — `test/ci_hygiene_test.rb:562` (its `bin/*` surface stays non-empty). NEW — no tracked file except `bin/log` (and `test/`) names Kaneo's write endpoints `/api/task`, `/api/label`, `/api/comment`; non-vacuous because it also asserts `bin/log` matches; mutation: restore `bin/migrate-record` → red. Priority: P2 (cheap); independent.

### F-S28-2 — Each `bin/log` write phase names its own "unfiled" record through ONE helper (`filing(what, payload) { … }`), replacing an auto-attach "at the ONE raise site" that cannot fire in the common outage plus four compensating per-verb rescues — `close` never got the patch
- Authoritative row: S28. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S28.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `bin/log:88` says `api` "attaches the payload at the ONE raise site", while payload-carrying `Unreachable`s are constructed at five sites with `e.payload || payload` merges (`:411`, `:563`, …); a write verb's first wire call is always a READ (workspace/project/board resolution), so when Kaneo is down `api` attaches `body = nil` and only a per-verb rescue saves the record — each added after a measured loss (234abebd, 0aae5900, da5244f9 — all three verified); `close` (`:587-603`, verified) has NO rescue of its own, so a failed PUT after a good board read spools `{"status":"done"}` under an opaque task id with no issue number (no test covers it); the spool's `# endpoint:` line records whichever request FAILED (a create against a dead server is filed under `/api/auth/organization/list`); `spool_dir` defaults to the RUNNING checkout's `tmp/log-pending` (`:605`).
- Current complexity / invalid state: `Unreachable#payload`/`#path` are two independently nullable fields filled from different places, so the pair can be contradictory (a POST body beside the path of a GET that failed) or incomplete (`close`: a body naming nothing); writing a correct new write verb requires knowing that `api`'s auto-attach does NOT cover the server-down case — the opposite of what `:87-92` tells the reader.
- Proposed representation: `Unfiled = Struct.new(:what, :payload)` on the exception (nil ⇒ a read failed: nothing to file); `filing(what, payload) { yield }` sets `e.unfiled ||= Unfiled.new(...)` and re-raises — the payload-before-wire rule becomes structural because Ruby evaluates the argument before the block; `create_task` = two SIBLING phases (create, then each label attach — the label trap cannot form); `note`/`close`/`setup` get one phase each around their write only (resolution reads stay OUTSIDE, so a read failure still spools nothing); `api`'s re-wrap goes; `spool!` prints `what` + the pretty payload and drops the endpoint line. Commands, exit codes 0/2/3/4/5 and the spool path unchanged.
- Smallest scope: `bin/log` (helper, accessor, `create_task`, `note`, `close`, `setup`, `main`/`spool!`), `test/log_cli_test.rb` (re-express the `"/api/label"` assertion as the attachment record; add the tests below).
- Risks: the spool text changes (only humans read it; `status` globs the directory); keep "#N was created" + the quoted label in the stderr message; `||=` (innermost wins) must stay; `close`'s resolution read stays outside its phase; no leg dependence (a no-Rails subprocess); §14j's env stripping untouched. The file has this repo's densest regression history — the existing mutation-checked spool tests make it safe.
- Validation: existing spool pins at `log_cli_test.rb:285-303,355-368,374-390,398-416,421-431,582-593,659-697,783-793,1264-1269`. NEW: (a) `close 10` with a good board and `PUT /task/status` answering 502 leaves an entry naming `#10` (mutation: remove close's phase → red); (b) `open` against the dead default port leaves an entry naming the create and NOT `/api/auth/organization/list` (mutation: put the failed path back → red); (c) optional funnel: every `SUBCOMMANDS` write verb against a dead port leaves a spool file (mutation: remove note's phase → red).
- Confidence: medium (both defects concrete; payoff confined to outage paths). Blast radius: one script. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2; `close` against a failing PUT spools a record with no issue number; the defect class has recurred four times). Exit codes 3/4/5 unchanged. Priority: P2; independent of F-S28-1.

### F-S29-1 — Make `render.mjs`'s declared exit-code wire TRUE: exit 3 ("nothing parsed") is unreachable, and "not a Gerber" today exits 1 inside node's crash report — the code the header reserves for node itself failing [DEMOTED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication; not ranked in §6]
- Authoritative row: S29. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S29.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `tools/gerber-svg/render.mjs:35-41` declares 0/2/3/4 and says 3 and 4 are separate "because the Ruby side answers them differently"; `:54-58` `const rendered = renderLayers(plot(await read(files)))` then `if (rendered.layers.length === 0) … exit(3)` — but a rejection of `read` escapes the top-level `await` as node's uncaught-exception exit 1, and the vendored bundle's parser throws `"File type not recognized"` (grep verified) for a file it cannot read, while a successful `read` yields one layer per input path (`files.length >= 1` is already enforced at `:49-52`), so the exit-3 arm cannot fire; `gerber.rb:91` maps every non-4 failure to `RenderError` with the first 300 bytes of stderr (verified), which for an uncaught exception is node's report of the 85,723-character minified source line, not the reason (reasoned from Node's fatal-exception format, not executed); `gerber_test.rb:212-213` records the false mechanism ("render.mjs exits 3 with 'no layer could be parsed'") and passes via exit 1; the sibling tool `main.rs` already has the needed shape (one handler, one line, one code).
- Current complexity / invalid state: the wire declares an outcome the tool cannot produce and omits the one it does; "this file is not Gerber/Excellon" and "tracespace or node crashed/OOM'd" share a code, so no caller can answer them differently without parsing a stack trace; `maker_previews.error` is expected to hold minified JavaScript; the policy hook `pdf.rb:49-63` uses ("content is not the claimed format" → `unsupported`, never re-enqueued) cannot even be expressed for a non-Gerber `.gbr` (GIMP's brush format shares the extension).
- Proposed representation: a `fail(code, why)` helper writing one line; `try { parsed = await read(files) } catch (e) { fail(3, \`no layer could be parsed: ${e.message}\`) }` — the ONLY place "not Gerber/Excellon" can arise; delete the unreachable `layers.length === 0` arm; rewrite the header table (0 SVG / 2 usage / 3 not Gerber-or-Excellon / 4 nothing to draw / else node or tracespace failed). Ruby needs no change (4 stays `Unsupported`, everything else `RenderError`, now with a readable reason); whether 3 should map to `Unsupported` per `pdf.rb`'s policy is S08's call.
- Smallest scope: `render.mjs` (the catch, delete `:55-58`, rewrite `:35-45`); `gerber_test.rb:212-220` (S08's file: correct the comment; assert code and reason). No change to `gerber.rb`, `gerber_zip.rb`, the job or `files-preview.md`.
- Risks: Ruby behaviour unchanged; board mode: one unrecognised member fails the board with 3 instead of 1; streams stay separate; the catch also classifies an I/O failure reading the temp file as 3 (the one-line message keeps the real reason); existing `failed` rows keep their old error text until re-rendered; `process.exit` right after a stderr write can drop the line on macOS async pipes (exits 2/3/4 already do this; Linux pipes are synchronous).
- Validation: existing empty-layer → Unsupported, node-not-runnable, timeout/process-group-kill, garbage → RenderError cases. NEW in `:214-220`: `assert_match(/failed \(3\)/)` and `/not recognized/` (mutation 1: remove the try/catch → exit 1 → red; mutation 2: restore a `layers.length === 0` check instead of the catch → still exit 1 → red); optional board-mode case with one garbage member → `(3)`.
- Confidence: medium (mechanism read from the bundle; the stderr consequence not executed; payoff is a true wire and readable failures). Blast radius: one script + one assertion. Effort: trivial.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): DEMOTED → the §4 defects list (a contract fix in a tool script: exit 3 cannot fire — `read` throws inside the top-level `await` and escapes as node's exit 1, `render.mjs:54-58`; Ruby maps every code except 4 to `RenderError`, `gerber.rb:91`, so behaviour is identical for 1 and 3). The change makes the declared wire true and the stored error readable; S08 decides whether exit 3 maps to Unsupported. Not ranked in §6.

### F-S29-2 — One resvg declaration per image that pins what actually renders (`--locked`), held equal by a hygiene test; retire `ci/ensure-resvg.sh`; make a missing preview toolchain RED in CI instead of a skip [NARROWED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication]
- Authoritative row: S29 (the script's two `ci.yml` steps are also removed by F-S26-2 — see §5). Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S29.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — three literal pins, none with `--locked` (`Dockerfile:107`, `ci/runner/Dockerfile:162`, `ci/ensure-resvg.sh:24`; verified), while `ci/runner/Dockerfile:175-181` records the repo's own measured rule that `cargo install` ignores a committed lock unless `--locked` ("a renderer whose dependencies drift silently between image rebuilds would change stored previews with nothing recording that it happened") and applies it to stl-thumbnailer only; the published resvg-0.47.0 crate asks for caret ranges (`tiny-skia = "0.12.0"`, `usvg = "0.47.0"`), so each image build resolves the rasteriser's closure to that day's patch releases; the script always exits at "already present" on the image-based fleet, its brew arm is unpinned, its cargo arm cannot write the root-owned `CARGO_HOME`, and it guards 2 of 3 backend legs; the "cannot disagree" claim (`ci/runner/Dockerfile:141-142`) is checked by nothing — pdfium already drifted the same way (CI native vs production WASM); a missing stl-thumbnailer/pdfium/vipsthumbnail/node degrades its tests to SKIPS with no signal (the recorded "STL never ran in CI" case, `ci.yml:112-116`); `docs/adding-a-preview-format.md:24-27` teaches the per-leg install pattern forward.
- Current complexity / invalid state: "which resvg does this repo mean" has three owners with three semantics, and even the agreeing literals admit different tiny-skia/usvg patch versions per image build — a production image and a CI image built on different days can rasterise the same SVG differently with no THUMBNAIL_REVISION bump; the script presents as CI's resvg gate but is a no-op wherever CI runs.
- Proposed representation: one owner of CI's toolchain (the runner image); `cargo install resvg --version 0.47.0 --locked` in both Dockerfiles, held equal by one `ci_hygiene` assertion (scan both files, assert the scanned set has exactly 2 entries — §14e non-empty — one unique version, all `--locked`); a CI-only presence test (`ENV["CI"]`) that FAILS unless every renderer the image promises answers `available?` (SvgRasterizer, Stl, Pdf, ImageThumb, Gerber), with Mesh named as deliberately absent (no F3D in the CI image); delete `ci/ensure-resvg.sh` and its two workflow steps; update the preview-format walkthrough.
- Smallest scope (order matters): (1) the CI-only presence test — land first and confirm green on the live image; (2) the pin assertion in `ci_hygiene_test.rb`; (3) `--locked` in `Dockerfile:107` (S24) and `ci/runner/Dockerfile:162`, reword `:141-142`; (4) delete the script and `ci.yml:218-219,231-232`; (5) `adding-a-preview-format.md:24-27`. Rebuild the CI image and redeploy for (3).
- Risks: `--locked` installs the published lock's tiny-skia/usvg — if either live image was built against later patches, PNG output can move with no revision bump: compare the fixtures' PNG bytes from old and new images before deploying and bump the SVG-family THUMBNAIL_REVISIONs only if they differ; the presence test turns "NON-FATAL BY DESIGN" (a developer-Mac-era choice) into a red gate — if the live image predates the Dockerfile, step (1) goes red at once, which is the gap it exists to surface; a future macos/windows job loses the fallback (already forced behind preflight); app runtime, data and the three legs untouched (the CI-only test skips outside CI, so run counts match).
- Validation: existing `available?`-honesty tests for a missing binary (svg_rasterizer, gerber, stl). Hygiene mutations: one Dockerfile at 0.46.0 → red; `--locked` dropped from one → red; one install line deleted → red on the count. Presence mutations: `CI=1 RESVG_BIN=/nonexistent` → red naming SvgRasterizer; `NODE_BIN=/nonexistent` → red naming Gerber; `STL_THUMBNAILER_BIN=/nonexistent` (set before boot) → red naming Stl.
- Confidence: medium (facts high; the risk is latent on today's fleet, but two concrete neighbours — STL never ran in Mac-era CI; pdfium differs between the images — show the model drifts in practice). Blast radius: two Dockerfiles, one script, two tests. Effort: small. Pairs with the renderer-parity defect recorded under S26.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): NARROWED; scope reconciled with §5. (1) `--locked` on both resvg installs (`Dockerfile:107`, `ci/runner/Dockerfile:162`); (2) one hygiene assertion that the two pins are equal AND locked, over a non-empty set; (3) delete the no-op `ci/ensure-resvg.sh`. The two `ci.yml` steps that call it (`:218-219`, `:231-232`) are F-S26-2's to remove (§5, §1 tie-break) — struck from this scope. The CI-only renderer-presence gate is a separate guard that would have to name Mesh as deliberately absent (F3D is not in the CI image): decide it together with the S26 renderer-parity defect (§4), not here. Effort corrected to small-medium (a CI image rebuild and redeploy, a before/after PNG byte comparison, possible THUMBNAIL_REVISION bumps). Priority: P2; lands with F-S26-2.
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): CO-OWNED S26 + S29 — S29 owns one of the five files (`ci/ensure-resvg.sh`); `Dockerfile:107` is S24's, `ci/runner/Dockerfile:162` and `test/ci_hygiene_test.rb` are S26's, and the renderer-parity defect the (deferred) presence gate would guard is recorded under S26. Order: the pin-equality assertion lands before F-S26-2 removes the two `ci.yml` steps.

### F-S27-1 — The harness owns the `FileLayer.current` memo's per-test lifetime: ONE unconditional `FileLayer.reset!` in the shared teardown, and `pin_file_layer_backend` retired
- Authoritative row: S27. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S27.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `test/test_helper.rb:243-257` resets the process-global memo only behind two flags (`@file_layer_backend_pinned`, set only by `pin_file_layer_backend`; `@fresh_share_library_roots`, set only by `use_fresh_share_library!`); a direct `FileLayer.current = X` sets neither, so its reset is the caller's job — 90 such sites in 45 test files (re-counted), 158 `FileLayer.reset!` calls; the CSP lambda resolves `FileLayer.current` on every response, so a leaked adapter is read by the next test's first request in the same forked worker; ≥16 files re-explain the obligation by hand ("load-bearing, not decoration"), several reset on the way IN out of distrust, two teardowns reset conditionally on an unrelated ivar, and `library_broadcast_test` snapshots the ivar itself; `test_helper.rb:249-253` and `:207-213` record two past leaks; `pin_file_layer_backend` (`:233-241`) is dead weight — its purpose (pinning `chunk`) went with Arc D Part 2, its only 6 calls are each followed by `use_fresh_share_library!`, which reassigns the memo the pin just reset, ~12 files carry a paragraph on why they do NOT use it, and `test/system/README.md:26-27` still tells authors to use it (verified) while no system test does. The 2026-08-26 audit's S39-2 proposed the block-form `with_file_layer` that never landed.
- Current complexity / invalid state: "an adapter a finished test installed is still `FileLayer.current`" is a representable state prevented only by convention at ~90 sites; three mechanisms with different teardown contracts for one act (a flag-guarded ENV pin, a flag-guarded fresh library, a raw assignment with no contract); a missed reset fails in a different test chosen by random order.
- Proposed representation: `teardown { FileLayer.reset!; @fresh_share_library_roots&.each { … remove_entry … } }` — the memo's lifetime becomes "one test" by construction; delete `pin_file_layer_backend`, both flags and the misplaced doc at `:104-114`; the 158 existing resets become redundant rather than wrong. Zero-rebuild variant if the s3 leg's timing shows it: snapshot `@current` in `setup` and restore in `teardown` (the shape `library_broadcast_test` hand-rolls).
- Smallest scope: `test_helper.rb` (~5 lines); a new harness guard test built on `fresh_share_library_cleanup_test`'s Probe pattern; delete the 6 redundant pin lines; `test/system/README.md:26-27`; fix the ~16 now-false comments (never drop an adjacent `remove_entry` of a directory the file minted).
- Risks: a test that passes today only because an earlier test leaked an adapter will now fail — a real hidden order dependence, desired; rebuild cost on the s3 leg (`S3.new` builds an Aws client eagerly; unmeasured, expected small; the snapshot variant is zero-cost); teardown order already verified (the base teardown runs last); harness-only, identical on all legs (+1/+2 runs on each).
- Validation: `fresh_share_library_cleanup_test.rb:81-89` stays green. NEW probe: assign `FileLayer::ChunkRemote.new` directly inside a Probe test, then assert the memo is nil after teardown — RED today, GREEN after (mutation: delete the reset line → both this probe and `:81-89` red). The 6 de-pinned tests stay green; mutation: swap their `use_fresh_share_library!` for a ChunkRemote assignment → "the scheduled fan-out declines on a scannable backend" red (the surviving line carries the weight). Time the s3 leg before/after.
- Confidence: high (mechanism fully traced; every assignment site sits inside setup/test/helper bodies). Blast radius: the harness. Effort: trivial.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T2; a convention kept by hand at ~89 sites becomes one unconditional reset; run counts rise identically on all legs). Effort corrected to small (the harness lines, a new harness guard test, six pin lines, the README, ~16 now-false comments, and timing the s3 leg before and after). Priority: P1 as a GUARD to land early — F-S17-1's new test assigns `FileLayer.current = FileLayer::ChunkRemote.new` directly, and F-X-1/F-S18-1's stub-adapter tests likewise rely on it. Before F-S27-2 (same file).
- Overlap pass (2026-10-19): Coordination — the six de-pinned tests are S06/S07 job tests; `library_broadcast_test.rb:22-33` (S01) belongs in the comment sweep (it also cites the retired `CHUNK_REMOTE_TOKEN` and `pin_file_layer_backend`). Harness-first: land this and F-S27-2's first step before the entries that add integration tests, so those tests get the reset by construction and use the verifying helper.

### F-S27-2 — One verifying web sign-in helper owned by the harness (`sign_in_web!`), failing at the helper if the sign-in did not take — the 2026-08-26 audit's accepted-then-deferred S39-1, grown from 75 raw sites to 129 [NARROWED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication]
- Authoritative row: S27. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S27.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `post user_session_path` appears 129 times in 107 test files (re-counted) and only ~6 assert the redirect within two lines; a failed sign-in (Devise's FailureApp, or the per-IP throttle `rate_limit to: 10, within: 3.minutes`) ALSO redirects, so only the destination tells failure from success (`test_helper.rb:56-64` records the throttle addition measuring 306 failures and 20 errors whose shape was "the test simply is not signed in"); `PublicFixture#sign_in_as` (`public_fixture.rb:100-102`, verified) checks nothing and 13 suites call it; 23 private copies check nothing; three files record "DEVIATION FROM THE PLAN … `sign_in_as` is not defined in test_helper.rb"; the landing-page change c80ea529 had to edit 8 files + web_helpers (every verifying site) while ~120 unverified sites noticed nothing; cb50a9f0 lists "S39-1's 60-file sweep" under "NOT done, deliberately".
- Current complexity / invalid state: "signed in" is something a test believes rather than checks; a bare POST lets every later request run anonymously, unnoticed when the assertions are negative — three current tests would pass with nobody signed in (`attach_from_files_test.rb:44-49` — `count: 0` passes on the redirect body; `:89-98` — `assert_difference 0` holds when both POSTs redirect; `public_project_test.rb:28-37` — the signed-in-owner half degenerates into the anonymous half, leaving the §14d rule it names unexercised); triggers: >10 sign-ins in one test, an unconfirmed or passwordless fixture user (`settings_hub_test.rb:33-36` creates one), any auth change.
- Proposed representation: `sign_in_web!(user, password: WEB_PASSWORD)` in `ActionDispatch::IntegrationTest` (`post` + `assert_redirected_to root_path` with a message naming the email); `PublicFixture#sign_in_as` delegates to it (13 suites verify at once); the 23 private copies become one-line delegations; the distinct name avoids the shadowing collision S39-1 warned about. Excluded on purpose: the auth/throttle/demo suites that drive failures deliberately, the system tier's browser `sign_in_as` (already asserts the path), the v1 device `login` helpers.
- Smallest scope: (a) `test_helper.rb` (helper + constant); (b) `public_fixture.rb:100-102` (one cross-row line, 13 suites covered); (c) the 23 private copies (mechanical, feature rows' files); (d) inline setup POSTs opportunistically. Sequence (c)/(d) as their own change.
- Risks: tests that "sign in" a user who cannot (unconfirmed, passwordless, non-default password) go red at the helper — intended, each was running anonymously; >10 sign-ins in one test now fail at the 11th instead of continuing anonymously; the next landing-page change becomes a one-line edit; no app change, no leg dependence, run counts unchanged.
- Validation: NEW harness tests — an unconfirmed user is refused with a message naming the email (mutation: delete the assertion → red); a confirmed user returns the user and a following `get projects_path` is 200. Before/after demonstration: make the helper post a wrong password → `attach_from_files_test:44-49,:89-98` and `public_project_test:28-37` go RED at the helper line, while the same mutation applied to today's local helpers leaves all three GREEN — the finding.
- Confidence: high on the hazard and helper (recorded incident; three vacuous shapes in the current tree; the authors' own plan-deviation notes); medium on scope (~100 files for the full sweep; the first step is 2 files). Blast radius: the harness + fixtures. Effort: small (first step), medium (sweep).
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): NARROWED. First step, which carries the value: the verifying helper in `test_helper.rb`; `PublicFixture#sign_in_as` (`public_fixture.rb:100-102`) delegating to it (13 suites); the three named vacuous tests fixed. The ~100-file sweep of raw `post user_session_path` sites is mechanical churn — do it opportunistically, never inside a product PR. Priority: P2 for the first step. Lands after F-S27-1 (same file).

### F-S12-1 — One parameter-schema representation per sidecar: `param_groups` only (finishes F-62 from the 2026-08-19 audit, whose out-of-repo gate is now met; applies the same to openscad) [DEMOTED 2026-10-19 — see adjudication; not ranked in §6]
- Authoritative row: S12. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S12.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `sidecar/boxes/app.py:387-391` emits `param_groups` AND a flat `params` ("Back-compat … Safe to drop once every app consumes `param_groups`") that is the non-advanced subset only; `sidecar/boxes/tests/test_api.py:316-321` (F-62) PINS the flat key rather than deleting it and records that it omits "2403 of the 5082 params — 47%"; `sidecar/openscad/schema.py:22-26` returns both `params` and `param_groups` and each param also carries `group` (`:37`, a third statement of membership); the openscad sidecar itself reads only the grouped form (`app.py:190`, `coerce.py`); the Rails fake (`fake_boxes_sidecar.rb`) nests every `params` inside a group; the sibling bundles read only `group.params`/`schema.param_groups` (`openscad/fields.js:130,136`, `openscad.js:175`; the only top-level `params` reads are a saved item's VALUES) — the reviewer also grepped every commit of `../tinkerex-addons` touching `addons/` and the deleted Rails `BoxesClient` (`6095b871^`), finding no reader of the flat key; `schema.py:1-5` still claims the shape matches `BoxesClient::Generator`, deleted 2026-08-03.
- Current complexity / invalid state: each sidecar's schema has two representations kept consistent only by tests, one of them LOSSY by design; a consumer binding to the flat key gets a form missing half the knobs with no error; the same key means different things across the two sidecars (boxes: the non-advanced subset; openscad: everything) while `params` on the REQUEST side means submitted values in both; ~60 lines of boxes test pin a key with no reader; openscad tests assert on the redundant list; a doubled payload per gallery load; docstrings describe a Rails consumer that no longer exists.
- Proposed representation: `normalize` returns `{"param_groups": _group(...)}` only (optionally `_group` pops each param's `group` so membership is stated once by the enclosing group); boxes' `/generators` entries carry `param_groups` only. At the top level `params` then means only "submitted values" on the wire.
- Smallest scope: `boxes/app.py:388-391` (delete); `boxes/tests/test_api.py:316-370` (the F-62 pin → an absence assertion, the follow-up F-62 itself named); `openscad/schema.py` (+docstring); `openscad/app.py:60` and the `/params` docstring (wording); four openscad tests re-pointed at the groups (`test_schema`, `test_api:95-107`, `test_catalog:93`, `test_coerce:296`); publish both images with `bin/publish-sidecar` (no Rails deploy); out-of-repo comment-only updates in the bundles.
- Risks: the only plausible break is an unknown out-of-tree addon reading the flat key (it would get an empty form, not an error) — no first-party bundle ever did, and the sidecar JSON shapes are not part of the documented addon API; deploy order does not matter; no Rails code, data or leg is touched; the openscad render cache keys on coerced values, not the schema; re-run the bundle-repo grep at implementation time. Neither sidecar's pytest runs in `bin/ci`, so verification is by hand (say so in the PR).
- Validation: existing boxes `test_generators_shape`/defaults/`choice_labels` sweeps and openscad schema/coerce tests (re-pointed). NEW: (a) boxes — no `/generators` entry carries a top-level `params` (mutation: restore `:391` → red); (b) openscad — `normalize(REAL_DUMP).keys() == {"param_groups"}` and `/params` answers only `param_groups` (mutation: re-add `"params"` → red).
- Confidence: medium-high (duplication, zero readers across the bundle repo's history, and the met F-62 gate all verified from source; the residual is an unknown third-party bundle). Blast radius: two sidecar images. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality pass, 2026-10-19): DEMOTED → ticket, done when the sidecar images are next published. It removes dead data (a lossy duplicate no first-party consumer reads — verified in the local, unfetched tinkerex-addons checkout) rather than a decision the app acts on, in sidecar code `bin/ci` does not run; the entry's mutation checks stand for the ticket. Not ranked in §6.

### F-S12-2 — Give the openscad catalog's static admission policy ONE owner — the Ruby guard `bin/ci` actually runs — and move the one rule that today runs nowhere (no `include <`/`use <`/`import(`/`surface(` in a bundled `.scad`)
- Authoritative row: S12 (the Ruby file is S25's). Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S12.md, Opportunity 2. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — the forbidden-licence rule exists three times (`test/deploy_hygiene_test.rb` `FORBIDDEN_LICENCE_TERMS`/`forbidden_licence?`, `sidecar/openscad/tests/test_catalog.py:14-31,54-55`, `test_api.py:49` — the third copy has its own mixed-case tuple and no `lgpl`, equivalent only because "gpl" is a substring of "lgpl"); the NOTICE-credit and on-disk-listing checks exist twice; NO gate runs the Python copies (`config/ci.rb` and `ci.yml` contain no pytest/python step — grep verified); the external-reference rule exists ONLY in `test_catalog.py:77-84` (verified) — the exact lesson the catalog's NOTICE records (assembled-wrench "passed an include/use grep cleanly and STILL pulled three external STLs via import()") — so today a catalog entry that `use <>`s or `import()`s an outside file passes every gate (the image build runs only `assert-capabilities.sh` on `probe.scad`); the duplication already cost one bug (the lowercased-needle bug fixed in one copy and avoided in the other).
- Current complexity / invalid state: "what may enter the catalog" is spread over three files in two languages; a policy change must be made two or three times; the only copy that can block a merge lacks the external-reference rule.
- Proposed representation: the Ruby catalog block owns every STATIC rule about catalog data (licence terms, attribution, ref, NOTICE, on-disk listing, no external references — `EXTERNAL_REFERENCES = ["include <", "use <", "import(", "surface("]`, ~10 lines, directory-driven through `CATALOG_GLOB` so a second sidecar's catalog is covered automatically); the Python suite keeps only what tests the sidecar's OWN behaviour or needs the binary (`test_every_entry_resolves_to_a_real_file`, `…_exposes_parameters`, `…_renders_a_substantial_model_at_its_defaults`).
- Smallest scope: `test/deploy_hygiene_test.rb` (+the rule; S25's file — coordinate); `test_catalog.py` (delete `BANNED`/`_is_forbidden`, the four duplicated tests and the moved one); `test_api.py:37-53` (delete); `openscad/README.md:156-167` (pytest counts); one sentence in `addons.md:53-71`.
- Risks: product/data/API/UI/legs unaffected; the only loss is defence in depth for someone who runs pytest inside the sidecar and never runs `bin/ci` (every PR runs `bin/ci`, so the gate is unchanged or stronger); the new Ruby test passes on today's tree (none of the three `.scad` files contains a needle — verified by the reviewer).
- Validation: the existing Ruby catalog block (mutation-checked four ways per addons.md:67-68) and the Python resolver/binary tests that stay. NEW mutation: in a scratch copy append `use <missing.scad>` to `catalog/probe_plate.scad` → the new Ruby test goes red; restore and clear bootsnap per the repo memory; repeat with `import("x.stl")` so each needle carries weight. The block's emptiness guard already keeps it from passing vacuously.
- Confidence: medium (triplication and the never-gated rule verified; materiality modest — catalog additions are rare and vetted). Blast radius: one Ruby test + two Python test files. Effort: small.
- Coordinator adjudication (materiality + schema passes, 2026-10-19): KEPT (T3; guard gap — the external-reference rule runs in no gate; the licence rule lives three times in two languages). Priority: P3; coordinate with S25 (`test/deploy_hygiene_test.rb`); lands after F-S25-1 (same file).

### F-S32-1 — The addon snapshot has one owner per addon KEY: copy the `boxes` index entry with the `boxes` directory (the entry has never matched any published version, and its hybrid state is now load-bearing)
- Authoritative row: S32. Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S32.md, Opportunity 1. Coordinator verification: CONFIRMED — `test/fixtures/addons/manifests.json:14-22` carries no `min_host`, no `layout`, no `permissions` and a test-prose `description`, while upstream `../tinkerex-addons/addons/manifests.json:24-46` declares `min_host: 3`, `layout: "wide"` and two permissions; all 18 `boxes/*.js` are byte-identical upstream (reviewer's `diff -rq`); upstream `bin/check-fixture-drift:18-30` excludes the index ON PURPOSE on the premise "`Tools::AddonsController` never gates on `available?` … `min_host` decides whether the /tools CARD greys out … Revisit if it ever gates more" — and it gates more since 8fc593ec (2026-10-08): `tools/home_controller.rb:25` `Registry.all.select(&:available?)` drops an unavailable tool from the New ▸ menu, and two snapshot-backed tests assert the Box Generator is offered there (`test/integration/web/new_menu_test.rb:99-100`, `test/system/new_menu_test.rb:22-33`); the snapshot contradicts itself (`boxes/boxes.js:14-15` says "manifests.json declares `min_host: 3`" beside an entry `Registry` reads as `min_host: 0`); `Host::LEVEL = 3` (`host.rb:34`).
- Current complexity / invalid state: one index holds three ownership states — verbatim-upstream (`boxes/`), test-owned (`fixture`, `sdk-fixture`) and one hand-edited hybrid (the `boxes` entry) that neither repo's guard checks and no deployment has ever served. The system tests drive a `/tools/boxes` page no deployment renders (no permissions list, no `container-wide`); every snapshot-backed availability decision sees `min_host: 0`, so "the host under test satisfies the floor the shipped bundle declares" cannot be asserted from this suite even in principle; the upstream guard carries a 13-line exception whose premise is now false. No current test outcome depends on the difference (reviewer-analysed: `LEVEL` 3 ≥ floor 3; `container-wide` is invisible at the 1400px test window; only `library:read` is enforced and neither copy declares it).
- Proposed representation: one rule keyed by addon — a key whose directory is copied from tinkerex-addons has its index entry copied with it, whole; keys that exist only here stay test-owned (their omissions are load-bearing, e.g. `fixture` declaring no `library:read` is the negative case of `addon_sdk_test.rb:335`). "The boxes snapshot" becomes one copied unit instead of "the directory but not its index row"; the upstream guard can then compare each snapshotted key's entry whole — neither the file equality it rightly rejects nor the "chosen SUBSET of keys" it rightly rejects.
- Smallest scope: `test/fixtures/addons/manifests.json:14-22` → upstream `:24-46` verbatim (~25 lines of fixture JSON; no Ruby/JS change). Out-of-repo follow-up for tinkerex-addons: a per-key entry comparison in `bin/check-fixture-drift:44-60` and shrink `:18-30` to one sentence — without it the entry can drift again the way it did three times (6095b871, bbd1d68, ba7ecb4/a9ff541).
- Risks: none to data, API or backend legs (a fixture). The `/tools/boxes` page under test gains a two-item permissions list and `main.container-wide`; analysed test-neutral, not run (`settle_addon_cards` compares card positions to each other). Deliberate consequence: a future upstream `min_host` raise beyond `Host::LEVEL` turns the host's New-menu tests red when mirrored — the floor check, enforcing the host-first order a9ff541's own message demands. The upstream comment's "must not be made into a mirror" argued from four reasons: one orthogonal to a per-key comparison (the synthetic addons), one a host choice (the description), one cost-free (layout/permissions are display-only) and one no longer true (`min_host` "would exercise nothing").
- Validation: existing — `new_menu_test.rb:80-103` (integration), `test/system/new_menu_test.rb:22-33`, `file_menu_test.rb:132-148`, the seven `/tools/boxes` system files, `template_hygiene_test.rb:537-541`. NEW weight without a new file — mutation: set `Maker::Addons::Host::LEVEL = 2`: on today's snapshot "the New menu lists the tools this deployment can run, and only those" stays GREEN (boxes reads `min_host: 0`); after the change it goes RED at `:99-100` (read that test's result specifically; `host_test.rb` and `addons_controller_test.rb:207-209` go red in both states). Upstream: with the per-entry comparison, setting the snapshot's `min_host` to 2 makes `check-fixture-drift` exit 1 (today the same edit prints `ok`).
- Confidence: medium (every claim read from source and git; the change is ~25 lines of JSON; materiality is modest — no current outcome changes — and the lasting value depends on the one-file upstream follow-up). Blast radius: one fixture file (+ a sibling-repo script). Effort: trivial here, small upstream. Priority: P3; independent.

### F-S33-1 — Stored attachment roles follow the vocabulary by ONE rule with one owner (a re-classify-`other` sweep on `Maker::FileAttachment`), not by a hand-copied data migration per widening — and the two widenings that never got one heal
- Authoritative row: S33 (the files are S06's; the migration tests S23's). Report: audit-2026-10-19-simplification/reports/S33.md, Opportunity 1.
